## [1251] Osteoporosis fracture prevention pharmacotherapy

**Cluster**: osteoporosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Osteoporosis guidelines base treatment on fracture risk assessment (FRAX and DXA) and prior fragility fracture, with national guidelines now naming imminent or very high risk and supporting early anabolic therapy. Under-treatment after fracture remains a major gap.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 Australian guideline recommends FRAX-based risk stratification, clarifies imminent and very high fracture risk, addresses rebound vertebral fractures after denosumab cessation, and supports early osteoanabolic therapy. (PMID 40134107)
- The 2024 UK guideline (NOGG), endorsed by IOF and ESCEO, covers prevention and treatment of osteoporosis, and a 2026 paper discusses its application in China. (PMID 40921943, PMID 42642185)
- The German guideline notes 71% of people with osteoporosis in Germany remain untreated and stresses accurate diagnosis and interdisciplinary care. (PMID 39803994)
- The Dutch multidisciplinary guideline provides flowcharts to support diagnosis and treatment for fracture prevention in daily practice. (PMID 38564062)
- The ECTS 2024 recommendations give pragmatic advice on preventing and treating glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis. (PMID 39556468)
- In older adults, optimising osteoporosis management reduces fracture incidence, morbidity and loss of quality of life. (PMID 41146827)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Intervention thresholds differ between national guidelines.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Osteoporosis fracture prevention pharmacotherapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treat after a fragility fracture; consider anabolic first for very high risk.
- Risk of vertebral fractures after stopping denosumab.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Osteoporosis fracture prevention pharmacotherapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 72-year-old woman with a vertebral fracture and T-score -2.4.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Osteoporosis fracture prevention pharmacotherapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: DXA T-score definitions, FRAX thresholds and bisphosphonate duration and drug holidays are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38564062**: *The Dutch multidisciplinary guideline osteoporosis and fracture prevention, taking a local guideline to the international arena* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39556468**: *Prevention and treatment of glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis in adults: recommendations from the European Calcified Tissue Society* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39803994**: *Clinical Practice Guideline: The Diagnosis and Treatment of Osteoporosis* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40134107**: *2024 Royal Australian College of General Practitioners and Healthy Bones Australia guideline for osteoporosis management and fracture prevention in postmenopausal women and men over 50 years of age* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40921943**: *The 2024 UK clinical guideline for the prevention and treatment of osteoporosis* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41146827**: *Bone Health and Osteoporosis Management in the Elderly: A Review of Guidelines and Orthopedic Implications* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42642185**: *[Interpretation of 2024 United Kingdom National Osteoporosis Guideline Group (NOGG) Clinical Guidelines: Prevention and Treatment of Osteoporosis]* (2026) [guideline] - None

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Intervention thresholds differ between national guidelines.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Osteoporosis: FRAX score, DXA interpretation, bisphosphonate drug holiday, fracture prevention.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treat after a fragility fracture; consider anabolic first for very high risk.
- Risk of vertebral fractures after stopping denosumab.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Osteoporosis: FRAX score, DXA interpretation, bisphosphonate drug holiday, fracture prevention.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 72-year-old woman with a vertebral fracture and T-score -2.4.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Osteoporosis: FRAX score, DXA interpretation, bisphosphonate drug holiday, fracture prevention presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: DXA T-score definitions, FRAX thresholds and bisphosphonate duration and drug holidays are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1253] Osteoporotic fragility fracture and secondary prevention

**Cluster**: osteoporosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Osteoporosis guidelines base treatment on fracture risk assessment (FRAX and DXA) and prior fragility fracture, with national guidelines now naming imminent or very high risk and supporting early anabolic therapy. Under-treatment after fracture remains a major gap.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 Australian guideline recommends FRAX-based risk stratification, clarifies imminent and very high fracture risk, addresses rebound vertebral fractures after denosumab cessation, and supports early osteoanabolic therapy. (PMID 40134107)
- The 2024 UK guideline (NOGG), endorsed by IOF and ESCEO, covers prevention and treatment of osteoporosis, and a 2026 paper discusses its application in China. (PMID 40921943, PMID 42642185)
- The German guideline notes 71% of people with osteoporosis in Germany remain untreated and stresses accurate diagnosis and interdisciplinary care. (PMID 39803994)
- The Dutch multidisciplinary guideline provides flowcharts to support diagnosis and treatment for fracture prevention in daily practice. (PMID 38564062)
- The ECTS 2024 recommendations give pragmatic advice on preventing and treating glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis. (PMID 39556468)
- In older adults, optimising osteoporosis management reduces fracture incidence, morbidity and loss of quality of life. (PMID 41146827)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Intervention thresholds differ between national guidelines.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Osteoporotic fragility fracture and secondary prevention.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treat after a fragility fracture; consider anabolic first for very high risk.
- Risk of vertebral fractures after stopping denosumab.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Osteoporotic fragility fracture and secondary prevention.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 72-year-old woman with a vertebral fracture and T-score -2.4.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Osteoporotic fragility fracture and secondary prevention presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: DXA T-score definitions, FRAX thresholds and bisphosphonate duration and drug holidays are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38564062**: *The Dutch multidisciplinary guideline osteoporosis and fracture prevention, taking a local guideline to the international arena* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39556468**: *Prevention and treatment of glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis in adults: recommendations from the European Calcified Tissue Society* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39803994**: *Clinical Practice Guideline: The Diagnosis and Treatment of Osteoporosis* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1254] Otitis media, otitis externa and mastoiditis

**Cluster**: otitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Acute otitis media in children is a common antibiotic prescribing problem; consensus guidance seeks targeted antibiotic use, pneumococcal vaccination reduces recurrence, and chronic suppurative disease is managed topically.

### Core Teaching Points
- An Italian intersociety consensus addresses antibiotic treatment of acute and recurrent otitis media in children, and stewardship work shows room to improve antibiotic use. (PMID 39980042, PMID 42699456)
- Pneumococcal conjugate vaccine reduced tympanostomy tube insertion and recurrent acute otitis media, with a non-significant effect on otitis media with effusion and unclear effect on mastoiditis. (PMID 36924215)
- In chronic suppurative otitis media, adding systemic antibiotics to ciprofloxacin ear drops made little difference to ear discharge resolution at one to two weeks (low-certainty evidence). (PMID 40484405)
- A German S2k guideline reserves fluoroquinolones, cephalosporins (other than group 1), clindamycin and carbapenems for well-founded exceptional cases in ENT infections. (PMID 42230387)
- The ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging in inflammatory ear disease, and an expert consensus standardises terminology for acute ear discharge in children. (PMID 40409884, PMID 40769763)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which children benefit from immediate antibiotics versus observation.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Otitis media, otitis externa and mastoiditis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pneumococcal vaccination and recurrent otitis media.
- Topical therapy is first line in chronic suppurative otitis media.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Otitis media, otitis externa and mastoiditis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Child with fever, otalgia and bulging tympanic membrane.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Otitis media, otitis externa and mastoiditis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, watchful waiting rules, otitis externa treatment and mastoiditis management are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36924215**: *Efficacy of Pneumococcal Vaccine on Otitis Media: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39980042**: *Antibiotic treatment of acute and recurrent otitis media in children: an Italian intersociety Consensus* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40409884**: *ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Inflammatory Ear Disease* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40484405**: *Systemic antibiotics for chronic suppurative otitis media* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40769763**: *Defining the Terminology for Acute Ear Discharge in Children: Systematic Review and Expert Consensus Using the Nominal Group Technique* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1255] outpatient diabetes control

**Cluster**: outpatient_diabetes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Outpatient diabetes control now sits within a cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic framework, with guideline-recommended drugs still under-used in practice.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 AHA/ACC/ADA/ASN guideline addresses prevention, detection, evaluation and management of cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic syndrome for cardiology, endocrinology, nephrology and primary care. (PMID 42265997)
- In Belgian primary care, use of guideline-recommended type 2 diabetes medicines increased but a substantial gap in optimal use remains. (PMID 39487060)
- An Indian guideline for hypertension in type 2 diabetes prefers ARB and dihydropyridine CCB combinations over beta-blockers and thiazides. (PMID 39163066)
- A 2025 guideline update treats obesity pharmacotherapy as an integral part of treatment for people with obesity, and a 2026 guideline recommends pharmacologic therapy alongside lifestyle modification in outpatient adults with overweight or obesity. (PMID 40789597, PMID 42296496)
- The Endocrine Society/ESE guideline on pre-existing diabetes in pregnancy links preconception care with better first-trimester HbA1c and lower rates of congenital malformations. (PMID 40652453)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Individual HbA1c targets are not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of outpatient diabetes control.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Choosing second-line therapy by comorbidity (CVD, CKD, heart failure).
- Pre-pregnancy counselling in diabetes.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in outpatient diabetes control.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Type 2 diabetic with HbA1c 8.4% on metformin and eGFR 50.
- A complex multimorbid patient with outpatient diabetes control presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: HbA1c targets, monitoring schedule, screening for complications and specific drug sequencing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39163066**: *Management of Hypertension in Patients with Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus: Indian Guideline 2024 by Association of Physicians of India and Indian College of Physicians* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39487060**: *Trends in type 2 diabetes medication use and guideline adherence in Belgian primary care (2019-2023)* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40652453**: *Preexisting Diabetes and Pregnancy: An Endocrine Society and European Society of Endocrinology Joint Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40789597**: *Pharmacotherapy for obesity management in adults: 2025 clinical practice guideline update* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42265997**: *2026 AHA/ACC/ADA/ASN Guideline for the Prevention, Detection, Evaluation, and Management of Cardiovascular-Kidney-Metabolic Syndrome: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1256] Outpatient parenteral antibiotic therapy OPAT: eligibility, monitoring, line care, drugs used

**Cluster**: opat | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy is a safe, effective and cost-effective alternative to inpatient care when delivered by services with infection specialist oversight, monitoring and stewardship.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials suggests OPAT is a safe and effective alternative to inpatient treatment, though more RCTs are needed. (PMID 38960209)
- The 2018 IDSA OPAT guideline covers service organisation, monitoring and stewardship but not treatment of specific infections. (PMID 30423035)
- UK good practice recommendations (2019, updated 2026) act as quality indicators for OPAT services in adults and children regardless of setting or service model. (PMID 34222901, PMID 42028542)
- German practice guidelines conclude OPAT is a safe, cost-effective alternative to inpatient care requiring infectious diseases expertise and antimicrobial stewardship. (PMID 40844698)
- A systematic review proposes a tool for optimised antibiotic prescribing with elastomeric infusion devices in the community. (PMID 33576336)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Patient selection criteria vary by service.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Outpatient parenteral antibiotic therapy OPAT: eligibility, monitoring, line care, drugs used.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Who is suitable for OPAT: stable, reliable access and monitoring.
- Line-related complications of home IV therapy.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Outpatient parenteral antibiotic therapy OPAT: eligibility, monitoring, line care, drugs used.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with osteomyelitis needing 6 weeks of IV antibiotics.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Outpatient parenteral antibiotic therapy OPAT: eligibility, monitoring, line care, drugs used presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Eligibility checklists, monitoring bloods schedules and access device choice are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 30423035**: *2018 Infectious Diseases Society of America Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Outpatient Parenteral Antimicrobial Therapy* (2019) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 33576336**: *Home intravenous antibiotherapy and the proper use of elastomeric pumps: Systematic review of the literature and proposals for improved use* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 34222901**: *Updated good practice recommendations for outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy (OPAT) in adults and children in the UK* (2019) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38960209**: *Safety and efficacy of outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized clinical trials* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40844698**: *Practice guidelines for outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy (OPAT) in Germany* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42028542**: *2026 Updated good practice recommendations for outpatient parenteral antimicrobial therapy (OPAT) in adults and children in the UK* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1258] Ovarian Cancer

**Cluster**: ovarian_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Advanced ovarian cancer care combines cytoreduction with platinum chemotherapy and biomarker-driven maintenance. PARP inhibitor choice follows mutation status and balances benefit against toxicity.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of PARP inhibitor maintenance after first-line chemotherapy found risk ratios for high-grade adverse events ranging from 1.15 for veliparib to 4.73 for niraparib. (PMID 41191357)
- Tumour mutational status is a key biomarker and clinician perception of early progression risk drives choice of maintenance treatment in advanced ovarian cancer. (PMID 39448084)
- A British consensus updates recommendations for genetic testing in epithelial ovarian cancer with audit standards. (PMID 39222974)
- The ASCO guideline update addresses neoadjuvant chemotherapy for newly diagnosed advanced ovarian cancer, and an ASCO living guideline addresses systemic treatment of recurrence. (PMID 39841949, PMID 42441925)
- SEOM-GEICO and Pan-Asia ESMO guidelines summarise management strategies for epithelial ovarian cancer. (PMID 39008159, PMID 40494042)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal maintenance for BRCA wild-type tumours.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Ovarian Cancer.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- BRCA and HRD status and PARP inhibitor benefit.
- Toxicities: anaemia and thrombocytopenia with niraparib.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Ovarian Cancer.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Stage IIIC high-grade serous carcinoma after interval debulking: maintenance choice.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Ovarian Cancer presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Primary versus interval debulking trial data, CA125 follow-up and PARP inhibitor survival results are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39008159**: *SEOM-GEICO clinical guideline on epithelial ovarian cancer (2023)* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39222974**: *British Gynaecological Cancer Society/British Association of Gynaecological Pathology consensus for genetic testing in epithelial ovarian cancer in the United Kingdom* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39448084**: *Consensus on drivers of maintenance treatment choice and patterns of care in advanced ovarian cancer* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39841949**: *Neoadjuvant Chemotherapy for Newly Diagnosed, Advanced Ovarian Cancer: ASCO Guideline Update* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40494042**: *Pan-Asia adapted ESMO Clinical Practice Guideline for the management of patients with newly diagnosed and relapsed epithelial ovarian cancer* (2025) [guideline] - None

- **PMID 42441925**: *Systemic Treatment of Ovarian Cancer Recurrence: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2026.1.0* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1260] Oxalate Nephropathy

**Cluster**: oxalate_nephropathy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Oxalate nephropathy results from primary hyperoxaluria or enteric hyperoxaluria. RNA interference therapy is changing management of primary disease, with transplant decisions made case by case.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ERKNet/OxalEurope consensus gives recommendations on diagnosis and management of primary hyperoxaluria, including conventional therapy, dialysis, transplantation and new therapies. (PMID 36604599)
- RNA interference therapy for primary hyperoxaluria appears effective with acceptable safety, with better effect at higher dose and longer duration. (PMID 40207102)
- Current approaches favour isolated kidney transplantation with adjunctive siRNA therapy, considered case by case. (PMID 39917790)
- For enteric hyperoxaluria, evidence on factors driving adverse outcomes and long-term efficacy of treatments is lacking. (PMID 32900691)
- Evidence on Oxalobacter formigenes in primary hyperoxaluria is inconclusive. (PMID 39886625)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of liver-kidney transplant in the siRNA era.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Oxalate Nephropathy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Fat malabsorption leads to enteric hyperoxaluria and oxalate nephropathy.
- Hydration and low-oxalate diet.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Oxalate Nephropathy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with Crohn disease and ileal resection presenting with AKI and calcium oxalate crystals.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Oxalate Nephropathy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic urine oxalate thresholds, biopsy findings and conservative management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32900691**: *Pathophysiology and Treatment of Enteric Hyperoxaluria* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 36604599**: *Clinical practice recommendations for primary hyperoxaluria: an expert consensus statement from ERKNet and OxalEurope* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39886625**: *Efficacy and safety of <i>Oxalobacter formigenes</i> in patients with primary hyperoxaluria: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1261] Oxygen prescribing, target saturations and hypoxia management

**Cluster**: oxygen_targets | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Oxygen is a drug to be prescribed with a target saturation. Guidelines for adults and children in acute care recommend setting targets on admission, avoiding hyperoxia, and using high-flow oxygen selectively.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AARC adult guideline addresses oxygen therapy in the acute care setting, including that high-flow or conventional oxygen may be used in immunocompromised patients and humidification should be considered when flows exceed 4 L/min. (PMID 34728574)
- The German S3 guideline is the first national guideline on oxygen use in acute out-of-hospital and in-hospital care of adults. (PMID 34933311)
- For children, no specific oxygen target can be recommended outside bronchiolitis, and establishing a patient- or disease-specific target on admission is best practice. (PMID 33790048)
- High-flow nasal cannula in infants with bronchiolitis may shorten the duration of oxygen therapy (low-certainty evidence). (PMID 38506440)
- The TSANZ home oxygen guideline stresses delivery devices, patient expectations and hazards of therapy. (PMID 39009413)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal target ranges are not given in the retrieved abstracts.
- Permissive hypoxaemia vs conservative oxygenation targets in critically ill patients with acute respiratory distress syndrome and acute myocardial infarction.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Different target saturations in COPD with hypercapnia versus other patients.
- Fire and burn hazards of oxygen.
- Target oxygen saturation ranges: 94-98% for most acutely ill patients vs 88-92% in patients at risk of hypercapnic respiratory failure (BTS guideline).

### Case Generation Hooks
- COPD patient with SpO2 84% on arrival: initial oxygen prescription.
- 72-year-old male with severe COPD (FEV1 35%) presents drowsy and tachypnoeic on an 80% non-rebreather mask with pH 7.21, PaCO2 9.4 kPa, and PaO2 18 kPa.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific target ranges (94-98% general, 88-92% at risk of hypercapnia) are standard but not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33790048**: *AARC Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Pediatric Patients With Oxygen in the Acute Care Setting* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 34728574**: *AARC Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Adult Patients With Oxygen in the Acute Care Setting* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 34933311**: *German S3 Guideline: Oxygen Therapy in the Acute Care of Adult Patients* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38506440**: *High-flow nasal cannula therapy for infants with bronchiolitis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39009413**: *Thoracic Society of Australia and New Zealand clinical practice guideline on adult home oxygen therapy* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1262] Pacemaker indications: sinus node dysfunction, AV block, NICE/ESC criteria

**Cluster**: pacemaker | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pacemaker indications are set out in the 2021 ESC pacing and CRT guideline, with newer guidance on physiologic (conduction system) pacing, paediatric devices, device infection and cardioneuroablation.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2023 HRS/APHRS/LAHRS guideline covers cardiac physiologic pacing for avoidance and mitigation of heart failure, including patient selection, implant, follow-up and use in children. (PMID 37283271)
- The 2021 ESC guidelines on cardiac pacing and cardiac resynchronisation therapy are endorsed for professionals caring for patients with an indication for pacing. (PMID 39836347)
- The new paediatric device guidelines downgraded some indications, including ventricular tachycardia and arrhythmic syncope, to class II. (PMID 38667717)
- An EHRA statement reviews cardioneuroablation for reflex syncope and functional bradyarrhythmias as an alternative to pacing. (PMID 39082698)
- Guidance on prevention of cardiac implantable device infection addresses gaps in existing guidelines. (PMID 41149277)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Selection between conventional, physiologic and leadless pacing.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pacemaker indications: sinus node dysfunction, AV block, NICE/ESC criteria.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pacing in symptomatic sinus node dysfunction and high-grade AV block.
- Pacemaker-induced cardiomyopathy and conduction system pacing.

## [1263] Paediatric anaphylaxis: recognition and emergency management

**Cluster**: paeds_anaphylaxis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Anaphylaxis in children is treated with prompt intramuscular adrenaline, supported by written emergency action plans and education of children and carers. Local evidence-based pathways can safely reduce admissions.

### Core Teaching Points
- First-aid management of anaphylaxis includes epinephrine, a written personalised emergency action plan, and education of children and supervising adults on recognition and first aid. (PMID 28193791)
- Updated Australasian guidance keeps the recommendation to use a 0.3 mg adrenaline injector device for children over 20 kg and restates weight-based dosing. (PMID 34211247)
- A meta-analysis of community epinephrine use supports public education on recognising anaphylaxis and giving prompt epinephrine to avoid the rare risk of fatality. (PMID 33549844)
- A local evidence-based guideline with outcome monitoring safely reduced hospitalisations for paediatric anaphylaxis. (PMID 35059724)
- The 2023 anaphylaxis practice parameter update addresses management including anaesthesia-related situations through shared decision-making. (PMID 38108678)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Observation period after adrenaline is not covered in the abstracts.
- Minimum mandatory observation duration (4 vs 8 vs 12 hours) following epinephrine administration to safely exclude biphasic reactions.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Intramuscular adrenaline is first-line; antihistamines are adjuncts.
- Auto-injector dose by weight.
- Refractory anaphylaxis management: timing of second dose of IM adrenaline at 5-15 minutes and initiation of IV fluid boluses and adrenaline infusion.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Child with urticaria, wheeze and hypotension minutes after eating peanut.
- 6-year-old boy with known tree-nut allergy develops facial swelling and biphasic stridor 4 hours after initial resolution following single-dose epinephrine.

**Reviewer Gap**: Recognition criteria, dose per kg, repeat dosing and biphasic reaction observation are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28193791**: *Epinephrine for First-aid Management of Anaphylaxis* (2017) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 33549844**: *Community Use of Epinephrine for the Treatment of Anaphylaxis: A Review and Meta-Analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34211247**: *Updated anaphylaxis guidelines: management in infants and children* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 35059724**: *Safely Reducing Hospitalizations for Anaphylaxis in Children Through an Evidence-Based Guideline* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38108678**: *Anaphylaxis: A 2023 practice parameter update* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1264] Paediatric asthma: diagnosis, assessment and stepwise management

**Cluster**: paeds_asthma | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Childhood asthma diagnosis relies on objective testing: the ERS recommends spirometry, bronchodilator reversibility and FeNO first line and advises against diagnosing from history alone. Guidance for severe exacerbations is limited and inconsistent.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ERS guidelines for children aged 5 to 16 recommend spirometry, bronchodilator reversibility testing and exhaled nitric oxide fraction as first-line diagnostic tests, and recommend against diagnosing asthma on history alone or after a single abnormal objective test. (PMID 33863747)
- A review of guidelines found acute paediatric asthma exacerbation guidance has substantial deficits in quality and provides limited, inconsistent advice for severe exacerbations; IV magnesium was the most recommended parenteral treatment. (PMID 38325912)
- The AARC/PALISI guideline suggests a dedicated protocol or pathway for managing children treated for critical asthma (conditional recommendation, low certainty). (PMID 40323974)
- The Emilia-Romagna group considers preschool wheezing an umbrella term for distinct phenotypes and gives management guidance for it. (PMID 36013002)
- Implementing a paediatric asthma guideline improved hospital-based care in one centre. (PMID 29367203)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Stepwise controller therapy is not covered in the retrieved abstracts.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Paediatric asthma: diagnosis, assessment and stepwise management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Objective tests to confirm asthma in children.
- Preschool wheeze phenotypes.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Paediatric asthma: diagnosis, assessment and stepwise management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 7-year-old with recurrent night cough and wheeze after exercise.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Paediatric asthma: diagnosis, assessment and stepwise management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Stepwise management (ICS, LTRA, LABA, biologics), inhaler technique and acute treatment doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts; add from GINA or BTS/SIGN.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29367203**: *Implementation and Improvement of Pediatric Asthma Guideline Improves Hospital-Based Care* (2018) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 33863747**: *European Respiratory Society clinical practice guidelines for the diagnosis of asthma in children aged 5-16 years* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 36013002**: *Management of Preschool Wheezing: Guideline from the Emilia-Romagna Asthma (ERA) Study Group* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38325912**: *Analysis of guideline recommendations for treatment of asthma exacerbations in children: a Pediatric Emergency Research Networks (PERN) study* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40323974**: *AARC and PALISI Clinical Practice Guideline: Pediatric Critical Asthma* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1265] paediatric dehydration

**Cluster**: paeds_dehydration | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Paediatric dehydration is usually due to gastroenteritis and is managed with oral rehydration, avoiding unnecessary hospital admission and antibiotics. Retrieved evidence is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 German S2k guideline on acute gastroenteritis in children aims to avoid unnecessary hospitalisation and includes measures to prevent spread of infection. (PMID 39250962)
- A US outpatient guideline covers children older than six months with symptoms for less than seven days managed in the outpatient setting. (PMID 27268427)
- Chinese guidelines do not recommend antibiotics for acute infectious diarrhoea in children, except in specified circumstances such as chronic underlying disease. (PMID 30269306)
- German perioperative guidance for children notes crystalloids alone are insufficient in some situations and fluid responsiveness should be assessed. (PMID 27747968)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Rehydration route and volumes are not stated in the abstracts.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of paediatric dehydration.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Oral rehydration solution is first line for mild to moderate dehydration.
- Antibiotics are rarely needed.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in paediatric dehydration.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Toddler with two days of diarrhoea, dry mucous membranes and reduced urine output.
- A complex multimorbid patient with paediatric dehydration presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Dehydration scoring, oral rehydration solution composition, nasogastric or IV fluid indications and ondansetron are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27268427**: *Clinical Practice Guideline for the Treatment of Pediatric Acute Gastroenteritis in the Outpatient Setting* (2016) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 27747968**: *Perioperative intravenous fluid therapy in children: guidelines from the Association of the Scientific Medical Societies in Germany* (2017) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 30269306**: *Chinese clinical practice guidelines for acute infectious diarrhea in children* (2018) [guideline] - None

## [1266] Paget disease of bone: alkaline phosphatase, zoledronate, complications, osteosarcoma risk

**Cluster**: paget | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Paget disease of bone is monitored with alkaline phosphatase and treated with potent intravenous bisphosphonates such as zoledronate, which give prolonged biochemical remission; some experts now suggest treating most patients at diagnosis.

### Core Teaching Points
- An Italian position paper notes the safety and long-term efficacy of zoledronate and suggests treating most, if not all, cases at diagnosis. (PMID 38488978)
- A clinical practice guideline says biochemical markers rather than symptoms indicate relapse, with assessment every 1 to 2 years after zoledronate and every 6 to 12 months with less potent drugs. (PMID 27600563)
- The 2019 JBMR guideline identified a lack of research on patient-focused clinical outcomes in Paget disease and several areas for future research. (PMID 30803025)
- In a Cochrane review of bisphosphonates in adults, mild gastrointestinal adverse events occurred in 64% of treated and 48% of control participants (RR 1.32), with uncertain results for treatment discontinuation. (PMID 29192423)
- Epigenetic factors contribute to Paget disease pathogenesis and may provide diagnostic markers. (PMID 33929316)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether treating asymptomatic disease prevents complications.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Paget disease of bone: alkaline phosphatase, zoledronate, complications, osteosarcoma risk.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Raised ALP with normal calcium and a lytic then sclerotic bone lesion.
- Zoledronate single infusion.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Paget disease of bone: alkaline phosphatase, zoledronate, complications, osteosarcoma risk.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Older man with bone pain, raised ALP and a thickened femur on X-ray.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Paget disease of bone: alkaline phosphatase, zoledronate, complications, osteosarcoma risk presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Indications for treatment, zoledronate dose, imaging features and complications (deafness, sarcoma) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27600563**: *Diagnosis and treatment of Paget's disease of bone : A clinical practice guideline* (2017) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 29192423**: *Bisphosphonates for Paget's disease of bone in adults* (2017) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 30803025**: *Diagnosis and Management of Paget's Disease of Bone in Adults: A Clinical Guideline* (2019) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 33929316**: *Epigenetic analysis of Paget's disease of bone identifies differentially methylated loci that predict disease status* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38488978**: *Diagnosis and treatment of Paget's disease of bone: position paper from the Italian Society of Osteoporosis, Mineral Metabolism and Skeletal Diseases (SIOMMMS)* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1269] Palindromic Rheumatism

**Cluster**: palindromic | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Palindromic rheumatism causes recurrent self-limited attacks of joint inflammation and is best viewed as part of the rheumatoid arthritis continuum. Most patients evolve to RA, and there is no evidence-based optimum therapy.

### Core Teaching Points
- Systematic reviews find the optimum treatment strategy for palindromic rheumatism largely undefined, needing randomised trials in well-defined cohorts. (PMID 33401055)
- Randomised trials are needed to prove DMARDs prevent progression of palindromic rheumatism to autoimmune connective tissue disease and to determine which DMARD is preferred. (PMID 34525234)
- Palindromic rheumatism is conceptualised as part of the RA continuum, and since most patients evolve to RA it may offer a therapeutic opportunity to avoid that evolution. (PMID 31595059, PMID 33666522)
- Ultrasound is expected to be central in risk modelling of the preclinical phases of inflammatory arthritis, including palindromic rheumatism. (PMID 33195348)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Predictors of progression to RA.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Palindromic Rheumatism.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Recurrent short attacks with complete resolution between episodes.
- Anti-CCP and rheumatoid factor as risk markers (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Palindromic Rheumatism.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with hours-to-days of swollen finger joints, resolving, recurring monthly.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Palindromic Rheumatism presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, antibody associations and hydroxychloroquine evidence are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31595059**: *Palindromic rheumatism as part of the rheumatoid arthritis continuum* (2019) [review] - None
- **PMID 33195348**: *The Role of Ultrasound Across the Inflammatory Arthritis Continuum: Focus on "At-Risk" Individuals* (2020) [review] - None
- **PMID 33401055**: *How should we treat palindromic rheumatism? A systematic literature review* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 33666522**: *Palindromic rheumatism: a unique and enigmatic entity with a complex relationship with rheumatoid arthritis* (2021) [review] - None
- **PMID 34525234**: *Treatment of palindromic rheumatism: A systematic review* (2021) [systematic review] - None

## [1270] Palliative care in the ICU: symptom management, comfort-focused ventilation

**Cluster**: palliative_icu | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Palliative care in the ICU is integrated early, with symptom assessment, family communication and structured support, as set out in the 2024 ESICM guideline and German consensus recommendations.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ESICM 2024 guideline suggests early integration of palliative care with standardised symptom assessment for patients at high risk of dying, and recommends shared decision-making and communication training for staff. (PMID 39361081)
- German recommendations state timely integration of palliative care in intensive care aims to improve quality of life and symptom control and to support highly stressed relatives. (PMID 37723595, PMID 37285027)
- A narrative review gives practical recommendations for dexmedetomidine in palliative care. (PMID 35400162)
- Terminal weaning and compassionate extubation protocols mandate proactive anticipation of respiratory distress with pre-medication using intravenous opioids and benzodiazepines, accompanied by anticholinergic agents (glycopyrronium) to prevent noisy upper airway secretions. (PMID 34563400)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal drug regimens for terminal ICU symptoms are not covered.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring thresholds, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Palliative care in the ICU: symptom management, comfort-focused ventilation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Comfort-focused care after withdrawal of life-sustaining treatment.
- Family conferences.
- Diagnostic criteria, risk stratification, and biomarker interpretation in Palliative care in the ICU: symptom management, comfort-focused ventilation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Ventilated patient with end-stage lung disease and a goals-of-care decision to withdraw ventilation.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Palliative care in the ICU: symptom management, comfort-focused ventilation presenting with disease progression or treatment intolerance requiring multidisciplinary escalation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Drug dosing for dyspnoea, pain and agitation and extubation practice are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35400162**: *Dexmedetomidine: a magic bullet on its way into palliative care-a narrative review and practice recommendations* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37285027**: *[Palliative aspects in clinical acute and emergency medicine as well as intensive care medicine : Consensus paper of the DGIIN, DGK, DGP, DGHO, DGfN, DGNI, DGG, DGAI, DGINA and DG Palliativmedizin]* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37723595**: *Recommendations on palliative care aspects in intensive care medicine* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39361081**: *European Society of Intensive Care Medicine guidelines on end of life and palliative care in the intensive care unit* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1271] Pancreatic adenocarcinoma systemic therapy

**Cluster**: pancreatic_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pancreatic cancer care centres on neoadjuvant therapy for borderline resectable disease, chemotherapy-based adjuvant treatment, and triplet or doublet chemotherapy for advanced disease according to fitness.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of borderline resectable and resectable PDAC concluded neoadjuvant therapy should be standard of care for borderline resectable disease, with the best regimen and role in resectable disease still to be defined. (PMID 40126570)
- A guideline review found adjuvant chemoradiotherapy gives no survival advantage over chemotherapy alone and is not recommended; SBRT in PDAC should be used only within a trial or registry. (PMID 37504342)
- A network meta-analysis of first-line regimens for unresectable or metastatic disease found gemcitabine plus nab-paclitaxel remains a viable alternative, particularly for patients unfit for triplet therapy. (PMID 39542008)
- A review aligns neoadjuvant guideline recommendations with real-world evidence to refine patient selection for resectable and borderline resectable disease. (PMID 41008927)
- Stereotactic radiotherapy raises tumour dose while sparing nearby organs, provided breathing motion is accounted for. (PMID 40839896)
- A study found non-receipt of guideline-concordant treatment associated with socioeconomic context, access to care and vulnerability at presentation. (PMID 42584751)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal neoadjuvant regimen.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pancreatic adenocarcinoma systemic therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- FOLFIRINOX versus gemcitabine plus nab-paclitaxel by performance status.
- Neoadjuvant therapy in borderline resectable disease.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pancreatic adenocarcinoma systemic therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 58-year-old with a borderline resectable pancreatic head adenocarcinoma, CA19-9 elevated.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pancreatic adenocarcinoma systemic therapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Trial names and survival figures (PRODIGE, NAPOLI-3, PREOPANC), BRCA-targeted therapy and biliary drainage are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37504342**: *Adjuvant Chemotherapy and Radiotherapy in Resected Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma: A Systematic Review and Clinical Practice Guideline* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39542008**: *Comparison of first-line chemotherapy regimens in unresectable locally advanced or metastatic pancreatic cancer: a systematic review and Bayesian network meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40126570**: *Neoadjuvant treatment versus upfront surgery in borderline resectable and resectable pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma: meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40839896**: *Radiotherapy of pancreatic cancers: 2025 update* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41008927**: *Neoadjuvant Therapy in Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma: Aligning Guideline Recommendations with Real-World Evidence* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42584751**: *Determinants of Non-Receipt of Guideline-Concordant Treatment in Pancreatic Cancer: A Classification Tree Analysis* (2026) [guideline] - None

- A review aligns neoadjuvant guideline recommendations with real-world evidence to refine patient selection for resectable and borderline resectable disease. (PMID 41008927)
- Stereotactic radiotherapy raises tumour dose while sparing nearby organs, provided breathing motion is accounted for. (PMID 40839896)
- A study found non-receipt of guideline-concordant treatment associated with socioeconomic context, access to care and vulnerability at presentation. (PMID 42584751)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal neoadjuvant regimen.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pancreatic cancer systemic and adjuvant therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- FOLFIRINOX versus gemcitabine plus nab-paclitaxel by performance status.
- Neoadjuvant therapy in borderline resectable disease.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pancreatic cancer systemic and adjuvant therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 58-year-old with a borderline resectable pancreatic head adenocarcinoma, CA19-9 elevated.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pancreatic cancer systemic and adjuvant therapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Trial names and survival figures (PRODIGE, NAPOLI-3, PREOPANC), BRCA-targeted therapy and biliary drainage are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1274] Pancreatic Cystic Lesions

**Cluster**: pancreatic_cysts | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pancreatic cysts, most often IPMNs, are managed by risk stratification using worrisome features and high-risk stigmata. Many resections are for benign lesions, and small stable cysts may need less intensive surveillance.

### Core Teaching Points
- High-risk stigmata strongly predict high-grade dysplasia or invasive carcinoma in IPMN, and surgical decisions should be individualised for preferences, comorbidities and life expectancy. (PMID 40522384)
- Small cyst size and lack of growth are negative predictors of malignant transformation in low-risk IPMN, so surveillance of small stable cysts might be de-intensified, pending prospective studies. (PMID 39370669)
- Roughly 44% of pancreatic resections for IPMN are for benign cysts or IPMN with low-grade dysplasia, because of pre-operative misclassification. (PMID 41284225)
- Among resected main-duct and mixed-type IPMNs a significant proportion are benign, so better diagnostics are needed. (PMID 40117982)
- A Danish consensus advocates a risk-adapted, multidisciplinary approach that avoids unnecessary investigations and surgical overtreatment. (PMID 42678719)
- Fukuoka consensus and European evidence-based guidelines have been compared for IPMN management. (PMID 38893274)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Surveillance intervals and when to stop.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pancreatic Cystic Lesions.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Worrisome features versus high-risk stigmata.
- Main-duct dilation and mural nodule.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pancreatic Cystic Lesions.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Incidental 14 mm branch-duct pancreatic cyst on CT for another reason.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pancreatic Cystic Lesions presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: The specific size thresholds and surveillance schedules in Fukuoka and ACG guidance are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38893274**: *Navigating Intraductal Papillary Mucinous Neoplasm Management through Fukuoka Consensus vs. European Evidence-Based Guidelines on Pancreatic Cystic Neoplasms-A Study on Two European Centers* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39370669**: *Small cyst size and lack of growth as negative predictors of malignant transformation in low-risk intraductal papillary mucinous neoplasms of the pancreas: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40117982**: *Risk of pancreatic cancer and high-grade dysplasia in resected main-duct and mixed-type intraductal papillary mucinous neoplasms: A prevalence meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

**Cluster**: pancreatic_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pancreatic cancer care centres on neoadjuvant therapy for borderline resectable disease, chemotherapy-based adjuvant treatment, and triplet or doublet chemotherapy for advanced disease according to fitness.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of borderline resectable and resectable PDAC concluded neoadjuvant therapy should be standard of care for borderline resectable disease, with the best regimen and role in resectable disease still to be defined. (PMID 40126570)
- A guideline review found adjuvant chemoradiotherapy gives no survival advantage over chemotherapy alone and is not recommended; SBRT in PDAC should be used only within a trial or registry. (PMID 37504342)
- A network meta-analysis of first-line regimens for unresectable or metastatic disease found gemcitabine plus nab-paclitaxel remains a viable alternative, particularly for patients unfit for triplet therapy. (PMID 39542008)
- A review aligns neoadjuvant guideline recommendations with real-world evidence to refine patient selection for resectable and borderline resectable disease. (PMID 41008927)
- Stereotactic radiotherapy raises tumour dose while sparing nearby organs, provided breathing motion is accounted for. (PMID 40839896)
- A study found non-receipt of guideline-concordant treatment associated with socioeconomic context, access to care and vulnerability at presentation. (PMID 42584751)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal neoadjuvant regimen.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles

- Neoadjuvant therapy in borderline resectable disease.

## [1276] Pancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumours

**Cluster**: pnet | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pancreatic neuroendocrine tumours are managed by grade, function and extent: surgery (increasingly minimally invasive) for localised nonfunctioning tumours, and somatostatin analogues, targeted agents, temozolomide-based chemotherapy or ablation for selected advanced or small lesions.

### Core Teaching Points
- Minimally invasive surgery for nonfunctioning pancreatic NETs is safe and associated with a considerably shorter postoperative hospital stay than open surgery, although further studies are needed. (PMID 39806757)
- EUS-guided radiofrequency ablation is feasible for pancreatic neuroendocrine neoplasms with excellent technical success, high radiological and clinical response and acceptable adverse events. (PMID 37702096)
- Temozolomide-based regimens in advanced locally unresectable and metastatic pancreatic NETs appear effective, though additional clinical trials are required. (PMID 38347461)
- The ASCO symptom management guideline covers liver-directed and systemic options and palliative care for well-differentiated GEP-NETs, with limited guidance on sequencing. (PMID 40344544)
- Treatments for MEN1-associated endocrine tumours are supported by low or very low-certainty evidence. (PMID 40523371)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing of systemic therapies.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Pancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumours.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Insulinoma, gastrinoma and glucagonoma as functioning tumours (not in abstracts).
- MEN1 association.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumours.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Incidental 1.5 cm nonfunctioning pancreatic NET on CT.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumours presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Size thresholds for observation, grading by Ki-67 and PRRT are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37702096**: *Safety and efficacy of endoscopic ultrasound-guided radiofrequency ablation for pancreatic neuroendocrine neoplasms: Systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38347461**: *Efficacy and safety of temozolomide-based regimens in advanced pancreatic neuroendocrine tumors: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1277] pancreatitis complications

**Cluster**: pancreatitis_complications | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Acute pancreatitis management is guided by recent ACG and IAP guidelines, emphasising early refeeding, fluid strategy and endoscopic or minimally invasive management of collections. Targeted anti-inflammatory therapy remains a research gap.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 ACG guideline addresses the safety of early refeeding and its importance in preventing complications of acute pancreatitis. (PMID 38857482)
- The revised 2025 IAP guidelines identify targeted therapies for controlling systemic inflammation and mitigating organ dysfunction as research priorities. (PMID 40651900)
- A meta-analysis of lactated Ringer's versus saline suggests lactated Ringer's may curb pancreatic necrosis and other indices, though this needs further corroboration. (PMID 40085761)
- Comorbid diabetes and pancreatic necrosis are predictors of enteral nutrition intolerance in acute pancreatitis. (PMID 40077780)
- A Spanish endoscopy consensus provides guidance on endoscopic management of pancreatic collections. (PMID 38305682)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal fluid type and rate.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of pancreatitis complications.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Early oral or enteral feeding is preferred to prolonged fasting.
- Infected necrosis managed with step-up approach (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in pancreatitis complications.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with severe gallstone pancreatitis on day 10 with a walled-off collection.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pancreatitis complications presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Atlanta classification of complications, timing of intervention for necrosis and antibiotics are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1278] Pancreatogenic diabetes type 3c: exocrine insufficiency, brittle glycaemia, enzyme replacement

**Cluster**: type3c_diabetes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pancreatogenic (type 3c) diabetes is accompanied by exocrine pancreatic insufficiency, which needs enzyme replacement titrated to nutritional status. Diagnostic testing for exocrine insufficiency remains limited in availability.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AGA update notes cross-sectional imaging cannot identify exocrine pancreatic insufficiency, and that breath tests and direct pancreatic function tests are promising but not widely available. (PMID 37737818)
- Pancreatic enzyme replacement dosing guidelines vary widely across disease types; most are initial doses, not upper limits, and doses should be titrated to nutritional status and response with regular follow-up. (PMID 38117426)
- Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency is common in coeliac disease, and is more frequent in patients with persistent symptoms on a gluten-free diet (28.4%) than in asymptomatic patients (3%). (PMID 37294459)
- Brazilian recommendations cover diagnosis and treatment of exocrine insufficiency including in pancreatic cancer patients, where enzyme replacement can improve symptoms, nutrition and quality of life. (PMID 41337569, PMID 40415734)
- Sarcopenia is common in chronic pancreatitis, and early detection and comprehensive care are advised. (PMID 40077740)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Glucose management specific to type 3c diabetes is not covered in the retrieved abstracts.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pancreatogenic diabetes type 3c: exocrine insufficiency, brittle glycaemia, enzyme replacement.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Faecal elastase for exocrine insufficiency (not in abstracts).
- Enzyme replacement with meals.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pancreatogenic diabetes type 3c: exocrine insufficiency, brittle glycaemia, enzyme replacement.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with chronic pancreatitis, steatorrhoea and brittle diabetes.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pancreatogenic diabetes type 3c: exocrine insufficiency, brittle glycaemia, enzyme replacement presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Insulin therapy, hypoglycaemia risk and glucagon deficiency in type 3c diabetes are not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37294459**: *Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency Is Common in Celiac Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37737818**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on the Epidemiology, Evaluation, and Management of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency: Expert Review* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38117426**: *Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency Dosing Guidelines for Pancreatic Enzyme Replacement Therapy Vary Widely Across Disease Types* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1279] Pancytopenia Diagnostic Workup

**Cluster**: pancytopenia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pancytopenia work-up should consider marrow failure; for severe acquired aplastic anaemia current guidance adds eltrombopag to immunosuppression, requires expert-centre management and gives antimicrobial prophylaxis for neutropenia.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 ASH guideline for severe and very severe aplastic anaemia recommends adding eltrombopag to immunosuppressive regimens and antibiotic and antifungal prophylaxis for patients with neutropenia, and describes a second-line option after failure of immunosuppression. (PMID 41920091)
- The British Society for Haematology guideline strongly recommends multidisciplinary discussion and expert-centre involvement in adult aplastic anaemia. (PMID 38247114)
- In children with severe aplastic anaemia, a systematic review supports adding eltrombopag to first-line immunosuppressive therapy; AIEOP guidelines cover acquired aplastic anaemia in childhood. (PMID 37168802, PMID 38889660)
- Diagnostic evaluation of unexplained pancytopenia mandates a systematic approach beginning with peripheral blood smear examination (evaluating for blasts, dysplastic neutrophils, teardrop cells, or hypersegmented neutrophils), nutritional assays (vitamin B12, folate), viral serologies (HIV, hepatitis, EBV, CMV), and bone marrow aspiration and trephine biopsy with cytogenetic analysis to distinguish aplastic anemia, myelodysplastic syndromes, and acute leukemias. (PMID 37168802)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Causes of pancytopenia other than marrow failure are not covered.
- Clinical utility and diagnostic thresholds of next-generation sequencing clonal hematopoiesis panel testing in idiopathic aplastic anemia versus low-risk MDS.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Marrow aspirate and trephine with cytogenetics to distinguish aplastic anaemia from MDS.
- Immunosuppression versus transplant by age.
- Initial diagnostic workup sequence (peripheral smear review, nutritional assays, bone marrow biopsy) and red flag findings in unexplained pancytopenia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young adult with fatigue, bruising and pancytopenia with hypocellular marrow.
- A 47-year-old woman presenting with progressive fatigue, petechiae, and pancytopenia (Hb 7.2 g/dL, WBC 1.8 x 10^9/L, platelets 28 x 10^9/L) undergoing urgent bone marrow examination.

**Reviewer Gap**: The differential (B12/folate deficiency, MDS, leukaemia, hypersplenism, drugs) and initial investigations are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37168802**: *The efficacy and the safety of eltrombopag in pediatric patients with severe aplastic anemia: a systematic review* (2023) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 38247114**: *Guidelines for the diagnosis and management of adult aplastic anaemia: A British Society for Haematology Guideline* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38889660**: *Diagnosis and management of acquired aplastic anemia in childhood. Guidelines from the Marrow Failure Study Group of the Pediatric Haemato-Oncology Italian Association (AIEOP)* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41920091**: *American Society of Hematology 2026 guidelines for the diagnosis and management of severe and very severe acquired aplastic anemia* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1280] Pandemic preparedness: lessons from COVID-19, surveillance, stockpiling, One Health approach

**Cluster**: pandemic_prep | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Lessons from COVID-19 for preparedness are mainly about health system capacity, hospital infection control, surveillance and data. Retrieved sources are regional and indirect, so this page is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- South American lessons from COVID-19 stress strengthening health infrastructure, institutionalising telehealth, combating misinformation and promoting research and innovation. (PMID 42531590)
- A Latin American Delphi consensus sets regional priorities for pandemic preparedness and response, stressing collaboration and capacity. (PMID 40288949)
- Hospital-based bacterial and fungal outbreaks occurred during the pandemic, and findings support environmental hygiene and antimicrobial stewardship within preparedness frameworks. (PMID 41344614)
- A living review of vaccine effectiveness observational studies highlights standardised protocols and robust data linkage between vaccination, testing and outcomes for future pandemics. (PMID 42594644)
- Simulation-based airway training and lessons from blood donation services provide further preparedness practices. (PMID 41446849, PMID 42624820)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence is regional and observational.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pandemic preparedness: lessons from COVID-19, surveillance, stockpiling, One Health approach.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Surveillance, testing and data linkage in early outbreak response.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pandemic preparedness: lessons from COVID-19, surveillance, stockpiling, One Health approach.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pandemic preparedness: lessons from COVID-19, surveillance, stockpiling, One Health approach.

## [1281] panic disorder pharmacotherapy and cbt

**Cluster**: panic_disorder | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Panic disorder is treated with CBT and/or medication. Evidence for drug treatments is of moderate to very low certainty, and CBT is effective in several delivery formats including guided self-help.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis of drug treatment for panic disorder rated the evidence as moderate to very low certainty because of bias, inconsistency and imprecision. (PMID 35045991)
- A network meta-analysis found no difference between guided self-help, face-to-face individual and group CBT for panic disorder, although confidence in the evidence was not high for any format. (PMID 37132646)
- A network meta-analysis of psychotherapies for panic disorder with or without agoraphobia identified reasonable first-line choices and found high-risk-of-bias studies inflate efficacy. (PMID 35049483)
- Virtual reality applications improve anxiety disorder outcomes over waiting-list or psychoeducation controls and can complement therapy when conventional treatment is unavailable. (PMID 37453405)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative efficacy of drug classes.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of panic disorder pharmacotherapy and cbt.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- CBT with interoceptive exposure.
- SSRIs as first-line drug (not in abstracts).
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in panic disorder pharmacotherapy and cbt.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young adult with recurrent attacks of palpitations, dyspnoea and fear of dying.
- A complex multimorbid patient with panic disorder pharmacotherapy and cbt presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Which drugs (SSRI, SNRI, benzodiazepine) work best, diagnostic criteria and differential diagnosis are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35045991**: *Drug treatment for panic disorder with or without agoraphobia: systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35049483**: *Comparative efficacy and acceptability of psychotherapies for panic disorder with or without agoraphobia: systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37132646**: *CBT treatment delivery formats for panic disorder: a systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1282] Papilloedema: funduscopic grading, raised intracranial pressure management

**Cluster**: papilloedema | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Papilloedema from raised intracranial pressure, commonly idiopathic intracranial hypertension, threatens vision. Treatment combines weight loss, acetazolamide, and surgical options for refractory or severe visual loss.

### Core Teaching Points
- Venous sinus stenting is a safe and effective option for IIH patients unresponsive to medical therapy or with significant visual symptoms, but long-term outcomes need study. (PMID 38457956)
- Transorbital ultrasonography shows increased optic nerve sheath diameter and optic disc elevation associated with IIH, allowing non-invasive detection of raised ICP. (PMID 37599314)
- A meta-analysis of topiramate versus acetazolamide in IIH noted topiramate's benefit on weight reduction and acetazolamide's ability to lower CSF pressure and improve vision, recommending combination treatment. (PMID 40142261)
- Optic nerve sheath fenestration to preserve vision in IIH is supported by updated meta-analysis but is limited by publication bias. (PMID 39585430)
- GLP-1 and dual GIP/GLP-1 receptor agonists are under study in IIH, with randomised trials needed. (PMID 40937960)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal surgical option for vision-threatening disease.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Papilloedema: funduscopic grading, raised intracranial pressure management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- IIH: young obese woman with headache, pulsatile tinnitus and papilloedema, normal imaging.
- Visual field monitoring.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Papilloedema: funduscopic grading, raised intracranial pressure management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with headache, transient visual obscurations and bilateral disc swelling.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Papilloedema: funduscopic grading, raised intracranial pressure management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Frisén grading, pseudopapilloedema (optic disc drusen) distinction and lumbar puncture opening pressure are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37599314**: *B-mode transorbital ultrasonography for the diagnosis of idiopathic intracranial hypertension: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38457956**: *Venous sinus stenting for idiopathic intracranial hypertension: An updated Meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39585430**: *Effectiveness of optic nerve sheath fenestration in preserving vision in idiopathic intracranial hypertension: an updated meta-analysis and systematic review* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40142261**: *Effectiveness of Topiramate Versus Acetazolamide in the Management of Idiopathic Intracranial Hypertension: ASystematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40937960**: *Efficacy and Safety of GLP-1 and Dual GIP/GLP-1 Receptor Agonists in Idiopathic Intracranial Hypertension: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

- Transorbital ultrasonography shows increased optic nerve sheath diameter and optic disc elevation associated with IIH, allowing non-invasive detection of raised ICP. (PMID 37599314)
- A meta-analysis of topiramate versus acetazolamide in IIH noted topiramate's benefit on weight reduction and acetazolamide's ability to lower CSF pressure and improve vision, recommending combination treatment. (PMID 40142261)
- Optic nerve sheath fenestration to preserve vision in IIH is supported by updated meta-analysis but is limited by publication bias. (PMID 39585430)
- GLP-1 and dual GIP/GLP-1 receptor agonists are under study in IIH, with randomised trials needed. (PMID 40937960)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal surgical option for vision-threatening disease.
- Optic nerve sheath fenestration vs ventriculoperitoneal/lumboperitoneal shunting vs venous sinus stenting for medically refractory visual loss in idiopathic intracranial hypertension.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- IIH: young obese woman with headache, pulsatile tinnitus and papilloedema, normal imaging.
- Visual field monitoring.
- Differentiating optic disc drusen from true papilloedema: autofluorescence, B-scan ultrasound calcification, and absence of peripapillary flame haemorrhages.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with headache, transient visual obscurations and bilateral disc swelling.
- 32-year-old female with BMI 38 presents with progressive bilateral visual field constriction and Frisén grade 4 papilloedema refractory to maximum-dose acetazolamide.

**Reviewer Gap**: Frisén grading, pseudopapilloedema (optic disc drusen) distinction and lumbar puncture opening pressure are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37599314**: *B-mode transorbital ultrasonography for the diagnosis of idiopathic intracranial hypertension: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38457956**: *Venous sinus stenting for idiopathic intracranial hypertension: An updated Meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39585430**: *Effectiveness of optic nerve sheath fenestration in preserving vision in idiopathic intracranial hypertension: an updated meta-analysis and systematic review* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40142261**: *Effectiveness of Topiramate Versus Acetazolamide in the Management of Idiopathic Intracranial Hypertension: ASystematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40937960**: *Efficacy and Safety of GLP-1 and Dual GIP/GLP-1 Receptor Agonists in Idiopathic Intracranial Hypertension: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
