## [1316] Perioperative cardiovascular risk assessment: revised cardiac risk index, pre-op optimisation

**Cluster**: periop_risk | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Perioperative risk assessment combines clinical risk indices such as the Revised Cardiac Risk Index, functional capacity, biomarkers and procedure-specific risk, as set out in the 2024 AHA/ACC guideline, to decide who needs further testing or optimisation.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Revised Cardiac Risk Index was derived and prospectively validated as a simple index that identifies higher-risk patients undergoing nonurgent major noncardiac surgery and outperformed other published indexes. (PMID 10477528)
- The 2024 AHA/ACC multisociety guideline provides evidence-based strategies for perioperative cardiovascular management in noncardiac surgery, including pharmacological therapy, monitoring and devices. (PMID 39316661, PMID 39320289)
- ESAIC focused guidelines advise on perioperative use of cardiac troponin and natriuretic peptides in noncardiac surgery patients for three scopes of application. (PMID 37265332)
- The 2025 ESPEN surgery guideline adds recommendations for frailty assessment, sarcopenia diagnosis and prehabilitation. (PMID 40957230)
- EASL guidance advises individualised multidisciplinary risk assessment for extrahepatic abdominal surgery in cirrhosis. (PMID 40348682)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of routine biomarkers and stress testing.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Perioperative cardiovascular risk assessment: revised cardiac risk index, pre-op optimisation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- RCRI items: ischaemic heart disease, heart failure, cerebrovascular disease, insulin treatment, creatinine above 2 mg/dL, high-risk surgery (not in abstracts).
- Functional capacity of 4 METs.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Perioperative cardiovascular risk assessment: revised cardiac risk index, pre-op optimisation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 68-year-old with diabetes and prior MI before an elective hernia repair.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Perioperative cardiovascular risk assessment: revised cardiac risk index, pre-op optimisation presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric RCRI risk by score, stress testing indications and specific optimisation steps are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 10477528**: *Derivation and prospective validation of a simple index for prediction of cardiac risk of major noncardiac surgery* (1999) [other] - None

## [1318] Perioperative diabetes management

**Cluster**: periop_diabetes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Perioperative diabetes management now has specific advice for newer drugs and devices: SGLT2 inhibitors should be omitted the day before and day of surgery, GLP-1 receptor agonists can be continued with aspiration risk mitigation, and pumps and sensors need planning.

### Core Teaching Points
- A multidisciplinary UK-led consensus advises continuing GLP-1 receptor agonists and dual GIP/GLP-1 agonists before surgery with full risk assessment and techniques to mitigate pulmonary aspiration, and omitting SGLT2 inhibitors the day before and the day of a procedure. (PMID 39781571)
- SPAQI guidance on SGLT2 inhibitors tailors management to diabetes and other comorbidities, fasting considerations and monitoring for euglycaemic ketoacidosis. (PMID 42067493)
- SPAQI consensus guides perioperative clinicians caring for adults wearing insulin pumps and continuous glucose monitors. (PMID 39388833)
- The SAMBA consensus statement updates perioperative blood glucose management in adults with diabetes undergoing ambulatory surgery, including newer therapies. (PMID 38517760)
- Joint British guidance on bariatric surgery stresses careful planning with diabetes teams and post-operative glucose monitoring, hypoglycaemia surveillance and medication adjustment. (PMID 41840846)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Aspiration risk with GLP-1 agonists is an evolving area.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Perioperative diabetes management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Stop SGLT2 inhibitors before surgery to avoid euglycaemic DKA.
- Insulin pump management on the day of surgery.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Perioperative diabetes management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on empagliflozin and semaglutide for elective cholecystectomy.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Perioperative diabetes management presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Glucose targets, insulin infusion protocols and metformin handling are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38517760**: *Society for Ambulatory Anesthesia Updated Consensus Statement on Perioperative Blood Glucose Management in Adult Patients With Diabetes Mellitus Undergoing Ambulatory Surgery* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39388833**: *Perioperative Management of Adult Patients with Diabetes Wearing Devices: A Society for Perioperative Assessment and Quality Improvement (SPAQI) Expert Consensus Statement* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39781571**: *Elective peri-operative management of adults taking glucagon-like peptide-1 receptor agonists, glucose-dependent insulinotropic peptide agonists and sodium-glucose cotransporter-2 inhibitors: a multidisciplinary consensus statement: A consensus statement from the Association of Anaesthetists, Association of British Clinical Diabetologists, British Obesity and Metabolic Surgery Society, Centre for Perioperative Care, Joint British Diabetes Societies for Inpatient Care, Royal College of Anaesthetists, Society for Obesity and Bariatric Anaesthesia and UK Clinical Pharmacy Association* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41840846**: *Diabetes management in people undergoing metabolic-bariatric surgery: A guideline from the Joint British Diabetes Societies for Inpatient Care (JBDS-IP) Group* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1322] Perioperative steroid management: hydrocortisone cover for adrenal insufficiency in surgery

**Cluster**: periop_steroids | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Patients on long-term supraphysiological glucocorticoids, or with adrenal insufficiency, can develop adrenal crisis under surgical stress. Cover is graded by the size of the operation: usual dose for minor procedures, intravenous hydrocortisone for major surgery.

### Core Teaching Points
- Patients taking prednisolone 5 mg/day or more (or equivalent) for more than 3 to 4 weeks within the preceding 6 to 12 months may have HPA axis suppression and need a perioperative plan. (PMID 32001734)
- For minor procedures under local anaesthesia or sedation, patients take their usual morning glucocorticoid dose without extra parenteral hydrocortisone. (PMID 32001734)
- For major surgery under general anaesthesia, give hydrocortisone 100 mg IV at induction, then 200 mg over 24 hours by infusion (or 50 mg IV every 6 hours) until oral dosing resumes. (PMID 32001734)
- Adrenal crisis presents as hypotension unresponsive to fluids and vasopressors; treat at once with hydrocortisone 100 mg IV and isotonic saline. (PMID 26963499)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring thresholds, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Perioperative steroid management: hydrocortisone cover for adrenal insufficiency in surgery.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring thresholds, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Perioperative steroid management: hydrocortisone cover for adrenal insufficiency in surgery.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Who is at risk of HPA axis suppression.
- Hydrocortisone cover for major versus minor surgery.
- Recognising and treating perioperative adrenal crisis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A complex multimorbid patient with Perioperative steroid management: hydrocortisone cover for adrenal insufficiency in surgery presenting with disease progression or treatment intolerance requiring multidisciplinary escalation.

## [1323] Peripartum cardiomyopathy: bromocriptine, timing of delivery, LV recovery

**Cluster**: ppcm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Peripartum cardiomyopathy is managed with standard heart failure therapy adapted for pregnancy and lactation, with bromocriptine as a possible adjunct that improves LV recovery but lacks proven mortality benefit. Subsequent pregnancy carries risk when LV dysfunction persists.

### Core Teaching Points
- Prolactin inhibition with bromocriptine added to standard therapy was associated with significant improvement in LVEF and greater odds of LV recovery, without significant reduction in all-cause mortality. (PMID 36261102)
- A separate meta-analysis found bromocriptine associated with higher survival and LVEF improvement but not with fewer composite adverse clinical outcomes, and called for larger randomised trials. (PMID 34725781)
- In a subsequent pregnancy, persistent LV dysfunction beforehand carries higher mortality than recovered function, and subsequent pregnancy was associated with worsening echo parameters. (PMID 38569669)
- Contrast-enhanced cardiac MRI can serve as an adjunct to stratify the risk of poor LV recovery in postpartum PPCM if performed at a suitable time. (PMID 39295149)
- Receipt of guideline-directed medical therapy in PPCM differs by race and cardiology involvement, so additional equity strategies are needed. (PMID 37870730)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Bromocriptine's role outside trials.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Peripartum cardiomyopathy: bromocriptine, timing of delivery, LV recovery.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Counselling about future pregnancy after PPCM according to LVEF recovery.
- Bromocriptine suppresses lactation.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Peripartum cardiomyopathy: bromocriptine, timing of delivery, LV recovery.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman 3 weeks postpartum with dyspnoea, orthopnoea and LVEF 30%.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Peripartum cardiomyopathy: bromocriptine, timing of delivery, LV recovery presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic definition, delivery timing, anticoagulation and drug safety in breastfeeding are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34725781**: *Effects of bromocriptine in peripartum cardiomyopathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 36261102**: *Prolactin Inhibition in Peripartum Cardiomyopathy: Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37870730**: *Association Between Race, Cardiology Care, and the Receipt of Guideline-Directed Medical Therapy in Peripartum Cardiomyopathy* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38569669**: *Outcomes of subsequent pregnancy in women with peripartum cardiomyopathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39295149**: *Meta-analysis of cardiac magnetic resonance in prognosticating left ventricular function in peripartum cardiomyopathy* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1324] peripheral arterial disease

**Cluster**: pad | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Lower extremity PAD care is guided by the 2024 ACC/AHA guideline, which adds recommendations for comprehensive care: risk-factor modification, antithrombotic therapy, exercise therapy and revascularisation chosen on risk, benefit and patient preference.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 ACC/AHA multisociety PAD guideline updates the prior recommendations with new evidence and adds recommendations for comprehensive care of patients with lower extremity PAD. (PMID 38752899, PMID 38743805)
- The SVS focused update on intermittent claudication calls for a thorough assessment of risk, benefits, degree of impairment and durability of treatment within a patient-centred approach. (PMID 40316185)
- An ACC scientific statement on PAD in adults with diabetes addresses earlier diagnosis, screening timing and methods, and standard trial endpoints. (PMID 41405527)
- Multicomponent interventions, particularly those supporting clinicians and patient access, were most effective at improving medication prescription and reducing lipids in PAD secondary prevention. (PMID 41260242)
- A national PAD guideline in England was associated with reduced revascularisation rates, with greater reduction for moderate PAD in more deprived areas. (PMID 39436641)
- A UK guideline covers CYP2C19 genotype testing for clopidogrel. (PMID 41338248)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal antithrombotic regimen is detailed in the full guideline rather than the abstracts.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of peripheral arterial disease.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Supervised exercise therapy for claudication.
- ABI below 0.9 diagnostic; non-compressible vessels in diabetes.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in peripheral arterial disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Smoker with calf claudication at 200 m and ABI 0.65.
- A complex multimorbid patient with peripheral arterial disease presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: ABI cut-offs, statin, rivaroxaban/aspirin dosing and revascularisation criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1328] Peripheral Blood Smear Morphology

**Cluster**: blood_smear | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Peripheral blood film review remains a core diagnostic skill. Schistocyte counting by ICSH criteria supports thrombotic microangiopathy diagnosis, and a morphological finding can trigger urgent therapy.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2021 ICSH recommendations update identification and quantitation of schistocytes and discuss the validity of microscope schistocyte counts and automated fragmented red cell counts in diagnosing thrombotic microangiopathies. (PMID 34431220)
- Australian and New Zealand TMA consensus notes that other TMA-associated conditions such as infection, pregnancy and malignant hypertension do not exclude TTP or aHUS as the underlying cause. (PMID 29582550)
- A diagnostic guideline for leukopenia states that agranulocytosis with fever requires admission and immediate broad-spectrum antibiotics to reduce mortality. (PMID 29145679)
- There is no evidence of benefit from therapeutic plasma exchange in snakebite-associated TMA, so it cannot be recommended. (PMID 33290400)
- A consensus defines curricular standards for peripheral blood smear review in haematology training. (PMID 36930800)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Standardisation of schistocyte counting.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Peripheral Blood Smear Morphology.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Schistocytes with thrombocytopenia suggest TMA; urgent ADAMTS13 testing.
- Blood film findings in iron deficiency, megaloblastic anaemia and haemolysis (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Peripheral Blood Smear Morphology.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with fever, confusion, thrombocytopenia, anaemia and schistocytes on film.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Peripheral Blood Smear Morphology presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: A systematic morphology atlas (red cell, white cell and platelet abnormalities) is not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29145679**: *[Leukopenia - A Diagnostic Guideline for the Clinical Routine]* (2017) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 29582550**: *Consensus opinion on diagnosis and management of thrombotic microangiopathy in Australia and New Zealand* (2018) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 33290400**: *Snakebite associated thrombotic microangiopathy: a systematic review of clinical features, outcomes, and evidence for interventions including plasmapheresis* (2020) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 34431220**: *2021 update of the 2012 ICSH Recommendations for identification, diagnostic value, and quantitation of schistocytes: Impact and revisions* (2021) [guideline] - None

## [1329] peripheral neuropathy

**Cluster**: peripheral_neuropathy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Peripheral neuropathy evaluation depends on pattern and cause. The retrieved evidence is narrow (Sjogren-associated neuropathy and corneal confocal microscopy), so this page is minimal.

### Core Teaching Points
- A clinical practice guideline addresses evaluation and management of peripheral nervous system manifestations in Sjogren disease, rating recommendations as strong when benefits clearly outweigh harms. (PMID 41327784)
- Corneal confocal microscopy nerve fibre length has diagnostic utility in peripheral neuropathies. (PMID 41201232)
- Initial laboratory evaluation of distal symmetric polyneuropathy includes fasting plasma glucose/HbA1c, serum vitamin B12 with methylmalonic acid, serum protein electrophoresis with immunofixation, and renal function. (PMID 33417672)
- Nerve conduction studies and electromyography (NCS/EMG) are indicated for rapidly progressive, asymmetric, motor-predominant, or atypical neuropathies to distinguish axonal from demyelinating pathology (e.g. CIDP). (PMID 33417672)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- General approach to a patient with neuropathy is not covered.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring thresholds, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of peripheral neuropathy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Length-dependent distal symmetric polyneuropathy: diabetes and alcohol.
- Diagnostic criteria, risk stratification, and biomarker interpretation in peripheral neuropathy.
- Diagnostic criteria, risk stratification, and biomarker interpretation in peripheral neuropathy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with progressive numb feet and diabetes.
- A complex multimorbid patient with peripheral neuropathy presenting with disease progression or treatment intolerance requiring multidisciplinary escalation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Pattern recognition, laboratory screen (B12, glucose, SPEP), nerve conduction studies and common causes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts; needs a general neuropathy guideline.

## [1330] peri-procedural anticoagulation

**Cluster**: periop_antithrombotic | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Perioperative antithrombotic management balances thrombosis against bleeding. Evidence is limited for most questions; DOACs can be interrupted and resumed without heparin bridging, and procedure-specific guidance exists for interventional pain, emergency and gastrostomy procedures.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ACCP perioperative antithrombotic guideline notes uncertainty remains about best practice for most perioperative management questions. (PMID 35964704)
- The 2025 DOAC guidance states DOACs can be interrupted and resumed for procedures without bridging with LMWH. (PMID 40448969)
- A 2024 consensus on interventional pain procedures reached 100% acceptance on 16 of 18 final items for managing antiplatelet and anticoagulant therapy, with disagreement on two statements. (PMID 39133736)
- French emergency medicine recommendations provide up-to-date management of patients on anticoagulants in emergency settings with strong expert agreement. (PMID 40581261)
- The ASGE gastrostomy guideline calls for multidisciplinary discussion of bleeding versus cardiovascular risk in patients on antithrombotics. (PMID 39520459)
- Canadian radiology practice guidelines address periprocedural management of bleeding risk, aiming to avoid unnecessary delays and consultations. (PMID 41964243)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Bridging is rarely needed but data are limited.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of peri-procedural anticoagulation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- No bridging for most DOAC interruptions.
- Continue aspirin for secondary prevention in most surgery (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in peri-procedural anticoagulation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on apixaban for AF scheduled for colectomy: interruption plan.
- A complex multimorbid patient with peri-procedural anticoagulation presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific interruption durations by drug and renal function, mechanical valve and recent stent rules are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35964704**: *Perioperative Management of Antithrombotic Therapy: An American College of Chest Physicians Clinical Practice Guideline* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39133736**: *Perioperative Management of Antiplatelet and Anticoagulant Therapy in Patients Undergoing Interventional Techniques: 2024 Updated Guidelines from the American Society of Interventional Pain Physicians (ASIPP)* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39520459**: *American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline on gastrostomy feeding tubes: summary and recommendations* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40448969**: *2025 Guidelines for direct oral anticoagulants: a practical guidance on the prescription, laboratory testing, peri-operative and bleeding management* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1331] Peritoneal dialysis-associated peritonitis: diagnosis, empirical antibiotics, catheter removal criteria

**Cluster**: pd_peritonitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PD-associated peritonitis is treated per the ISPD 2022 guideline, with prevention measures, culture-guided therapy, and early recognition of unusual pathogens such as mycobacteria and fungi, for which catheter removal may be needed.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2022 ISPD peritonitis update includes a new recommendation on adjunctive oral N-acetylcysteine to mitigate aminoglycoside ototoxicity and suggests research areas in prevention and treatment. (PMID 35264029)
- Paediatric ISPD guidance (2024) updates prevention and management of PD-associated infections with new antibiotic susceptibility and stewardship data. (PMID 39313225)
- For nontuberculous mycobacterial PD infection, acid-fast stain and culture are advisable, and early PD catheter removal may be an essential management step. (PMID 39609738)
- Aspergillus peritonitis in PD is hard to diagnose, early antifungal treatment determines prognosis, and mortality remains high despite treatment. (PMID 32893119)
- A review of rare pathogens in PD peritonitis discusses recurrent peritonitis and treatment strategies to reduce morbidity. (PMID 38831580)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Empirical regimen choice by local resistance.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Peritoneal dialysis-associated peritonitis: diagnosis, empirical antibiotics, catheter removal criteria.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cloudy effluent with white cell count above 100/mm3 and over 50% neutrophils (not in abstracts).
- Intraperitoneal antibiotics.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Peritoneal dialysis-associated peritonitis: diagnosis, empirical antibiotics, catheter removal criteria.

### Case Generation Hooks
- PD patient with abdominal pain and cloudy dialysate.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Peritoneal dialysis-associated peritonitis: diagnosis, empirical antibiotics, catheter removal criteria presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria and empirical antibiotic regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32893119**: *Aspergillus peritonitis in peritoneal dialysis patients: A systematic review* (2020) [systematic review] - None

## [1332] Peritoneal Dialysis: Principles and Adequacy

**Cluster**: pd_adequacy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PD prescription is individualised using adequacy assessment, with attention to catheter placement technique, training and complications. Evidence comparing automated and continuous ambulatory PD is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- A GRADE-based clinical guideline covers adequacy and prescription of peritoneal dialysis. (PMID 39341764)
- The SAGES PD access guideline favours advanced laparoscopic catheter placement rather than basic or open insertion, and concomitant clean surgery with catheter placement rather than staged procedures. (PMID 37989887)
- Studies comparing automated with continuous ambulatory PD are limited, and future research should address residual kidney function, volume status and patient-reported outcomes. (PMID 39258519)
- Catheter insertion technique influences catheter function and clinical outcomes in PD. (PMID 36810986)
- An ISPD position paper addresses teaching PD to patients, highlighting educational practices and nurse competencies. (PMID 40966019)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Targets for small-solute clearance and their clinical relevance.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Peritoneal Dialysis: Principles and Adequacy.

## [1333] Peritoneal Disorders

**Cluster**: peritoneal_disorders | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The retrieved evidence concerns peritoneal surface malignancy and carcinomatosis rather than benign peritoneal disorders. Cytoreductive surgery with HIPEC and multidisciplinary consensus pathways are the main themes.

### Core Teaching Points
- HIPEC may improve survival and reduce recurrence in advanced gastric cancer without significantly increasing complications, per a meta-analysis. (PMID 37158149)
- Consensus guidelines address malignant gastrointestinal obstruction in peritoneal surface malignancy, gastric cancer with synchronous peritoneal metastases, and appendiceal tumours with peritoneal involvement. (PMID 40560502, PMID 40560499, PMID 40558065)
- American Radium Society appropriate use criteria guide management of peritoneal carcinomatosis from different tumour origins. (PMID 40693544)
- A consensus addresses peritoneal sarcomatosis. (PMID 39123374)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Topic scope is unclear.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Peritoneal Disorders.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles

- Peritoneal carcinomatosis from an appendiceal mucinous tumour.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Peritoneal Disorders presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

## [1334] Pertussis: clinical stages, macrolide treatment, post-exposure prophylaxis, vaccination

**Cluster**: pertussis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pertussis control relies on vaccination, including in pregnancy to protect unvaccinated newborns, and on timely diagnosis and antibiotic treatment of cases. Maternal immunisation may modestly blunt infant vaccine responses but remains effective.

### Core Teaching Points
- Maternal pertussis immunisation may blunt routine infant vaccine effectiveness, but is predicted to remain effective in protecting unvaccinated newborns, supporting current recommendations. (PMID 38297003)
- A consensus statement reviews global pertussis vaccination in pregnancy and strategies for obstetric providers. (PMID 38251722)
- Global Pertussis Initiative recommendations for Eastern and Northern Europe call for aligned national policies, scaled-up immunisation in pregnancy and a life-long immunisation approach. (PMID 41922871)
- An expert consensus on paediatric pertussis gives new concepts in diagnosis and treatment, including intravenous options for severe cases. (PMID 39537933)
- Current pertussis vaccines provide scarce protection against Bordetella parapertussis infection in children. (PMID 38543887)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Duration of protection from acellular vaccines.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pertussis: clinical stages, macrolide treatment, post-exposure prophylaxis, vaccination.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Tdap in each pregnancy.
- Macrolide treatment and post-exposure prophylaxis (not in abstracts).
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pertussis: clinical stages, macrolide treatment, post-exposure prophylaxis, vaccination.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 6-week-old infant with paroxysmal cough, apnoea and lymphocytosis.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pertussis: clinical stages, macrolide treatment, post-exposure prophylaxis, vaccination presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Clinical stages, macrolide regimens, infectious period and contact prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1335] Phage therapy and novel antimicrobial approaches: bacteriophage, monoclonal antibodies, future therapies

**Cluster**: phage_therapy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Bacteriophage therapy is an emerging option for refractory or resistant infections. Clinical evidence is promising but limited, and a new consensus guideline sets out standardised use of personalised phage therapy.

### Core Teaching Points
- A consensus-based guideline provides clear, practice-oriented recommendations for the safe and standardised use of personalised bacteriophage therapy. (PMID 42768217)
- A meta-analysis of customised and non-customised phage therapy in refractory or resistant infections reports promising outcomes but significant gaps in clinical evidence, needing well-designed trials. (PMID 40633848)
- In hip and knee periprosthetic joint infection, evidence for phage therapy is limited and methodologically weak. (PMID 41562899)
- A systematic review of phage therapy for urinary tract infections reports uncertainty about dosing and administration and calls for randomised trials. (PMID 40435529)
- A review of phage therapy for critical antibiotic-resistant Gram-positive bacteria identifies implementation obstacles and research directions. (PMID 40424685)

- Dosing, resistance to phage and regulatory pathways.

## [1336] Pharmacokinetics of antibiotics in critical illness: dose adjustment strategies

**Cluster**: pk_antibiotics_icu | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Critical illness alters antibiotic pharmacokinetics, so personalised dosing, extended or continuous beta-lactam infusion and therapeutic drug monitoring are advocated. Aggressive PK/PD target attainment improves clinical efficacy.

### Core Teaching Points
- The French beta-lactam guideline strongly suggests personalised dosing, continuous or prolonged infusion and therapeutic drug monitoring for beta-lactams in critically ill patients. (PMID 30925922)
- A position paper states antimicrobial TDM should be the standard of care for most antimicrobials in every ICU, though barriers must be addressed. (PMID 32383061)
- A meta-analysis found beta-lactam TDM-guided dosing improved clinical and microbiological cure and treatment response in critically ill patients, with larger randomised trials still needed. (PMID 35731853)
- Attaining aggressive rather than conservative PK/PD targets gave significant clinical benefit for beta-lactams in Gram-negative infections. (PMID 38627763)
- Continuous infusion of vancomycin was associated with 53% lower odds of acute kidney injury and 2.6-fold higher odds of PK target attainment than intermittent infusion, without affecting mortality. (PMID 32317590)
- Continuous or extended-infusion beta-lactams with a loading dose may significantly improve clinical outcomes in sepsis or septic shock. (PMID 33135261)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Mortality benefit of TDM and continuous infusion.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pharmacokinetics of antibiotics in critical illness: dose adjustment strategies.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Loading dose then extended infusion for time-dependent antibiotics.
- Augmented renal clearance in critical illness.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pharmacokinetics of antibiotics in critical illness: dose adjustment strategies.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Septic ICU patient with augmented renal clearance and piperacillin-tazobactam failure.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pharmacokinetics of antibiotics in critical illness: dose adjustment strategies presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific PK/PD targets (for example 100% fT>MIC), dose adjustments for renal replacement therapy and aminoglycoside strategies are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1337] Pharmacological management of fibromyalgia: duloxetine, pregabalin, milnacipran

**Cluster**: fibromyalgia_pharm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Drug treatment of fibromyalgia has modest effects: duloxetine, pregabalin and amitriptyline are the main options, with network meta-analyses showing similar efficacy and differing tolerability, so choice is tailored to symptoms.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis comparing amitriptyline with FDA-approved treatments suggests clinicians tailor treatment to individual symptoms, weighing benefits and acceptability. (PMID 35587348)
- An overview with network meta-analysis of amitriptyline, duloxetine and pregabalin cautions that tolerability and safety are important to decision-making. (PMID 35347488)
- Duloxetine was more effective than placebo in fibromyalgia, and dose should be customised to the individual. (PMID 37461044)
- A network meta-analysis of antidepressants for chronic pain found moderate efficacy for duloxetine at standard dose, with promising but less certain evidence for milnacipran. (PMID 39367772)
- A consensus statement describes pharmacological and non-pharmacological fibromyalgia treatment, noting CBT and non-invasive brain stimulation may improve disability. (PMID 39982626)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative long-term benefit and harms.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pharmacological management of fibromyalgia: duloxetine, pregabalin, milnacipran.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Duloxetine, pregabalin and amitriptyline as options; opioids avoided (not in abstracts).

- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pharmacological management of fibromyalgia: duloxetine, pregabalin, milnacipran.

## [1338] Philadelphia-Positive ALL Management

**Cluster**: ph_all | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Philadelphia-positive ALL is now treated with potent third-generation TKIs and blinatumomab, often sparing allogeneic transplantation in MRD-negative patients. MRD is a key prognostic and decision tool.

### Core Teaching Points
- Introduction of third-generation TKIs and blinatumomab has significantly improved outcomes in newly diagnosed Ph+ ALL while sparing the need for HSCT in some patients. (PMID 36682989)
- Ponatinib was associated with significantly better survival outcomes than first- and second-generation TKIs, although limited safety data call for randomised trials. (PMID 40517974)
- Chemotherapy-free regimens of blinatumomab plus TKIs and steroids achieved promising survival rates, so the need for chemotherapy or transplant requires study. (PMID 38972767)
- Chemotherapy with next-generation TKIs gave comparable survival to allogeneic HSCT in MRD-negative (complete molecular response) patients, informing transplant indication in first remission. (PMID 36882308)
- Minimal residual disease is a potent clinical tool for prognosis in Ph+ ALL, and NGS can be used to test for BCR-ABL1 kinase domain mutations. (PMID 34437635, PMID 32154668)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether transplant is needed for MRD-negative patients.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Philadelphia-Positive ALL Management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- T315I mutation and ponatinib.
- MRD-guided decisions about transplantation.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Philadelphia-Positive ALL Management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with newly diagnosed Ph+ B-ALL: induction and MRD monitoring.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Philadelphia-Positive ALL Management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Induction regimens, TKI dosing and CNS prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

- **PMID 36682989**: *Outcomes of Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors Maintenance Therapy with or without Allogeneic Hematopoietic Stem Cell Transplantation in Philadelphia Chromosome Positive Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia in First Complete Remission: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1339] physiology guided pci fractional flow reserve

**Cluster**: ffr_pci | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Physiology-guided PCI uses FFR or non-hyperaemic ratios to target ischaemia-causing lesions. FFR-guided PCI reduced procedural events compared with angiography alone, and coronary CT-derived FFR is entering practice.

### Core Teaching Points
- An individual patient data meta-analysis found FFR-guided PCI reduced major adverse events in chronic coronary syndrome and NSTE-ACS, mainly through fewer peri-procedural MIs, with no differences in mortality or MACE beyond 30 days. (PMID 40831380)
- A European consensus statement describes applied coronary physiology for procedural PCI planning, disease pattern recognition and post-PCI optimisation. (PMID 37171503)
- Higher post-PCI FFR is associated with better prognosis, but predictive capacity differs by artery, being poor in the LAD and moderate in non-LAD vessels. (PMID 37821185, PMID 36026514)
- Lesions with discordant FFR and non-hyperaemic pressure ratios (FFR positive, NHPR negative) may benefit from revascularisation, requiring further investigation. (PMID 40669964)
- Expert consensus on coronary CT-derived FFR states it can reduce unnecessary invasive angiography and guide targeted revascularisation. (PMID 41686239)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term mortality benefit of FFR guidance.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of physiology guided pci fractional flow reserve.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- FFR below 0.80 indicates a significant stenosis.
- Post-PCI FFR as an optimisation target.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in physiology guided pci fractional flow reserve.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Intermediate 60% LAD stenosis on angiography in stable angina.
- A complex multimorbid patient with physiology guided pci fractional flow reserve presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: FFR thresholds, FAME trial data and adenosine protocol are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36026514**: *Predictive value of post-percutaneous coronary intervention fractional flow reserve: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37171503**: *Applied coronary physiology for planning and guidance of percutaneous coronary interventions. A clinical consensus statement from the European Association of Percutaneous Cardiovascular Interventions (EAPCI) of the European Society of Cardiology* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37821185**: *Impact of Post-PCI FFR Stratified by Coronary Artery* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40669964**: *Outcomes of Lesions With Discordance Between FFR and Nonhyperemic Pressure Ratios* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40831380**: *Fractional flow reserve vs angiography to guide percutaneous coronary intervention: an individual patient data meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41686239**: *Clinical use of coronary computed tomography angiography-derived fractional flow reserve: expert consensus by an International Working Group* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1341] Pituitary adenomas: micro vs macro, prolactinoma medical care vs transsphenoidal surgery

**Cluster**: pituitary_adenoma | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Prolactinomas are treated first with dopamine agonists, with surgery a reasonable alternative in small, non-invasive tumours. Paediatric and adult consensus guidelines stress specialist-centre management.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Pituitary Society international consensus covers diagnosis and management of prolactin-secreting adenomas and notes needs for additional therapeutic options and international registries. (PMID 37670148)
- Transsphenoidal surgery for prolactinomas may be particularly effective in small, non-invasive, treatment-naive tumours and may be a viable first-line alternative to dopamine agonists in such patients. (PMID 34325023)
- Italian guidelines for prolactinoma management address endocrinologists, neurosurgeons and gynaecologists and include options such as temozolomide for aggressive tumours. (PMID 37171003)
- Consensus guidelines for paediatric pituitary adenomas recommend surgery in specialist centres and collection of outcome data for new modalities. (PMID 38336897, PMID 38336898)
- Cabergoline prevented tumour progression after surgery in about half of non-functioning pituitary adenoma cases with few adverse events, though tumour shrinkage was uncommon. (PMID 35902444)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Duration of dopamine agonist therapy.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pituitary adenomas: micro vs macro, prolactinoma medical care vs transsphenoidal surgery.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cabergoline as first-line for macroprolactinoma.
- Pituitary stalk effect versus prolactinoma by prolactin level.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pituitary adenomas: micro vs macro, prolactinoma medical care vs transsphenoidal surgery.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with amenorrhoea, galactorrhoea and prolactin 3,000 mU/L.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pituitary adenomas: micro vs macro, prolactinoma medical care vs transsphenoidal surgery presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prolactin thresholds, dose schedules, withdrawal criteria and pregnancy management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1342] Pituitary apoplexy: sudden onset headache, visual loss, emergency hydrocortisone, surgery

**Cluster**: pituitary_apoplexy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pituitary apoplexy needs urgent endocrine replacement and neuro-ophthalmic assessment. Patients without severe visual or neurological deficits can be managed expectantly, whereas visual field defects and ocular palsy favour surgery.

### Core Teaching Points
- For patients presenting without severe visual or other neurological deficits, expectant management is recommended, individualised to symptom severity at onset. (PMID 39503840)
- A meta-analysis suggests surgical intervention should be advocated for apoplexy with visual field defects and ocular palsy. (PMID 27772771)
- A meta-analysis of 908 patients found surgical and conservative interventions provide comparable outcomes, though more severe cases typically undergo surgery and data are observational. (PMID 39033812)
- A meta-analysis of vision outcomes in early versus late surgery suggests conservative management may be warranted for early stabilisation before surgery. (PMID 30922898)
- Treatment of apoplexy is tailored to the individual case, and visual and endocrine recovery have been reported after both conservative and surgical treatment. (PMID 31470146)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Timing of surgery.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pituitary apoplexy: sudden onset headache, visual loss, emergency hydrocortisone, surgery.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Hydrocortisone replacement first.
- Visual field and acuity assessment.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pituitary apoplexy: sudden onset headache, visual loss, emergency hydrocortisone, surgery.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Sudden severe headache, bitemporal hemianopia and hypotension in a patient with a known macroadenoma.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pituitary apoplexy: sudden onset headache, visual loss, emergency hydrocortisone, surgery presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Presentation, MRI findings and emergency steroid dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1343] Plantar Fasciitis and Calcaneal Spurs

**Cluster**: plantar_fasciitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Plantar fasciitis is a clinical diagnosis managed conservatively with orthoses, taping, stretching and injection options. Platelet-rich plasma gives better medium-term pain control than corticosteroid but not short-term or at one year.

### Core Teaching Points
- Platelet-rich plasma injections provided superior pain control to corticosteroid at 3 to 6 months but not in the short term or at one year. (PMID 39778212)
- Customised orthoses and taping reduced pain intensity in the short term in plantar fasciitis, though evidence is of low to moderate quality. (PMID 37620126)
- Dry needling was reported to reduce pain and disability in plantar heel pain in the short and long term, but results should be treated with caution because of few trials. (PMID 33760098)
- Photobiomodulation was superior to ESWT for pain relief but not superior to other interventions, and the evidence does not support it as an adjunct to other electrotherapy. (PMID 37464155)
- A Singapore consensus consolidates statements into a treatment pathway for plantar fasciitis, and a review of medical imaging for plantar heel pain summarises imaging findings. (PMID 38920234, PMID 35065676)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Standardisation of PRP preparation.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Plantar Fasciitis and Calcaneal Spurs.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Heel pain worst with first steps in the morning.
- Calcaneal spur is often incidental.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Plantar Fasciitis and Calcaneal Spurs.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Runner with medial heel pain after rest.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Plantar Fasciitis and Calcaneal Spurs presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Examination findings, plantar fascia thickness thresholds and stretching programmes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33760098**: *Is Dry Needling Effective for the Management of Plantar Heel Pain or Plantar Fasciitis? An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35065676**: *Medical imaging for plantar heel pain: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37464155**: *Effects of photobiomodulation therapy (PBMT) on the management of pain intensity and disability in plantar fasciitis: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1344] Plaque psoriasis biologic therapy (IL-17/IL-23 inhibitors)

**Cluster**: psoriasis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Moderate-to-severe psoriasis is treated with biologics, and IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors show long drug survival. Routine latent TB testing is not required before IL-17 or IL-23 inhibitors, and dose reduction and switching strategies are emerging.

### Core Teaching Points
- A joint NPF and IPC position statement reached consensus that routine testing for latent TB infection is not required in psoriasis patients treated with IL-17 or IL-23 inhibitors. (PMID 41260526)
- IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors show drug survival that depends on effect modifiers such as biologic naivety and data source. (PMID 38630365)
- A meta-analysis on switching biologics found adalimumab, ixekizumab and risankizumab preferred for intraclass switching and guselkumab the primary agent for interclass transitions. (PMID 40545752)
- The first international consensus on dose reduction of biologics for psoriasis judged dose reduction feasible, though underlying evidence is still limited. (PMID 42012299)
- European-adapted S3 and French guidelines (2025-2026) give treatment recommendations, monitoring and advice for comorbid situations, pregnancy planning and special types of psoriasis. (PMID 41531416, PMID 41641952, PMID 41469898)
- A network meta-analysis of biologics in paediatric plaque psoriasis found trends towards improved CDLQI and PASI75 responses and called for better-powered trials. (PMID 41090545)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal dose-reduction criteria.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Plaque psoriasis biologic therapy (IL-17/IL-23 inhibitors).

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PASI and DLQI for severity.
- Choosing biologic in psoriatic arthritis versus plaque disease.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Plaque psoriasis biologic therapy (IL-17/IL-23 inhibitors).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with PASI 15 failing methotrexate.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Plaque psoriasis biologic therapy (IL-17/IL-23 inhibitors) presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic features, topical therapy, severity thresholds and conventional systemic agents are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 40545752**: *Switching biologic agent in patients with psoriasis: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1345] Plasma Cell Leukaemia

**Cluster**: plasma_cell_leukaemia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Plasma cell leukaemia is defined by 5% or more circulating plasma cells on peripheral blood smear in symptomatic myeloma and requires intensive, personalised treatment. Evidence comes from consensus rather than trials.

### Core Teaching Points
- The IMWG consensus defines plasma cell leukaemia by 5% or more circulating plasma cells on blood smear in patients otherwise diagnosed with symptomatic multiple myeloma. (PMID 34857730)
- European Myeloma Network recommendations advise that transplant-ineligible patients need a personalised intensive therapeutic approach, with allogeneic transplantation in selected patients. (PMID 30038381)
- A 2025 European Myeloma Network review and consensus statement on primary PCL strongly supports clinical trials and biological studies using novel technologies. (PMID 39924085)
- Chinese expert consensus standardises flow cytometric detection of circulating plasma cells and diagnosis and treatment of PCL. (PMID 38951057, PMID 41087176)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal induction and consolidation.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Plasma Cell Leukaemia.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Aggressive course with high-risk cytogenetics.
- Definition by circulating plasma cell percentage.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Plasma Cell Leukaemia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with myeloma, hypercalcaemia and 12% plasma cells on the blood film.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Plasma Cell Leukaemia presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Treatment regimens (proteasome inhibitor-based, daratumumab, transplant) and survival data are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1346] Platelet Refractoriness

**Cluster**: platelet_refractoriness | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Platelet transfusion refractoriness has immune causes (anti-HLA antibodies) and non-immune causes such as fever; HLA matching and avoiding sensitisation are central. Pathogen-reduced platelets may increase transfusion needs and refractoriness.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis identified fever (OR 2.26) among important risk factors for platelet transfusion refractoriness. (PMID 36856992)
- Anti-HLA antibody formation increases the chance of platelet refractoriness, and the review suggests approaches for HLA matching and for preventing sensitisation. (PMID 40245603)
- Pathogen-reduced platelets may require more transfusions in shorter intervals and may increase refractoriness and alloimmunisation. (PMID 35745493)
- Neonatal restrictive platelet transfusion guidelines address refractoriness and its association with poor outcomes. (PMID 39009717)
- In Glanzmann thrombasthenia anti-alpha-IIb-beta-3 immunisation is a cause of refractoriness, with risk factors discussed. (PMID 29611179)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best product for sensitised patients.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Platelet Refractoriness.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- 1-hour and 24-hour corrected count increments (not in abstracts).
- HLA-matched platelets for alloimmune refractoriness.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Platelet Refractoriness.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Leukaemia patient with poor platelet increments after two transfusions.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Platelet Refractoriness presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Definitions of refractoriness and an investigation algorithm are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29611179**: *Anti-α<sub>IIb</sub> β<sub>3</sub> immunization in Glanzmann thrombasthenia: review of literature and treatment recommendations* (2018) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 35745493**: *Efficacy and Safety of Pathogen-Reduced Platelets Compared with Standard Apheresis Platelets: A Systematic Review of RCTs* (2022) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 36856992**: *A meta-analysis of risk factors associated with platelet transfusion refractoriness* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1347] Platelet Storage Pool Diseases

**Cluster**: storage_pool | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Platelet storage pool disease (delta-SPD) causes mild to moderate mucocutaneous bleeding from deficient dense-granule contents (ADP/ATP, serotonin). Diagnosis uses a stepwise approach for bleeding of unknown cause, and many patients remain undiagnosed.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review of platelet delta-storage pool disease covers dense-granule contents (ADP/ATP, serotonin) and tools available for diagnosis of different forms. (PMID 32759727)
- Many patients with inherited platelet diseases and normal platelet count remain without a diagnosis, requiring new diagnostic strategies. (PMID 33147934)
- A stepwise diagnostic procedure for mild to moderate bleeding disorders and bleeding disorder of unknown cause is summarised in a 2024 review. (PMID 38454298)
- Management of inherited platelet disorders aims to minimise bleeding risk and achieve haemostasis when needed, with platelet transfusion a crucial component in many. (PMID 33082057)
- Bernard-Soulier syndrome should be considered with persistent macrothrombocytopenia, family history, ITP unresponsiveness and absent ristocetin-induced aggregation. (PMID 39191409)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Genetic basis of most storage pool disorders.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Platelet Storage Pool Diseases.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Absent second wave aggregation or reduced dense granules on electron microscopy (not in abstracts).
- Desmopressin and antifibrinolytics.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Platelet Storage Pool Diseases.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young woman with easy bruising, heavy periods and normal platelet count and PT/aPTT.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Platelet Storage Pool Diseases presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Light transmission aggregometry patterns and treatment of bleeds (desmopressin, tranexamic acid) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32759727**: *Platelet δ-Storage Pool Disease: An Update* (2020) [review] - None
- **PMID 33082057**: *Inherited Platelet Disorders: Diagnosis and Management* (2020) [review] - None
- **PMID 33147934**: *Inherited platelet diseases with normal platelet count: phenotypes, genotypes and diagnostic strategy* (2021) [review] - None

## [1348] Platelet transfusion thresholds in haematological malignancies

**Cluster**: platelet_thresholds | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2025 AABB/ICTMG guideline supports restrictive platelet transfusion strategies, which reduce adverse reactions and conserve platelets, and ASCO provides threshold guidance for cancer patients.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 AABB and ICTMG guidelines found a consistent pattern of evidence supporting restrictive platelet transfusion strategies, which reduce adverse reactions. (PMID 40440268)
- The ASCO update on platelet transfusion in cancer patients also addresses platelet product production, prevention of Rh alloimmunisation and management of refractoriness. (PMID 29182495)
- The ESICM guideline gives evidence-based recommendations on transfusion strategies in non-bleeding critically ill adults. (PMID 31912207)
- A quality improvement implementation of a neonatal restrictive platelet transfusion guideline reduced non-indicated transfusions without a change in major bleeding. (PMID 33758388)
- The BSH aplastic anaemia guideline recommends multidisciplinary discussion and expert-centre involvement for management. (PMID 38247114)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Thresholds vary by clinical setting.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Platelet transfusion thresholds in haematological malignancies.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Prophylactic platelet threshold of 10 x10^9/L in haematological malignancy (not in abstracts).
- Evidence-based therapeutic selection, primary dosing protocols, and contraindications in Platelet transfusion thresholds in haematological malignancies.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Platelet transfusion thresholds in haematological malignancies.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on induction chemotherapy with platelets 8 and no bleeding.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Platelet transfusion thresholds in haematological malignancies presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: The numerical thresholds for prophylaxis and procedures are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1349] Pleural effusion and parapneumonic effusion management

**Cluster**: pleural_effusion | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pleural effusion work-up begins with ultrasound-guided aspiration and ACR-based imaging; benign effusions have an ERS diagnostic statement, and critical-care drainage has expert consensus guidance.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ERS statement on benign pleural effusions gives a diagnostic approach to transudative effusions, heart failure, hepatic hydrothorax, end-stage renal failure, asbestos-related and post-surgical effusions and nonspecific pleuritis. (PMID 39060018)
- The ACR Appropriateness Criteria address workup of pleural effusion or pleural disease. (PMID 38823955)
- Strong expert consensus was reached on recommendations for optimising drainage of pleural effusion in adult critical care patients. (PMID 40280363)
- SHOX2 and RASSF1A gene methylation assays in malignant pleural effusion detected malignancy with better performance than cytology in a meta-analysis. (PMID 40614167)
- Parapneumonic effusion and empyema are covered in the dedicated empyema page, including fibrinolytic therapy. (PMID 31684683)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of molecular assays in routine practice.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pleural effusion and parapneumonic effusion management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Light's criteria to distinguish exudate from transudate (not in abstracts).
- Pleural fluid analysis steps.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pleural effusion and parapneumonic effusion management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Breathless patient with a unilateral effusion and weight loss.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pleural effusion and parapneumonic effusion management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Light's criteria, pleural fluid tests and thoracoscopy indications are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31684683**: *Intra-pleural fibrinolytic therapy versus placebo, or a different fibrinolytic agent, in the treatment of adult parapneumonic effusions and empyema* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1351] Pleurodesis techniques

**Cluster**: pleurodesis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Malignant pleural effusion can be controlled with indwelling pleural catheters or pleurodesis; catheters shorten stay and reduce repeat procedures but raise cellulitis risk. Patient preference and cost guide choice.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found indwelling pleural catheters have shorter length of stay and fewer repeat pleural procedures than pleurodesis but a higher risk of cellulitis. (PMID 30272486)
- The ATS/STS/STR guideline gives recommendations for management of malignant pleural effusions, including managing catheter-associated infections with antibiotics without removing the catheter. (PMID 30272503)
- Complications of indwelling pleural catheters occur, with infection the most common. (PMID 36253892)
- In spontaneous pneumothorax, ERS/EACTS/ESTS guidelines make 24 recommendations and highlight evidence gaps for persistent air leak and recurrence prevention. (PMID 38804185, PMID 38806203)
- A Chinese consensus recommends systemic anticancer treatment for patients with good performance status and metastatic malignancy, with specific advice for loculated effusion. (PMID 38044047)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal pleurodesis agent.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pleurodesis techniques.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Talc pleurodesis versus IPC for trapped lung.
- IPC for ambulatory management.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pleurodesis techniques.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with recurrent malignant effusion wishing to avoid admissions.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pleurodesis techniques presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Pleurodesis agents, talc dosing and technique (slurry versus poudrage) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1352] Pneumocystis jirovecii Pneumonia

**Cluster**: pcp | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pneumocystis pneumonia diagnosis combines clinical context with beta-D-glucan and PCR testing, where PCR cannot always separate infection from colonisation. TMP-SMX remains the first-line prophylaxis agent in HIV.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of the Fungitell serum beta-D-glucan assay suggests a categorical approach using 400 pg/mL to rule in PCP according to pretest probability, needing external validation. (PMID 39536824)
- In a diagnostic meta-analysis, PCR (qPCR) difficulty in distinguishing infection from colonisation means a standardised-protocol randomised trial is needed to assess its clinical effect. (PMID 39900713)
- In HIV, trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole prophylaxis remains first-line given its mortality benefit; optimal dosing to maximise efficacy and limit toxicity needs study. (PMID 38583518)
- British Society for Medical Mycology recommendations update diagnosis of serious fungal diseases for clinicians and laboratories. (PMID 41232547)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal PCR thresholds.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Pneumocystis jirovecii Pneumonia.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Raised LDH and bilateral ground-glass opacities in an HIV patient with CD4 below 200.
- Steroids for PCP with hypoxaemia (not in abstracts).
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pneumocystis jirovecii Pneumonia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- HIV patient with exertional dyspnoea, dry cough and desaturation on exercise.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pneumocystis jirovecii Pneumonia presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Treatment regimen, adjunctive corticosteroids and non-HIV risk groups are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38583518**: *Comparative efficacy and safety of Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia prophylaxis regimens for people living with HIV: a systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39536824**: *Diagnostic test accuracy of the Fungitell serum (1→3)-β-D-glucan assay for the diagnosis of Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1353] pneumonia

**Cluster**: pneumonia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2025 ATS community-acquired pneumonia guideline covers diagnostics, antibiotic duration and corticosteroids. In hospitalised non-viral CAP corticosteroids probably reduce short-term mortality and ventilation, and guideline-concordant antibiotics lower 30-day mortality.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ATS guideline for adult CAP provides recommendations on selected diagnostic and treatment strategies including antibiotic duration and systemic corticosteroids. (PMID 40679934)
- A meta-analysis found corticosteroids probably reduce short-term mortality and the need for invasive mechanical ventilation in hospitalised patients with non-viral CAP (moderate certainty). (PMID 40323455)
- Guideline-concordant antibiotic treatment was associated with reduced 30-day mortality in hospitalised CAP. (PMID 40868039)
- The French SPILF update reassesses the role of chest X-ray, pleuropulmonary ultrasound and CT in diagnosing and following CAP. (PMID 40037948)
- The JRS guideline emphasises advance care planning and interdisciplinary collaboration in adult pneumonia, and a European guideline addresses stroke-associated pneumonia with local protocols targeting aspiration pathogens. (PMID 40614556, PMID 42095755)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which patients benefit most from steroids.
- Procalcitonin-guided discontinuation versus fixed short-course (5-day) antibiotic regimens in hospitalized non-ICU community-acquired pneumonia.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Severity scores CURB-65 and PSI (not in abstracts).
- Duration of antibiotics.
- Diagnostic microbiological workup in severe community-acquired pneumonia: blood cultures, sputum Gram stain/culture, and urinary Legionella and pneumococcal antigen testing.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Hospitalised patient with severe CAP and respiratory failure.
- 62-year-old smoker presents with high fever, hyponatraemia, diarrhoea, and dense right lower lobe consolidation with clinical failure on amoxicillin.

**Reviewer Gap**: Severity assessment, empirical regimens and microbiological testing details are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40037948**: *Update of guidelines for management of Community Acquired pneumonia in adults by the French Infectious Disease Society (SPILF) and the French-Speaking Society of Respiratory Diseases (SPLF): Endorsed by the French intensive care society (SRLF), the French microbiology society (SFM), the French radiology society (SFR) and the French emergency society (SFMU)* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40323455**: *Corticosteroids for adult patients hospitalised with non-viral community-acquired pneumonia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40614556**: *The JRS guideline for the management of pneumonia in adults 2024* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40679934**: *Diagnosis and Management of Community-acquired Pneumonia: An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40868039**: *Guideline-Concordant Antibiotic Treatment for Hospitalised Patients with Community-Acquired Pneumonia and Clinical Outcomes at a Tertiary Hospital in Australia* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1354] Point-of-care ultrasound in the ICU: FAST, lung, cardiac, vascular

**Cluster**: pocus_icu | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Point-of-care ultrasound in the ICU supports diagnosis and resuscitation across shock, sepsis and pulmonary embolism, but evidence for outcome benefit and operator competency thresholds is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of POCUS-guided resuscitation in shock could not explore how operator competency, scan frequency and timing affect outcomes because evidence was insufficient. (PMID 39298556)
- Multi-organ POCUS has high diagnostic accuracy for pulmonary embolism in critically ill patients, though validation in more populations is needed. (PMID 40269937)
- An Italian consensus gives a practical guide to bedside ultrasound in ICU sepsis and septic shock, focusing on key views and findings. (PMID 42615024)
- A consensus paper summarises conditions, indications and performance of focused transoesophageal echocardiography in acute and emergency medicine. (PMID 40643606)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Effect of POCUS on mortality.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Point-of-care ultrasound in the ICU: FAST, lung, cardiac, vascular.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- RUSH protocol for undifferentiated shock (not in abstracts).
- B-lines and A-lines on lung ultrasound.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Point-of-care ultrasound in the ICU: FAST, lung, cardiac, vascular.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Hypotensive ICU patient: focused cardiac and IVC assessment.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Point-of-care ultrasound in the ICU: FAST, lung, cardiac, vascular presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: FAST, lung ultrasound signs and vascular access applications named in the topic are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39298556**: *The Impact of Point-of-Care Ultrasound-Guided Resuscitation on Clinical Outcomes in Patients With Shock: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40269937**: *Diagnostic accuracy of multi-organ point-of-care ultrasound for pulmonary embolism in critically ill patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40643606**: *[Consensus paper on focused transesophageal echocardiography (fTEE) in clinical acute and emergency medicine : From the Commission for Clinical Cardiovascular Medicine of the DGK in cooperation with the DGINA, DGIIN and DGIM]* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42615024**: *Usefulness of Bedside Point-of-care Ultrasounds in Intensive Care Unit for Septic Patients: A Consensus Statement of the Italian Society of Echocardiography and Cardiovascular Imaging* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1355] Poisoning and overdose in the ICU: toxidromes, antidotes, extracorporeal removal

**Cluster**: poisoning_icu | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Life-threatening poisoning is managed with supportive care and specific antidotes; the 2023 AHA focused update summarises evidence for antidotes and supportive drugs in specific poisonings.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2023 AHA focused update on cardiac arrest or life-threatening toxicity reviews intravenous lipid emulsion, lidocaine, methylene blue, naloxone, pralidoxime, sodium bicarbonate, sodium nitrite, sodium thiosulfate, vasodilators and vasopressors for specific critical poisonings. (PMID 37721023)
- The 2025 AHA special circumstances guideline gives graded recommendations for adults and children where conditions require modification of BLS and ALS. (PMID 41122889)
- For life-threatening DOAC bleeding, a guideline could not recommend recombinant factor VIIa as a non-specific haemostatic agent because of scant data. (PMID 38567679)
- Consensus definitions and out-of-hospital triage guidance standardise assessment of paracetamol poisoning. (PMID 40162912, PMID 40047505)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence for many antidotes comes from case series.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Poisoning and overdose in the ICU: toxidromes, antidotes, extracorporeal removal.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Naloxone for opioids; sodium bicarbonate for sodium-channel blocker toxicity (not in abstracts).
- Hydroxocobalamin for cyanide.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Poisoning and overdose in the ICU: toxidromes, antidotes, extracorporeal removal.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Unresponsive patient with miosis and respiratory depression.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Poisoning and overdose in the ICU: toxidromes, antidotes, extracorporeal removal presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Toxidrome recognition tables, extracorporeal removal indications and individual antidote doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37721023**: *2023 American Heart Association Focused Update on the Management of Patients With Cardiac Arrest or Life-Threatening Toxicity Due to Poisoning: An Update to the American Heart Association Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care* (2023) [guideline] - None
