## [1356] Polyarteritis Nodosa

**Cluster**: pan | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Polyarteritis nodosa is managed under the 2021 ACR/Vasculitis Foundation guideline, with distinct approaches for cutaneous PAN, childhood PAN and DADA2, where anti-TNF therapy is indicated.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2021 ACR/Vasculitis Foundation guideline gives recommendations on diagnostic strategies, pharmacologic agents and imaging for polyarteritis nodosa. (PMID 34235883)
- In childhood-onset PAN and DADA2, anti-TNF treatment is indicated for vasculitic DADA2, and cyclophosphamide could be switched to mycophenolate once remission is confirmed in severe PAN. (PMID 33901990)
- Evidence for managing cutaneous polyarteritis nodosa is limited, and further studies are needed on remission, relapse and mortality. (PMID 33944902)
- DADA2 has a wide phenotypic spectrum, and early diagnosis and treatment are mandatory because of morbidity and mortality. (PMID 37179309)
- Inflammatory muscle involvement in systemic vasculitis responds well overall to conventional immunosuppressants and glucocorticoids. (PMID 34971804)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best remission-maintenance drug.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Polyarteritis Nodosa.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Hepatitis B association and spared lungs (not in abstracts).
- Mesenteric angiography microaneurysms.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Polyarteritis Nodosa.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with fever, mononeuritis multiplex, livedo and hypertension.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polyarteritis Nodosa presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Classification criteria, biopsy and angiography features, and induction doses are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33901990**: *Systematic review of childhood-onset polyarteritis nodosa and DADA2* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 33944902**: *Therapeutic options for cutaneous polyarteritis nodosa: a systematic review* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 34235883**: *2021 American College of Rheumatology/Vasculitis Foundation Guideline for the Management of Polyarteritis Nodosa* (2021) [guideline] - None

## [1357] Polycystic Kidney Disease: ADPKD

**Cluster**: pkd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2025 KDIGO guideline is the central reference for ADPKD evaluation and treatment, with adaptations for paediatrics, liver and pancreatic cysts, and intracranial aneurysm screening in at-risk patients. ARPKD evidence was not retrieved.

### Core Teaching Points
- The KDIGO 2025 guideline addresses evaluation, management and treatment of ADPKD for providers and patients. (PMID 39848746)
- A KDIGO executive summary sets out recommendations for paediatric ADPKD and the transition to adult care. (PMID 41569965)
- Risk factors for unruptured intracranial aneurysm in asymptomatic ADPKD identify patients who may benefit from early screening. (PMID 39951701)
- An ERA commentary covers KDIGO recommendations on hepatopancreatic cysts, and KDOQI and CARI commentaries adapt the guideline to US and Australasian practice. (PMID 40810467, PMID 41864657, PMID 42011799)
- A narrative review integrates guideline recommendations with patient perspectives and real-world effectiveness to ease implementation. (PMID 41926217)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence base for many recommendations is limited.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Polycystic Kidney Disease: ADPKD.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Tolvaptan for rapidly progressing ADPKD (not in abstracts).
- Screening for intracranial aneurysm in a family history of rupture.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Polycystic Kidney Disease: ADPKD.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 45-year-old with bilateral large kidneys, hypertension and family history.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polycystic Kidney Disease: ADPKD presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Tolvaptan indications, Mayo imaging classification and ARPKD (paediatric) management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39848746**: *KDIGO 2025 clinical practice guideline for the evaluation, management, and treatment of autosomal dominant polycystic kidney disease (ADPKD): executive summary* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39951701**: *Risk factors for unruptured intracranial aneurysms in asymptomatic patients with autosomal dominant polycystic kidney disease: who needs screening? A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1359] Polycystic ovary syndrome metabolic management

**Cluster**: pcos | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PCOS management is metabolic and reproductive: lifestyle and metformin, with GLP-1 receptor agonists such as liraglutide adding benefit in overweight patients, and Italian guidance for infertility.

### Core Teaching Points
- Combined liraglutide and metformin in overweight or obese women with PCOS offered more comprehensive metabolic and hormonal benefits (including LH, FSH, testosterone and cholesterol) than metformin alone. (PMID 40855964)
- Liraglutide improved metabolic and reproductive outcomes in PCOS, though larger and longer trials are warranted to validate its reproductive efficacy and safety. (PMID 41508932)
- Continuing metformin after a positive pregnancy test seems to have greater clinical benefit than discontinuing it, but high-quality research is needed. (PMID 40473092)
- Italian guidelines adapt recommendations for diagnosis and management of infertility in PCOS, and the Society for Endocrinology guideline covers evaluation of androgen excess. (PMID 40055752, PMID 40364581)
- A global systematic review of PCOS prevalence informs international guideline diagnostic criteria and emphasises early, accurate diagnosis. (PMID 41528735)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term reproductive effects of GLP-1 treatment.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Polycystic ovary syndrome metabolic management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Rotterdam criteria: oligo-anovulation, hyperandrogenism, polycystic ovaries (not in abstracts).
- Letrozole for ovulation induction.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Polycystic ovary syndrome metabolic management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with oligomenorrhoea, hirsutism and BMI 32.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polycystic ovary syndrome metabolic management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, endometrial protection and contraception options are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40055752**: *Diagnosis and management of infertility in patients with polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS): guidelines from the Italian Society of Human Reproduction (SIRU) and the Italian Centers for the Study and Conservation of Eggs and Sperm (CECOS Italy)* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40364581**: *Society for Endocrinology Clinical Practice Guideline for the Evaluation of Androgen Excess in Women* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40473092**: *Preconception and first-trimester metformin on pregnancy outcomes in women with polycystic ovary syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40855964**: *Combined liraglutide and metformin therapy in overweight or obese women with polycystic ovary syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41508932**: *Efficacy of liraglutide on metabolic and reproductive outcomes in women with polycystic ovary syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41528735**: *Prevalence of polycystic ovary syndrome: a global and regional systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1361] Polycythaemia Vera

**Cluster**: polycythaemia_vera | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Polycythaemia vera management aims to prevent thrombosis through haematocrit control, aspirin and cytoreduction, with interferon increasingly considered including in patients under 60. Retrieved evidence is consensus-level and does not show the haematocrit target or ruxolitinib data.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of cytoreductive therapy in PV patients under 60 suggests early cytoreduction, particularly with disease-modifying recombinant interferon alpha, may benefit them, and randomised trials are needed. (PMID 38507746)
- An Italian Delphi consensus did not reach consensus on whether bone marrow biopsy is necessary for diagnosis, and 93.4% agreed collaboration between transfusionists and haematologists is important. (PMID 40600747)
- A Japanese Delphi consensus guides selection of patients for ropeginterferon alfa-2b, and a US Delphi consensus defines disease progression and disease modification in PV. (PMID 41779123, PMID 41091181)
- JAK2V617F allele burden has been related to haematological parameters, symptoms and complications, though variable methods prevented high-quality meta-analysis. (PMID 38652240)
- NCCN 2026 myeloproliferative neoplasm insights focus on recent updates for myelofibrosis. (PMID 42425157)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Place of ruxolitinib after hydroxyurea resistance.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Polycythaemia Vera.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Haematocrit target below 45% (not in abstracts).
- Thrombosis prevention with aspirin.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Polycythaemia Vera.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 50-year-old with haematocrit 0.58, itch after hot baths and JAK2 V617F.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polycythaemia Vera presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Haematocrit target, aspirin evidence, venesection, hydroxyurea and ruxolitinib (RESPONSE) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38507746**: *Cytoreductive therapy in younger adults with polycythemia vera: a meta-analysis of safety and outcomes* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38652240**: *Association of JAK2V617F allele burden and clinical correlates in polycythemia vera: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40600747**: *Recommendations for best management of polycythemia vera in Italy: a Delphi consensus statement* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41091181**: *Results from a US modified Delphi consensus to define disease progression and disease modification in polycythemia Vera* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41779123**: *Consensus on the use of ropeginterferon alfa-2b in Japanese patients with polycythemia vera: a modified Delphi survey* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42425157**: *NCCN Guidelines® Insights: Myeloproliferative Neoplasms, Version 2.2026* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1363] Polycythemia vera: ruxolitinib for hydroxyurea-resistant or intolerant disease

**Cluster**: polycythaemia_vera | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Polycythaemia vera management aims to prevent thrombosis through haematocrit control, aspirin and cytoreduction, with interferon increasingly considered including in patients under 60. Retrieved evidence is consensus-level and does not show the haematocrit target or ruxolitinib data.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of cytoreductive therapy in PV patients under 60 suggests early cytoreduction, particularly with disease-modifying recombinant interferon alpha, may benefit them, and randomised trials are needed. (PMID 38507746)
- An Italian Delphi consensus did not reach consensus on whether bone marrow biopsy is necessary for diagnosis, and 93.4% agreed collaboration between transfusionists and haematologists is important. (PMID 40600747)
- A Japanese Delphi consensus guides selection of patients for ropeginterferon alfa-2b, and a US Delphi consensus defines disease progression and disease modification in PV. (PMID 41779123, PMID 41091181)
- JAK2V617F allele burden has been related to haematological parameters, symptoms and complications, though variable methods prevented high-quality meta-analysis. (PMID 38652240)
- NCCN 2026 myeloproliferative neoplasm insights focus on recent updates for myelofibrosis. (PMID 42425157)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Place of ruxolitinib after hydroxyurea resistance.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Polycythemia vera: ruxolitinib for hydroxyurea-resistant or intolerant disease.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Haematocrit target below 45% (not in abstracts).
- Thrombosis prevention with aspirin.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Polycythemia vera: ruxolitinib for hydroxyurea-resistant or intolerant disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 50-year-old with haematocrit 0.58, itch after hot baths and JAK2 V617F.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polycythemia vera: ruxolitinib for hydroxyurea-resistant or intolerant disease presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Haematocrit target, aspirin evidence, venesection, hydroxyurea and ruxolitinib (RESPONSE) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38507746**: *Cytoreductive therapy in younger adults with polycythemia vera: a meta-analysis of safety and outcomes* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38652240**: *Association of JAK2V617F allele burden and clinical correlates in polycythemia vera: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40600747**: *Recommendations for best management of polycythemia vera in Italy: a Delphi consensus statement* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41091181**: *Results from a US modified Delphi consensus to define disease progression and disease modification in polycythemia Vera* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41779123**: *Consensus on the use of ropeginterferon alfa-2b in Japanese patients with polycythemia vera: a modified Delphi survey* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42425157**: *NCCN Guidelines® Insights: Myeloproliferative Neoplasms, Version 2.2026* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1364] polymyalgia rheumatica

**Cluster**: pmr | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Polymyalgia rheumatica is treated with glucocorticoids tapered to minimise exposure in a frail population, with exercise added and early referral recommended for suspected cases. Treat-to-target recommendations now exist for PMR and GCA.

### Core Teaching Points
- Treat-to-target recommendations for GCA and PMR define treatment targets and strategies to assess, achieve and maintain them, and set a research agenda. (PMID 36828585)
- French recommendations aim to reduce corticosteroid exposure and side effects in a fragile population in day-to-day PMR care. (PMID 38583691)
- German, Austrian and Swiss recommendations advise that frail patients be offered an individualised exercise programme in addition to drug therapy. (PMID 41346249, PMID 40560440)
- International recommendations support early referral of suspected PMR, complementing EULAR/ACR guidance for established disease. (PMID 38050004)
- An executive summary calls for multidisciplinary integrated protocols for PMR and GCA and increased clinical suspicion. (PMID 38142973)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal steroid taper and role of steroid-sparing drugs.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of polymyalgia rheumatica.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Rapid response to low-dose prednisolone and raised ESR/CRP.
- Look for giant cell arteritis symptoms.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in polymyalgia rheumatica.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 70-year-old with bilateral shoulder and hip girdle stiffness for 4 weeks and ESR 80.
- A complex multimorbid patient with polymyalgia rheumatica presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Classification criteria, starting dose and taper schedule, and relapse prevention drugs are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36828585**: *Treat-to-target recommendations in giant cell arteritis and polymyalgia rheumatica* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38050004**: *Recommendations for early referral of individuals with suspected polymyalgia rheumatica: an initiative from the international giant cell arteritis and polymyalgia rheumatica study group* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38142973**: *Executive summary on the optimization of the multidisciplinary and integrated approach to polymyalgia rheumatica and giant cell arteritis in Madrid region* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38583691**: *Recommendations of the French Society of Rheumatology for the management in current practice of patients with polymyalgia rheumatica* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40560440**: *[S2e guidelines on the treatment of polymyalgia rheumatica: update 2024 : Evidence-based guidelines of the German Society for Rheumatology and Clinical Immunology (DGRh), the Austrian Society for Rheumatology and Rehabilitation (ÖGR) and the Swiss Society for Rheumatology (SGR) and the participating medical scientific specialist societies and other organizations]* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41346249**: *Recommendations of the German, Austrian and Swiss rheumatology and other medical societies for the management of polymyalgia rheumatica* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1367] Polymyositis and Dermatomyositis: muscle biopsy findings, extramuscular features

**Cluster**: myositis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Idiopathic inflammatory myopathies need antibody-based classification, cancer screening per IMACS risk groups and tailored immunosuppression; IVIg and rituximab have supportive but limited evidence. Statin myopathy must be distinguished from immune-mediated necrotising myopathy.

### Core Teaching Points
- The IMACS cancer screening guideline performed satisfactorily in a myositis cohort, with most patients in high- or moderate-risk groups for whom enhanced screening is recommended, and a refinement score improved risk stratification (AUC 0.762 vs 0.699). (PMID 41978693, PMID 42585765)
- IVIg probably improves disability and muscle strength in refractory idiopathic inflammatory myopathy compared with placebo (SMD 0.86 and 0.78). (PMID 40787733)
- Rituximab in adult inflammatory myositis shows benefit but infusion reactions and infections need careful monitoring, and controlled trials are needed. (PMID 39527803)
- Evidence for targeted immunosuppressive therapies in inflammatory myopathy is very uncertain. (PMID 40747756)
- In anti-MDA5 dermatomyositis with interstitial lung disease, age, smoking, fever, inflammatory markers and rapidly progressive ILD predict mortality. (PMID 40746563)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best second-line therapy.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Polymyositis and Dermatomyositis: muscle biopsy findings, extramuscular features.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Anti-HMGCR myopathy persists after statin cessation (not in abstracts).
- Cancer screening in dermatomyositis.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Polymyositis and Dermatomyositis: muscle biopsy findings, extramuscular features.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with proximal weakness, raised CK and statin use for 2 years.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polymyositis and Dermatomyositis: muscle biopsy findings, extramuscular features presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Distinguishing statin myopathy (CK, EMG, biopsy, anti-HMGCR) is not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39527803**: *Rituximab Treatment in Adult Patients With Idiopathic Inflammatory Myositis: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40746563**: *Risk factors for mortality in anti-MDA5 antibody-positive dermatomyositis with interstitial lung disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40747756**: *Targeted immunosuppressive and immunomodulatory therapies for idiopathic inflammatory myopathies* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40787733**: *Non-targeted immunosuppressive and immunomodulatory therapies for idiopathic inflammatory myopathies* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41978693**: *Performance and potential impact of the IMACS cancer screening guideline in myositis: analysis in a myositis referral centre* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42585765**: *Cancer-associated myositis in Chinese patients with idiopathic inflammatory myopathies: Malignancy spectrum, risk factors, and validation of the IMACS guideline* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1370] Polypharmacy and deprescribing: STOPP/START criteria

**Cluster**: polypharmacy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Deprescribing reviews regular medicines to minimise harm in older people; deprescribing preventive medicines in frail or palliative patients was not associated with worse outcomes, though certainty is very low. Pharmacist-led and guideline-supported approaches are available.

### Core Teaching Points
- A 2026 deprescribing guideline summary supports health professionals in reviewing regular medicines, minimising harm and planning ongoing treatment or monitoring. (PMID 41943558)
- Deprescribing preventive medications in older adults with advanced frailty, dementia or limited life expectancy was not associated with worse outcomes, but evidence certainty was very low. (PMID 41840386)
- In community-dwelling older adults the individual-level association of deprescribing was very small, but at population level the outcomes may be large given the prevalence of polypharmacy and potentially inappropriate medication. (PMID 40338546)
- A guideline addresses deprescribing statins in adults aged 65 and older, to be discussed with patients and carers. (PMID 42728062)
- Consolidated, implementation-ready deprescribing guidance for diuretics is needed, and guidelines for multimorbidity and polypharmacy need better methodology and patient input. (PMID 41825469, PMID 41679655)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Effect of deprescribing interventions on hard outcomes.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Polypharmacy and deprescribing: STOPP/START criteria.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- STOPP/START criteria to identify inappropriate and omitted medicines (not in abstracts).
- Anticholinergic burden.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Polypharmacy and deprescribing: STOPP/START criteria.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 85-year-old on 12 medicines with falls and cognitive decline.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Polypharmacy and deprescribing: STOPP/START criteria presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: The STOPP/START criteria content and medication review process steps are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40338546**: *Deprescribing in Community-Dwelling Older Adults: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41679655**: *Quality and recommendations of guidelines for multimorbidity and polypharmacy in older adults: A systematic review* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41825469**: *Guidelines and tools to assess appropriateness of diuretic prescribing and aid deprescribing: a systematic review* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41840386**: *Deprescribing preventive medications in older adults with advanced frailty, dementia, or limited life expectancy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41943558**: *Deprescribing in Older People: A Clinical Practice Guideline Summary* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42728062**: *Deprescribing statins for adults 65 years of age and older: Evidence-based clinical practice guideline* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1371] portal hypertension

**Cluster**: portal_hypertension | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Portal hypertension care covers primary and secondary prophylaxis of varices, treatment of acute variceal bleeding with endoscopic therapy, and non-cirrhotic causes. Updated APASL guidance covers both cirrhosis and non-cirrhotic portal hypertension.

### Core Teaching Points
- The updated APASL guidelines on acute variceal bleeding give a contemporary template for management in both cirrhosis and non-cirrhotic portal hypertension. (PMID 40886248)
- APASL recommendations address diagnosis and management of non-cirrhotic portal fibrosis and idiopathic portal hypertension, combining evidence with expert opinion. (PMID 39546143)
- For gastric variceal bleeding, injection of cyanoacrylate glue is the commonest endoscopic treatment, and EUS-guided angiotherapy with glue with or without coils has expanded options. (PMID 39480369)
- International Liver Transplantation Society guidance updates management of portopulmonary hypertension. (PMID 40094355)
- The ACG perioperative guideline advises evidence-based risk assessment for patients with cirrhosis undergoing surgery. (PMID 40899690)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal therapy for gastric varices.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of portal hypertension.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Non-selective beta-blocker plus band ligation for secondary prophylaxis (not in abstracts).
- Terlipressin and antibiotics in acute variceal bleeding.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in portal hypertension.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Cirrhotic patient with haematemesis and haemodynamic instability.
- A complex multimorbid patient with portal hypertension presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Primary prophylaxis, vasoactive drugs, TIPS indications and Baveno criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39480369**: *Endoscopic devices and techniques for the management of gastric varices (with videos)* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39546143**: *Non-cirrhotic portal fibrosis/idiopathic portal hypertension: APASL recommendations for diagnosis and management* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40094355**: *International Liver Transplantation Society practice guideline update on portopulmonary hypertension* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40886248**: *Management of acute variceal bleeding: updated APASL guidelines* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40899690**: *ACG Clinical Guideline: Perioperative Risk Assessment and Management in Patients With Cirrhosis* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1373] Portal Hypertension Non-Cirrhotic

**Cluster**: portal_hypertension | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Portal hypertension care covers primary and secondary prophylaxis of varices, treatment of acute variceal bleeding with endoscopic therapy, and non-cirrhotic causes. Updated APASL guidance covers both cirrhosis and non-cirrhotic portal hypertension.

### Core Teaching Points
- The updated APASL guidelines on acute variceal bleeding give a contemporary template for management in both cirrhosis and non-cirrhotic portal hypertension. (PMID 40886248)
- APASL recommendations address diagnosis and management of non-cirrhotic portal fibrosis and idiopathic portal hypertension, combining evidence with expert opinion. (PMID 39546143)
- For gastric variceal bleeding, injection of cyanoacrylate glue is the commonest endoscopic treatment, and EUS-guided angiotherapy with glue with or without coils has expanded options. (PMID 39480369)
- International Liver Transplantation Society guidance updates management of portopulmonary hypertension. (PMID 40094355)
- The ACG perioperative guideline advises evidence-based risk assessment for patients with cirrhosis undergoing surgery. (PMID 40899690)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal therapy for gastric varices.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Portal Hypertension Non-Cirrhotic.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Non-selective beta-blocker plus band ligation for secondary prophylaxis (not in abstracts).
- Terlipressin and antibiotics in acute variceal bleeding.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Portal Hypertension Non-Cirrhotic.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Cirrhotic patient with haematemesis and haemodynamic instability.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Portal Hypertension Non-Cirrhotic presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Primary prophylaxis, vasoactive drugs, TIPS indications and Baveno criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39480369**: *Endoscopic devices and techniques for the management of gastric varices (with videos)* (2025) [guideline] - None

- **PMID 40886248**: *Management of acute variceal bleeding: updated APASL guidelines* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40899690**: *ACG Clinical Guideline: Perioperative Risk Assessment and Management in Patients With Cirrhosis* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1374] Post-cardiac arrest syndrome: targeted temperature management, haemodynamic optimisation

**Cluster**: post_arrest_care | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Post-cardiac arrest care now centres on temperature control to normothermia, avoiding hyperoxia and hypotension, structured prognostication and survivorship. The 2025 AHA and ERC-ESICM guidelines are current.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 AHA post-cardiac arrest care guideline covers care after ROSC, survivorship for patients, caregivers and rescuers, and the potential role of organ donation. (PMID 41122894)
- ERC and ESICM 2025 post-resuscitation guidelines address temperature control, seizure control, general intensive care management, prognostication, long-term outcome, rehabilitation and organ donation. (PMID 41117575)
- ERC-ESICM 2022 advice is to achieve normothermia in patients with mild hypothermia after ROSC and not to use prehospital cooling with large volumes of cold intravenous fluid. (PMID 35131119)
- Canadian best-practice guidance aims to harmonise out-of-hospital arrest care nationally to improve survival and functional outcomes. (PMID 38604702)
- A consensus guideline defines determination of brain death/death by neurologic criteria, including examination, apnoea and ancillary testing. (PMID 37821233)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal target temperature and duration.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Post-cardiac arrest syndrome: targeted temperature management, haemodynamic optimisation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Prognostication should be delayed and multimodal.
- Targets for oxygen and blood pressure after ROSC (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Post-cardiac arrest syndrome: targeted temperature management, haemodynamic optimisation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Comatose patient 24 hours after out-of-hospital VF arrest.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-cardiac arrest syndrome: targeted temperature management, haemodynamic optimisation presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific temperature targets and durations, neuroprognostication algorithm timings and haemodynamic targets are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35131119**: *ERC-ESICM guidelines on temperature control after cardiac arrest in adults* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37821233**: *Pediatric and Adult Brain Death/Death by Neurologic Criteria Consensus Guideline* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38604702**: *Canadian Cardiovascular Society/Canadian Cardiovascular Critical Care Society/Canadian Association of Interventional Cardiology Clinical Practice Update on Optimal Post Cardiac Arrest and Refractory Cardiac Arrest Patient Care* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41117575**: *European Resuscitation Council and European Society of Intensive Care Medicine Guidelines 2025 Post-Resuscitation Care* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41122894**: *Part 11: Post-Cardiac Arrest Care: 2025 American Heart Association Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1376] Post-ERCP pancreatitis prevention

**Cluster**: post_ercp_pancreatitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Rectal indomethacin or diclofenac should be given to patients undergoing ERCP unless renal failure; pancreatic stenting and hydration may add benefit, and early needle-knife or transpancreatic sphincterotomy lower PEP in difficult cannulation.

### Core Teaching Points
- Indomethacin or diclofenac given before or soon after ERCP is inexpensive and safe and is recommended in every patient without renal failure. (PMID 28167118)
- A network meta-analysis of NSAIDs, intravenous fluids, pancreatic stents and their combinations found these effective for preventing PEP, though evidence for some combinations is thin. (PMID 34214449)
- Early needle-knife and transpancreatic sphincterotomy were superior to other techniques in reducing PEP in difficult biliary cannulation. (PMID 34543649)
- ESGE advises that pancreatic sphincterotomy be reserved for patients with coexisting bile duct obstruction or biliary sphincter of Oddi dysfunction. (PMID 27299638)
- Japanese and ESGE guidelines cover risk factors, diagnosis and prevention of PEP and other ERCP-related adverse events. (PMID 40132896, PMID 31863440)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal hydration regimen.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Post-ERCP pancreatitis prevention.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Rectal NSAID prophylaxis in all ERCP.
- Pancreatic stent in high-risk patients.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Post-ERCP pancreatitis prevention.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young woman with suspected SOD undergoing ERCP with pancreatic duct cannulation.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-ERCP pancreatitis prevention presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Indomethacin dose, fluid type and rate, and stent selection are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27299638**: *Papillary cannulation and sphincterotomy techniques at ERCP: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Clinical Guideline* (2016) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 28167118**: *Indomethacin and diclofenac in the prevention of post-ERCP pancreatitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis of prospective controlled trials* (2017) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 31863440**: *ERCP-related adverse events: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline* (2020) [guideline] - None

## [1377] Post-exposure prophylaxis PEP: occupational and non-occupational, 72-hour window, regimen

**Cluster**: pep_hiv | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
HIV post-exposure prophylaxis is guided by 2025 CDC (non-occupational) and USPHS (occupational) recommendations, with transition to PrEP for those at ongoing risk. Doxycycline PEP is a separate bacterial-STI strategy.

### Core Teaching Points
- CDC 2025 nPEP guidance states clinicians should offer PrEP to people with ongoing indications and create an nPEP-to-PrEP transition plan. (PMID 40331832)
- The 2025 USPHS guidelines on occupational HIV exposure stress prompt reporting, adherence to recommended HIV PEP regimens when indicated, expert consultation and follow-up of exposed personnel. (PMID 41569270)
- The 2025 Canadian guideline updates HIV pre- and post-exposure prophylaxis to expand access to biomedical prevention. (PMID 41326046)
- Occupational HBV exposure post-exposure prophylaxis uptake among health workers in highly endemic regions is suboptimal, with barriers at individual and facility levels. (PMID 38559369)
- CDC and BASHH guidance on doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis advises STI testing at baseline and every 3-6 months with HIV screening. (PMID 38833414, PMID 40505019)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal antiretroviral regimen details are not in the abstracts.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Post-exposure prophylaxis PEP: occupational and non-occupational, 72-hour window, regimen.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Start PEP as soon as possible within 72 hours (not in abstracts).
- Follow-up testing after exposure.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Post-exposure prophylaxis PEP: occupational and non-occupational, 72-hour window, regimen.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Needlestick from a patient with unknown HIV status.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-exposure prophylaxis PEP: occupational and non-occupational, 72-hour window, regimen presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Risk assessment, preferred regimens and the 28-day course, and testing schedule are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1378] Post-Infectious Glomerulonephritis

**Cluster**: pign | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Post-infectious (post-streptococcal) glomerulonephritis is usually self-limited in children, with severe hypertension and its complications the main acute dangers. Early antibiotic treatment of infection may help prevent it.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review suggests early treatment of bacterial infections supports preventing postinfectious glomerulonephritis, relevant also as antimicrobial resistance grows. (PMID 35314652)
- Posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome can complicate acute postinfectious glomerulonephritis, where severe hypertension is central and prompt evaluation and management are crucial. (PMID 34546419)
- Acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis can rarely be associated with rheumatic fever, thrombocytopenia, haemolytic anaemia, Henoch-Schonlein purpura, reactive arthritis or uveitis. (PMID 31392657)
- A systematic review of paediatric post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis reports clinical manifestations and proportions of severe outcomes, with a heterogeneous RPGN/crescentic composite. (PMID 42856850)
- A clinical review describes the presentation, pathology and therapeutic approach of the spectrum of acute glomerulonephritis. (PMID 35461559)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Frequency of crescentic disease.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Post-Infectious Glomerulonephritis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Low C3 that normalises by 8 weeks (not in abstracts).
- Hypertension management is the priority.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Post-Infectious Glomerulonephritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Child with cola-coloured urine and periorbital oedema two weeks after a sore throat.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-Infectious Glomerulonephritis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Complement pattern, ASO titre, fluid management and indications for biopsy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31392657**: *Extrarenal Immune-Mediated Disorders Linked with Acute Poststreptococcal Glomerulonephritis: a Systematic Review* (2019) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 34546419**: *Posterior reversible leukoencephalopathy syndrome associated with acute postinfectious glomerulonephritis: systematic review* (2022) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 35314652**: *Examining the Efficacy of Antimicrobial Therapy in Preventing the Development of Postinfectious Glomerulonephritis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35461559**: *Acute glomerulonephritis* (2022) [review] - None
- **PMID 42856850**: *Clinical manifestations and proportions of severe outcomes in pediatric acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis: a systematic review and meta-analysis of predominantly hospital-based cohorts* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1379] Post-intensive care syndrome and long-term ICU outcomes

**Cluster**: pics | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Post-intensive care syndrome affects physical, cognitive and psychological health, and also families. Multimodal, multidisciplinary rehabilitation is recommended, and risk rises with longer ICU stay.

### Core Teaching Points
- A German multimodal rehabilitation guideline states PICS requires an individual, multidisciplinary approach that assesses and treats motor, cognitive and psychological impairments. (PMID 37525219)
- A meta-analysis found an association between ICU length of stay and development of PICS, underscoring the need for targeted interventions for prolonged stays. (PMID 40338918)
- PICS and family PICS strongly affect quality of life, rehabilitation, financial stress and potentially preventable readmission. (PMID 38622971)
- No universally accepted standard exists for assessing PICS; consensus-based recommendations are limited and need practical implementation across settings and languages. (PMID 40429591)
- Position statements address optimised nutrition therapy after critical illness, and energy and protein adequacy in general wards among ICU survivors is limited by barriers such as inappropriate tube removal. (PMID 41163099, PMID 39503062)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best PICS prevention bundle.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Post-intensive care syndrome and long-term ICU outcomes.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Domains of PICS: physical, cognitive, mental health.
- ICU diaries and follow-up clinics (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Post-intensive care syndrome and long-term ICU outcomes.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Survivor of prolonged ventilation with weakness, memory problems and nightmares.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-intensive care syndrome and long-term ICU outcomes presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Risk factors, screening tools and the evidence for ICU follow-up clinics are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37525219**: *Guideline on multimodal rehabilitation for patients with post-intensive care syndrome* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38622971**: *Quality of life in ICU survivors and their relatives with post-intensive care syndrome: A systematic review* (2024) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 39503062**: *Energy and protein nutrition adequacy in general wards among intensive care unit survivors: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40338918**: *Prevalence of Post-intensive care syndrome among intensive care unit-survivors and its association with intensive care unit length of stay: Systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40429591**: *Consensus-Based Recommendations for Assessing Post-Intensive Care Syndrome: A Systematic Review* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41163099**: *Towards optimised nutrition therapy after critical illness: a position statement and research framework by the global research initiative on post-intensive care nutrition (GRIP) consortium* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1381] Post-MI beta-blocker therapy contemporary evidence

**Cluster**: post_mi_bb | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In patients with LVEF of at least 50% after myocardial infarction and no other beta-blocker indication, an individual-patient-data meta-analysis found no reduction in death, myocardial infarction or heart failure, supporting stopping routine long-term beta-blockade in this group.

### Core Teaching Points
- An individual-patient-data meta-analysis of five randomised trials found beta-blockers did not reduce death from any cause (HR 1.04), myocardial infarction (HR 0.89) or heart failure (HR 0.87) in patients with LVEF of at least 50% after MI without other indications. (PMID 41211954)
- Earlier meta-analyses showed beta-blocker use for a year or more does not reduce mortality in MI patients without heart failure in the reperfusion era. (PMID 35058412)
- A Cochrane review found beta-blockers in suspected or diagnosed acute MI probably reduce the risk of reinfarction during follow-up (RR 0.82). (PMID 31845756)
- For long-term oral beta-blockers after PCI for STEMI, the only high-quality RCT data suggested a neutral effect, so interpretation should be cautious. (PMID 33136764)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Patients with mildly reduced LVEF (40-49%) are not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Post-MI beta-blocker therapy contemporary evidence.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Beta-blockers are indicated after MI with reduced LVEF or other indications.
- Evidence changed after modern reperfusion era.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Post-MI beta-blocker therapy contemporary evidence.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient 1 year after STEMI with LVEF 58%, on bisoprolol, asking whether to continue.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-MI beta-blocker therapy contemporary evidence presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Individual trial names (REDUCE-AMI, ABYSS) and current guideline classes are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31845756**: *Beta-blockers for suspected or diagnosed acute myocardial infarction* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 33136764**: *Efficacy of Long-Term Oral Beta-Blocker Therapy in Patients Who Underwent Percutaneous Coronary Intervention for ST-Segment Elevation Myocardial Infarction With Preserved Left Ventricular Ejection Fraction: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35058412**: *Long-Term Beta-Blocker Therapy After Myocardial Infarction Without Heart Failure in the Reperfusion Era-Systemic Review and Meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1382] Postoperative ileus: prevention and management

**Cluster**: postop_ileus | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Postoperative ileus prolongs stay and increases cost. ERAS protocols, early feeding, gum chewing, perioperative dexmedetomidine and electroacupuncture have supportive but variable evidence.

### Core Teaching Points
- Postoperative ileus carries a substantial global cost, and reducing its incidence and impact is a priority. (PMID 37352872)
- ERAS protocols in gastrectomy and colorectal resection reduce lung infections, shorten hospital stay and speed recovery without serious complications. (PMID 37130316)
- Perioperative intravenous dexmedetomidine may enhance postoperative gastrointestinal function recovery and reduce length of stay. (PMID 38085848)
- Electroacupuncture improved gastrointestinal functional recovery and reduced pain after colorectal surgery. (PMID 37916930)
- Gum chewing reduced ileus symptoms and hospitalisation time, and motility agents may be considered within ERAS, though standardised trials are needed. (PMID 40689540, PMID 40439889)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best drug for prevention.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Postoperative ileus: prevention and management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Opioid-sparing analgesia and early mobilisation reduce ileus.
- Rule out mechanical obstruction.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Postoperative ileus: prevention and management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day 3 after colectomy with distension and no flatus.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Postoperative ileus: prevention and management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, alvimopan, nasogastric decompression and nutrition rules are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37130316**: *The Application of Enhanced Recovery After Surgery for Gastrectomy and Colorectal Resection: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37352872**: *Global cost of postoperative ileus following abdominal surgery: meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1383] Postoperative nausea and vomiting: prevention and treatment

**Cluster**: ponv | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PONV prevention uses risk-stratified multimodal antiemetics. Olanzapine and esketamine are emerging adjuncts, and CPIC guidance links CYP2D6 genotype to 5-HT3 antagonist efficacy.

### Core Teaching Points
- Olanzapine prevented postoperative nausea and vomiting with a pivotal role relative to traditional antiemetics, though dosing and efficacy across surgical populations need further study. (PMID 39952307)
- Esketamine reduced nausea and vomiting after anaesthesia in a meta-analysis, though its risk-benefit profile needs clarification. (PMID 41454547)
- CPIC provides therapeutic recommendations for 5-HT3 receptor antagonists based on CYP2D6 genotype, where variation is linked to reduced drug efficacy. (PMID 41979467)
- China-specific 2025 PONV guidelines provide standardised protocols supporting ERAS. (PMID 41929930)
- In paediatric tonsillectomy, some analgesic regimens lowered PONV but tended to increase postoperative bleeding. (PMID 41420409)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal combinations by risk score.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Postoperative nausea and vomiting: prevention and treatment.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Apfel risk factors (not in abstracts).
- Dexamethasone and ondansetron combination.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Postoperative nausea and vomiting: prevention and treatment.

### Case Generation Hooks

## [1384] post-operative respiratory failure

**Cluster**: postop_resp_failure | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Postoperative pulmonary complications are reduced by preoperative physiotherapy and individualised ventilation; sugammadex may reduce them versus neostigmine. Routine postoperative CPAP, NIV or high-flow oxygen to prevent pneumonia is not supported.

### Core Teaching Points
- Preoperative physiotherapy was associated with 47% lower odds of postoperative pulmonary complications after elective abdominal surgery (adjusted OR 0.53), with effects on mortality uncertain. (PMID 38472053)
- Routine postoperative CPAP, NIV or high-flow nasal oxygen is not supported to prevent pneumonia after elective surgery in adults. (PMID 34916050)
- Sugammadex reversal was associated with fewer pulmonary complications (pneumonia, atelectasis, NIV, reintubation) than neostigmine, though this may not change length of stay, recovery quality or mortality. (PMID 37081384, PMID 38036323)
- Individualised PEEP during one-lung ventilation was associated with fewer postoperative pulmonary complications and better oxygenation. (PMID 34260559)
- High-flow nasal oxygen therapy in postoperative adults was associated with shorter hospital stay in randomised trials. (PMID 30587060)
- A broad meta-analysis of perioperative interventions for pulmonary complications calls for new low-risk-of-bias trials. (PMID 32161042)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which prevention bundle is best.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of post-operative respiratory failure.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Risk factors for postoperative pulmonary complications.
- Incentive spirometry has limited evidence (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in post-operative respiratory failure.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Smoker with COPD for upper abdominal surgery: optimisation steps.
- A complex multimorbid patient with post-operative respiratory failure presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Definitions of postoperative respiratory failure and treatment (NIV versus reintubation) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 30587060**: *The Effect of High-Flow Nasal Oxygen Therapy on Postoperative Pulmonary Complications and Hospital Length of Stay in Postoperative Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 32161042**: *Perioperative interventions for prevention of postoperative pulmonary complications: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34260559**: *Individualized positive end-expiratory pressure (PEEP) during one-lung ventilation for prevention of postoperative pulmonary complications in patients undergoing thoracic surgery: A meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34916050**: *Routine postoperative noninvasive respiratory support and pneumonia after elective surgery: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised trials* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37081384**: *Postoperative pulmonary complications after sugammadex reversal of neuromuscular blockade: a systematic review and meta-analysis with trial sequential analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38036323**: *The effect of sugammadex on patient morbidity and quality of recovery after general anaesthesia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1385] postpartum haemorrhage tranexamic acid maternal mortality

**Cluster**: pph_txa | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Tranexamic acid reduces the risk of life-threatening postpartum bleeding without increasing thrombosis. It is not recommended for all births, but should be considered before a PPH diagnosis in women at high risk of death.

### Core Teaching Points
- An individual-patient-data meta-analysis found tranexamic acid reduces life-threatening postpartum bleeding, with no increase in thromboembolic events (0.2% in both groups). (PMID 39461793)
- The authors do not recommend tranexamic acid in all women giving birth, but advise considering it before diagnosis of PPH in women at high risk of death. (PMID 39461793)
- Prophylactic tranexamic acid at caesarean delivery lowered postpartum blood loss and haemoglobin drop in updated meta-analyses, with further research needed in high-risk populations. (PMID 34582795, PMID 37311484)
- SOGC Guideline 431 provides recommendations for postpartum haemorrhage and haemorrhagic shock for the whole care team. (PMID 36567097)
- Strategies are suggested for early detection and first response to PPH at caesarean birth, with implementation the next step. (PMID 38719306)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Timing and dose for prophylaxis.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of postpartum haemorrhage tranexamic acid maternal mortality.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- TXA within 3 hours of birth for PPH (WOMAN trial; not in abstracts).
- Uterotonics first.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in postpartum haemorrhage tranexamic acid maternal mortality.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Atonic uterus with 1.5 L blood loss after vaginal delivery.
- A complex multimorbid patient with postpartum haemorrhage tranexamic acid maternal mortality presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: The WOMAN trial itself, dosing and uterotonic ladder are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34582795**: *Tranexamic acid for the prevention of postpartum hemorrhage in women undergoing cesarean delivery: an updated meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 36567097**: *Guideline No. 431: Postpartum Hemorrhage and Hemorrhagic Shock* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37311484**: *Tranexamic acid for the prevention of blood loss after cesarean section: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38719306**: *Strategies for optimising early detection and obstetric first response management of postpartum haemorrhage at caesarean birth: a modified Delphi-based international expert consensus* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39461793**: *Tranexamic acid for postpartum bleeding: a systematic review and individual patient data meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1386] post-renal acute kidney injury

**Cluster**: postrenal_aki | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Post-renal AKI is relieved by urgent decompression; percutaneous nephrostomy and retrograde stenting are both used, with nephrostomy preferable for post-procedure quality of life but a slightly longer stay.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found percutaneous nephrostomy preferable to retrograde ureteral stenting for acute upper obstructive uropathy because of less haematuria and dysuria, though hospital stay is slightly longer. (PMID 33758312)
- In emphysematous pyelonephritis, risk factors predict patients who need aggressive treatment, while minimally invasive modalities carry lower mortality. (PMID 35103928)
- Consensus on fetal lower urinary tract obstruction states vesico-amniotic shunting in selected cases increases perinatal survival, and a Delphi study defines a core outcome set. (PMID 35136187, PMID 38748971)
- EAU/ESPU guidelines summarise paediatric urology including congenital lower urinary tract obstruction. (PMID 38627150, PMID 39909817)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal drainage in infected obstruction.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of post-renal acute kidney injury.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Obstructed infected system needs urgent decompression.
- Renal ultrasound as first investigation.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in post-renal acute kidney injury.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Older man with anuria, bladder outlet obstruction and creatinine rising.
- A complex multimorbid patient with post-renal acute kidney injury presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Causes, imaging findings, post-obstructive diuresis and timing of relief are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33758312**: *Percutaneous nephrostomy versus retrograde ureteral stenting for acute upper obstructive uropathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35103928**: *A systematic review and meta-analysis of risk factors and treatment choices in emphysematous pyelonephritis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35136187**: *Definition, diagnosis and management of fetal lower urinary tract obstruction: consensus of the ERKNet CAKUT-Obstructive Uropathy Work Group* (2022) [guideline] - None

## [1387] Post-Transplant Complications

**Cluster**: post_transplant | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Post-transplant care centres on infection prevention and metabolic complications. Post-transplant diabetes worsens kidney allograft outcomes, and vaccination and CMV monitoring are core parts of follow-up.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found post-transplant diabetes mellitus carries prognostic impact in kidney allograft recipients, underscoring comprehensive management strategies. (PMID 39134508)
- A systematic review supports SGLT2 inhibitors in diabetic solid organ transplant recipients, though larger studies are needed for long-term effects across transplant types. (PMID 41561830)
- Meta-analyses of tirzepatide and of belatacept versus calcineurin inhibitors suggest possible roles for obesity, post-transplant diabetes and reduced immunosuppressive toxicity, pending larger prospective studies. (PMID 41908834, PMID 41766891)
- CMV infection after kidney transplant is common enough that early virological and immunological monitoring, especially in high-risk groups, is advised to reduce morbidity and preserve graft function. (PMID 41483466)
- An updated review integrates evolving vaccination guidelines into the care of adult solid organ transplant recipients. (PMID 41597712)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term safety of newer diabetes drugs in transplant recipients.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Post-Transplant Complications.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- CMV monitoring in high-risk recipients.
- Post-transplant diabetes as a modifiable risk.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Post-Transplant Complications.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Kidney transplant recipient with new hyperglycaemia on tacrolimus and steroids.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Post-Transplant Complications presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Rejection, malignancy surveillance and immunosuppression regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39134508**: *Prognostic impact of post-transplant diabetes mellitus in kidney allograft recipients: a meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41483466**: *Incidence of Cytomegalovirus (CMV) Infection in After Kidney Transplant Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41561830**: *Sodium-Glucose Cotransporter 2 Inhibitors in Diabetic Solid Organ Transplant Recipients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Comparative Studies* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41597712**: *The Role of Vaccination in Adult Solid Organ Transplantation: Updated Reviews with Recent Guidelines* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41766891**: *Systematic review and meta-analysis of belatacept versus calcineurin inhibitors on risk of post-transplant diabetes mellitus in kidney transplant recipients* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41908834**: *Safety and efficacy of tirzepatide in transplant recipients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1388] postural hypotension

**Cluster**: pots | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
POTS evidence is thin: oral drug treatment is poorly supported, and mast cell activation disorders are reported with POTS but data are inconsistent. Orthostatic hypotension, a separate entity, has pyridostigmine data in a meta-analysis.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review of oral medications for POTS found that treatments directly targeting the underlying autonomic dysfunction are lacking. (PMID 39882369)
- A systematic review of mast cell activation disorders and hereditary alpha tryptasemia in POTS found that further research with strict validated diagnostic criteria is needed to clarify any true association. (PMID 40185471)
- An AGA update on hypermobile Ehlers-Danlos syndrome advises considering testing for mast cell disorders in patients with hEDS/HSDs and DGBI who have episodic symptoms suggesting generalised mast cell disease. (PMID 40387691)
- Pyridostigmine in orthostatic hypotension raised standing BP in a meta-analysis, with possible supine hypertension, and response may be greater when baroreflex function and sympathetic reserve are preserved. (PMID 40132893)
- The pooled prevalence of orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson's disease is 33.1%, more often with older age, longer duration and more severe motor symptoms. (PMID 40715652)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best drug for POTS.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson's disease.
- Pyridostigmine for orthostatic hypotension.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young woman with palpitations on standing and hypermobility.

**Reviewer Gap**: POTS diagnostic criteria (heart-rate rise on standing/tilt) and non-drug measures are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39882369**: *Oral medications for the treatment of postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome; a systematic review of studies before and during the COVID-19 pandemic* (2024) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 40132893**: *Pyridostigmine in the management of orthostatic hypotension: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40185471**: *Prevalence of mast cell activation disorders and hereditary alpha tryptasemia among patients with postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome and Ehlers-Danlos syndrome: A systematic review* (2025) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 40387691**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on GI Manifestations and Autonomic or Immune Dysfunction in Hypermobile Ehlers-Danlos Syndrome: Expert Review* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40715652**: *Prevalence and impact of orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson's disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1391] Pouchitis

**Cluster**: pouchitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pouchitis and inflammatory pouch disorders are covered by AGA and international consensus guidelines. Probiotics show benefit for relapsing pouchitis in a meta-analysis.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AGA clinical practice guideline provides a patient-centred approach to managing pouchitis and other inflammatory pouch disorders. (PMID 38128971)
- International consensus guidelines address treatment of pouchitis, Crohn's disease of the pouch, cuffitis and other inflammatory pouch disorders, and manage extraintestinal manifestations similarly to patients without pouches. (PMID 34774224)
- An overview of meta-analyses found probiotics associated with remission and relapse prevention in UC and with benefit for relapsing pouchitis, but no significant effect in Crohn's disease. (PMID 39106167)
- A RAND/UCLA expert process standardised items for measuring pouchitis disease activity in clinical trials. (PMID 33735522)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal antibiotic and biologic sequencing.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Pouchitis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pouchitis after ileal pouch-anal anastomosis for UC.
- Probiotics for relapsing pouchitis.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pouchitis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with restorative proctocolectomy and new frequent loose stools.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pouchitis presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific antibiotic regimens, endoscopic diagnosis and biologic recommendations are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

## [1392] Precision Oncology and Molecular Profiling

**Cluster**: precision_oncology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Precision oncology depends on quality-assured molecular testing and structured molecular tumour boards. ESMO and national societies issue recommendations to harmonise tissue and liquid biopsy testing.

### Core Teaching Points
- ESMO Precision Oncology Working Group recommendations set the structure and quality indicators for molecular tumour boards to harmonise standards and speed integration of precision oncology into practice. (PMID 40194904)
- An Austrian, German and Swiss guideline establishes expert consensus on evidence-based standards and implementation of precision oncology. (PMID 40058270)
- Italian best-practice documents on molecular testing in solid tumours cover standards for tissue and liquid biopsies to harmonise diagnostics. (PMID 40963486)
- ESMO guidance on breast cancer germline multigene panel testing states that decades of surveillance and risk-reducing surgery for carriers should be justified by strong evidence of improved cancer mortality or quality of life. (PMID 40523834)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which biomarkers justify routine broad panel testing.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Precision Oncology and Molecular Profiling.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Role of the molecular tumour board.
- Tissue versus liquid biopsy.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Precision Oncology and Molecular Profiling.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with advanced solid tumour and a comprehensive genomic profile result.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Precision Oncology and Molecular Profiling presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: ESCAT actionability tiers and specific tumour-agnostic approvals are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 40194904**: *ESMO Precision Oncology Working Group recommendations on the structure and quality indicators for molecular tumour boards in clinical practice* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1393] Precocious puberty: central vs peripheral causes, GnRH agonist treatment

**Cluster**: precocious_puberty | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
An Endocrine Society guideline now covers central precocious puberty, advising against routine genetic testing but considering MKRN3 sequencing in familial CPP. GnRH analogue therapy may raise BMI short-term with normalisation by adult height.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Endocrine Society guideline on central precocious puberty advises against routine genetic testing, while suggesting genetic testing such as MKRN3 sequencing be considered for familial CPP through shared decision-making. (PMID 42287186)
- A systematic review supports the Endocrine Society guideline, including use of bone age for GnRH analogue discontinuation. (PMID 42287191)
- Meta-analyses found GnRH analogue treatment may increase BMI in the short term, with BMI likely returning to normal by adult height. (PMID 38185120, PMID 38407229)
- A meta-analysis of pharmacotherapy for CPP or early puberty advises considering the increasing risk of polycystic ovary syndrome before treatment. (PMID 40760548)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal stopping age for GnRH analogue.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Precocious puberty: central vs peripheral causes, GnRH agonist treatment.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Central versus peripheral precocious puberty.
- GnRH analogue as therapy for CPP.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Precocious puberty: central vs peripheral causes, GnRH agonist treatment.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Girl aged 7 with breast development and advanced bone age.

## [1394] Prediabetes: impaired fasting glucose, impaired glucose tolerance, prevention interventions

**Cluster**: prediabetes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Prediabetes progression risk is shaped by identifiable factors. Lifestyle intervention is central, and exercise appears superior to metformin in prediabetes, whereas metformin appears superior in established type 2 diabetes.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis identified risk factors for progression from prediabetes to type 2 diabetes, and called for longer follow-up and clarification of diagnostic criteria. (PMID 40165126)
- A network meta-analysis found exercise appears superior to metformin for glucose metabolism in prediabetes, while metformin appears superior to exercise in type 2 diabetes. (PMID 39242178)
- A clinical guideline provides detailed lifestyle strategies for treatment and remission of type 2 diabetes and prediabetes in adults. (PMID 40546763)
- A Brazilian position statement prefers the 1-hour OGTT over the 2-hour OGTT as more practical and superior, and gives a structured reevaluation interval. (PMID 40038723)
- The 2026 AACE algorithm emphasises a complications- and comorbidities-centric approach beyond glucose levels for choosing pharmacological treatment in type 2 diabetes. (PMID 41842862)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best diagnostic test for risk stratification.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Prediabetes: impaired fasting glucose, impaired glucose tolerance, prevention interventions.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Lifestyle versus metformin in prediabetes.
- Diagnostic thresholds for impaired fasting glucose.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Prediabetes: impaired fasting glucose, impaired glucose tolerance, prevention interventions.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with HbA1c in the prediabetes range and obesity.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Prediabetes: impaired fasting glucose, impaired glucose tolerance, prevention interventions presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diabetes Prevention Program results and diagnostic cut-offs are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
