## [1395] pre-eclampsia

**Cluster**: preeclampsia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pre-eclampsia has lasting consequences: offspring have higher blood pressure and affected women have persistent subclinical atherosclerosis, supporting early follow-up. Evidence for arginine, citrulline and statins in prevention is tentative.

### Core Teaching Points
- Offspring of pregnancies with preeclampsia are at increased risk of high blood pressure during childhood and young adulthood, so BP monitoring is important. (PMID 38780756)
- Women with a history of pre-eclampsia show subclinical atherosclerosis during pregnancy that persists long term, supporting early follow-up. (PMID 40796266)
- L-arginine and L-citrulline may help prevent and treat pre-eclampsia, but findings need cautious interpretation and more trials on dose and timing. (PMID 39800868)
- Statin use in pregnancy has been associated with reduced preeclampsia risk, but observations need confirmation in high-quality trials. (PMID 40796817)
- Pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension are linked to altered lipid levels during pregnancy. (PMID 40407883)
- PlGF-based testing for suspected preterm preeclampsia depends on assay selection, with cutoffs either single or gestational-age specific. (PMID 42336060)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Preventive agents beyond aspirin.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of pre-eclampsia.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Long-term cardiovascular risk after pre-eclampsia.
- PlGF testing in suspected preterm pre-eclampsia.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in pre-eclampsia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman 5 years after pre-eclampsia asking about cardiovascular follow-up.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pre-eclampsia presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Aspirin prophylaxis, diagnostic criteria, magnesium sulfate and delivery timing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38780756**: *Association Between Preeclampsia and Blood Pressure in Offspring: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39800868**: *L-Arginine and L-Citrulline for Prevention and Treatment of Pre-Eclampsia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40407883**: *Pre-eclampsia, gestational hypertension, and lipid levels during pregnancy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40796266**: *Subclinical atherosclerosis in women after pre-eclampsia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40796817**: *The role of statins during pregnancy on maternal risk of preeclampsia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 42336060**: *Laboratory Perspectives on Assay Selection and Implementation of PlGF-Based Testing for Suspected Preterm Preeclampsia* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1398] Pregnancy and the Kidney

**Cluster**: pregnancy_kidney | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
CKD raises the risk of adverse pregnancy outcomes including pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension, and requires multidisciplinary counselling. RAAS inhibitor guidance for women of childbearing potential is inconsistent across guidelines.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of pregnant women with chronic kidney disease found adverse pregnancy outcomes, including pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension, with heterogeneity in how CKD in pregnancy is defined. (PMID 38488268)
- A best-practice position statement advises extensive personalised counselling with a multidisciplinary team and close monitoring during medically assisted reproduction and subsequent pregnancy in CKD. (PMID 37354277)
- A review of national and international guidelines found inconsistent wording on RAAS blocker prescription in women of childbearing potential, in pregnancy and postpartum, and called for more explicit advice. (PMID 40044928)
- Normal physiological changes in pregnancy induce marked renal vasodilation, increasing glomerular filtration rate by 40-50% with an associated physiological reduction in serum creatinine (normal pregnancy reference: 35-65 umol/L or 0.4-0.7 mg/dL); serum creatinine levels >=75 umol/L (>=0.8 mg/dL) signify underlying renal impairment and require nephrological investigation. (PMID 37354277)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Standard definition of CKD in pregnancy.
- Safety profile and fetal outcomes of continuing non-selective immunosuppressants (tacrolimus, azathioprine) during lupus nephritis flare in pregnancy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pre-pregnancy counselling in CKD.
- RAAS inhibitors and pregnancy.
- Normal physiological renal adaptations in pregnancy (increased GFR, decreased baseline serum creatinine) and identifying pathological thresholds.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with CKD and ACE inhibitor planning pregnancy.
- A 28-year-old primigravida at 16 weeks gestation found on routine booking labs to have serum creatinine 84 umol/L (0.95 mg/dL) and microscopic hematuria requiring formal nephrology review.

**Reviewer Gap**: Stage-specific risks, aspirin use and dialysis in pregnancy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37354277**: *Chronic kidney disease, female infertility, and medically assisted reproduction: a best practice position statement by the Kidney and Pregnancy Group of the Italian Society of Nephrology* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38488268**: *Adverse pregnancy outcomes in pregnant women with chronic kidney disease: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40044928**: *RAAS inhibitors in pregnancy, breastfeeding and women of childbearing potential: a review of national and international clinical practice guidelines* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1399] Pregnancy in chronic kidney disease

**Cluster**: pregnancy_kidney | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
CKD raises the risk of adverse pregnancy outcomes including pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension, and requires multidisciplinary counselling. RAAS inhibitor guidance for women of childbearing potential is inconsistent across guidelines.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of pregnant women with chronic kidney disease found adverse pregnancy outcomes, including pre-eclampsia and gestational hypertension, with heterogeneity in how CKD in pregnancy is defined. (PMID 38488268)
- A best-practice position statement advises extensive personalised counselling with a multidisciplinary team and close monitoring during medically assisted reproduction and subsequent pregnancy in CKD. (PMID 37354277)
- A review of national and international guidelines found inconsistent wording on RAAS blocker prescription in women of childbearing potential, in pregnancy and postpartum, and called for more explicit advice. (PMID 40044928)
- In women with chronic kidney disease contemplating pregnancy, adverse maternal and fetal risks (pre-eclampsia, preterm birth, accelerated decline in maternal eGFR) correlate directly with baseline CKD stage, pre-existing hypertension, and degree of proteinuria; teratogenic medications (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, MRA, mycophenolate mofetil) must be safely transitioned to pregnancy-compatible alternatives (labetalol, nifedipine, azathioprine) prior to conception. (PMID 37354277)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Standard definition of CKD in pregnancy.
- Impact of intensified hemodialysis schedules (>=20-36 hours/week) on improving live-birth rates in women with end-stage kidney disease who conceive on maintenance dialysis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pre-pregnancy counselling in CKD.
- RAAS inhibitors and pregnancy.
- Pre-conception risk stratification and medication safety transitions (discontinuing ACEi/ARBs and mycophenolate) in reproductive-aged women with CKD.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with CKD and ACE inhibitor planning pregnancy.
- A 32-year-old woman with IgA nephropathy (eGFR 55 mL/min/1.73m2, proteinuria 1.2 g/day) taking ramipril seeking preconception counseling.

**Reviewer Gap**: Stage-specific risks, aspirin use and dialysis in pregnancy are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37354277**: *Chronic kidney disease, female infertility, and medically assisted reproduction: a best practice position statement by the Kidney and Pregnancy Group of the Italian Society of Nephrology* (2023) [guideline] - None

Preparing for renal replacement therapy spans vascular access planning, ICU RRT prescription, and conservative management or withdrawal of dialysis as an informed choice.

## [1403] pre-renal acute kidney injury

**Cluster**: prerenal_aki | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Consensus reports address haemodynamic and perfusion-related AKI in specific settings: sepsis, cirrhosis (hepatorenal) and cardiac surgery. Early recognition matters because splanchnic vasoconstrictors can reverse hepatorenal AKI, and euvolaemia and perfusion-guided therapy may mitigate cardiac surgery AKI.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ADQI consensus on sepsis-associated AKI gives graded recommendations for management, notes that therapeutic options are limited, and highlights care bundles and extracorporeal techniques as under study. (PMID 36823168)
- In cirrhosis, early recognition of HRS-AKI is crucial because splanchnic vasoconstrictors may reverse the AKI and bridge to liver transplantation, the only curative option; a joint ICA-ADQI meeting developed new diagnostic criteria and recommendations. (PMID 38527522)
- For adult cardiac surgery AKI, prevention and early detection are paramount: attention to presurgical kidney health, a cardiopulmonary bypass bundle, strategies to maintain intravascular euvolaemia, and goal-directed therapy prioritising oxygen delivery and end-organ perfusion may mitigate incidence. (PMID 37355415)
- The current lack of specific therapy for AKI prevention or treatment demands refinement of clinical trial design, including predictive and prognostic enrichment. (PMID 39115567)
- A paediatric Delphi consensus recommends adjudicating fluid balance by disease severity and broadening AKI phenotype definitions. (PMID 36178697)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Therapeutic options for AKI are limited.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of pre-renal acute kidney injury.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- HRS-AKI responds to splanchnic vasoconstrictors.
- Maintain euvolaemia and perfusion in cardiac surgery.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in pre-renal acute kidney injury.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Cirrhotic patient with ascites and rising creatinine after diuretics.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pre-renal acute kidney injury presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Classic pre-renal versus ATN distinction (urine sodium, FENa, urea ratio), fluid challenge and nephrotoxin avoidance are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page covers consensus reports by setting only.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36178697**: *Consensus-Based Recommendations on Priority Activities to Address Acute Kidney Injury in Children: A Modified Delphi Consensus Statement* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 36823168**: *Sepsis-associated acute kidney injury: consensus report of the 28th Acute Disease Quality Initiative workgroup* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37355415**: *Adult Cardiac Surgery-Associated Acute Kidney Injury: Joint Consensus Report* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38527522**: *Acute kidney injury in patients with cirrhosis: Acute Disease Quality Initiative (ADQI) and International Club of Ascites (ICA) joint multidisciplinary consensus meeting* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39115567**: *Recommendations for clinical trial design in acute kidney injury from the 31st acute disease quality initiative consensus conference. A consensus statement* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1404] Pressure ulcers: staging, prevention and wound management

**Cluster**: pressure_ulcers | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pressure injury prevention bundles reduce hospital-acquired injuries, though certainty is very low, and nutritional interventions have very uncertain effects.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review found pressure injury prevention care bundles decrease hospital-acquired pressure injuries, but the certainty of this evidence is very low. (PMID 38642429)
- A Cochrane review found the evidence very uncertain for the effect of protein, arginine, zinc and antioxidants and several other nutritional supplements on pressure ulcers. (PMID 38345088)
- The international pressure ulcer/injury guidelines, updated since 2009, are widely cited, indicating worldwide dissemination. (PMID 39254059)
- The 2019 international guideline's nutrition recommendations are translated into policy and practice points for wound healing. (PMID 34462964)
- Implementation of the RNAO prevention guideline in Chile shows coordinated governance, standardised indicators and structured support can scale evidence-based practice. (PMID 42266850)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which bundle components matter.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pressure ulcers: staging, prevention and wound management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Prevention bundles reduce hospital-acquired pressure injury.
- Nutrition for wound healing.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pressure ulcers: staging, prevention and wound management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Immobile inpatient with non-blanching erythema over the sacrum.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pressure ulcers: staging, prevention and wound management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

## [1405] preventing type 1 diabetes

**Cluster**: prevent_t1d | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Screening for islet autoantibodies identifies early-stage type 1 diabetes. Teplizumab is an approved immunotherapy to delay progression, with consensus statements on selection, monitoring and side effects.

### Core Teaching Points
- ISPAD 2024 guidelines cover screening, staging and strategies to preserve beta-cell function and discuss FDA approval of teplizumab to delay progression. (PMID 39662065)
- The Pediatric Endocrine Society statement gives a protocol for teplizumab administration and calls for long-term surveillance of efficacy and safety. (PMID 38663372)
- UK recommendations for pre-stage 3 type 1 diabetes address islet autoantibody confirmation, monitoring in early-stage disease, starting insulin and single autoantibody positivity. (PMID 40988096)
- A consensus describes the imperatives for implementing general-population islet autoantibody screening for early-stage T1D in Europe. (PMID 41821267)
- A network meta-analysis examined interventions to preserve beta cell function in people newly diagnosed with type 1 diabetes. (PMID 40598585)
- A British Society consensus covers teplizumab in stage 2 T1D, including patient selection, monitoring and common side effects. (PMID 42051156)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term benefit of teplizumab.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of preventing type 1 diabetes.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Staging of T1D by autoantibodies and dysglycaemia.
- Teplizumab delays stage 3 onset.

## [1406] Preventive cardiology: cardiovascular risk calculators, primary prevention guidelines

**Cluster**: preventive_cardiology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Primary prevention rests on cardiovascular risk assessment tools guiding lipid-lowering therapy, with targeted screening for asymptomatic atherosclerosis to individualise treatment.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review links CVD risk assessment tools to lipid-lowering recommendations in primary prevention and highlights targeted screening for asymptomatic atherosclerosis to individualise therapy. (PMID 40217673)
- A living guideline on type 2 diabetes therapeutics assesses cardiovascular, kidney and weight-loss effects, noting cost and availability will affect implementation. (PMID 40813129)
- A systematic review found interventions targeting both clinician support and patient access were most effective at improving secondary cardiovascular risk reduction prescribing and lowering lipids in peripheral artery disease. (PMID 41260242)
- The 2025 AHA/ACC hypertension guideline update emphasises tailored strategies and equitable access to preventive therapy. (PMID 41843050)
- An Italian call to action says cardiovascular prevention must start in childhood. (PMID 41366616)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of imaging for risk reclassification.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Preventive cardiology: cardiovascular risk calculators, primary prevention guidelines.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Risk calculators guide statin initiation.
- Primary versus secondary prevention.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Preventive cardiology: cardiovascular risk calculators, primary prevention guidelines.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Asymptomatic 55-year-old with borderline risk and family history.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Preventive cardiology: cardiovascular risk calculators, primary prevention guidelines presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific calculators, thresholds and LDL targets are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1407] Primary adrenal insufficiency Addisons disease: autoimmune causes, crisis prevention

**Cluster**: addison | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Primary adrenal insufficiency carries a 2.5-fold increase in mortality, mostly from adrenal crisis. Glucocorticoid replacement is linked with more fractures, and pregnancy outcomes are worse.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found mortality in primary adrenal insufficiency increased 2.5-fold, with adrenal crisis the main cause of death in CAH. (PMID 40966723)
- Patients with adrenal insufficiency on glucocorticoid replacement had a higher rate of fractures than controls. (PMID 33846948)
- A systematic review of interventions to prevent adrenal crisis found an emerging field with limited studies and a need for behaviour change intervention research. (PMID 35536876)
- Women with primary adrenal insufficiency had more negative pregnancy and fetal outcomes, including spontaneous abortion, preterm birth and small for gestational age infants. (PMID 39277840)
- Antiphospholipid syndrome-associated adrenal haemorrhage is severe with increased mortality, and most patients develop permanent primary adrenal insufficiency. (PMID 38244823)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal replacement dosing.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Primary adrenal insufficiency Addisons disease: autoimmune causes, crisis prevention.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Adrenal crisis as the leading cause of death.
- APS-associated adrenal haemorrhage.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Primary adrenal insufficiency Addisons disease: autoimmune causes, crisis prevention.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with Addison's disease presenting with vomiting and hypotension.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary adrenal insufficiency Addisons disease: autoimmune causes, crisis prevention presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Autoimmune aetiology, diagnostic ACTH stimulation testing and stress dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33846948**: *Rate of fracture in patients with glucocorticoid replacement therapy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35536876**: *Interventions for the prevention of adrenal crisis in adults with primary adrenal insufficiency: a systematic review* (2022) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 38244823**: *Clinical presentation and outcomes in patients with antiphospholipid syndrome-associated adrenal hemorrhage. A multicenter cohort study and systematic literature review* (2024) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 39277840**: *Increased prevalence of negative pregnancy and fetal outcomes in women with primary adrenal insufficiency. A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1408] Primary aldosteronism diagnosis and treatment

**Cluster**: primary_aldosteronism | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The Endocrine Society guideline covers primary aldosteronism, with adrenal vein sampling recommendations and a push for broader screening among hypertensive patients.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Endocrine Society guideline advises that in people on MRA therapy, renin should be monitored, and and gives further advice for those whose hypertension remains uncontrolled with suppressed renin. (PMID 40658480)
- A supporting systematic review addresses managing primary aldosteronism, including outcomes such as mortality, atrial fibrillation and stroke at very low certainty. (PMID 40658500)
- Australian and New Zealand recommendations cover preparation, performance and interpretation of adrenal vein sampling. (PMID 39360599)
- An expert consensus proposes a primary aldosteronism severity classification to grade recommendations for AVS before surgery. (PMID 40485198)
- Contemporary guidelines are aligning towards earlier and broader primary aldosteronism screening across the hypertensive population. (PMID 41645899)
- A European Reference Network guideline covers genetically proven familial hyperaldosteronism. (PMID 38571460)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Who should have AVS.
- Adrenal venous sampling (AVS) cutoffs with ACTH stimulation vs non-stimulated protocols, and concordance with dedicated adrenal CT.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Aldosterone-renin ratio screening.
- Adrenal vein sampling for lateralisation.
- Medical therapy with mineralocorticoid receptor antagonists (spironolactone vs eplerenone) in bilateral adrenal hyperplasia or non-surgical candidates.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young hypertensive patient with hypokalaemia.
- 45-year-old female with treatment-resistant hypertension on 4 agents and unprovoked hypokalaemia (K+ 2.9 mmol/L) with ARR > 30 and non-suppressible post-saline aldosterone.

**Reviewer Gap**: Screening ratio cut-offs and the surgical versus MRA decision are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1409] primary angioplasty versus thrombolytic therapy acute myocardial infarction

**Cluster**: stemi_pci | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Primary PCI is the preferred reperfusion strategy for STEMI; where timely access is unavailable, other reperfusion strategies matter. The 2025 ACC/AHA ACS guideline is the current US reference.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis of reperfusion strategies identifies primary PCI as the preferred treatment for STEMI, with implications for settings where timely primary PCI is not available. (PMID 32500800)
- The 2025 ACC/AHA/ACEP/NAEMSP/SCAI acute coronary syndromes guideline updates many earlier recommendations with new evidence and adds new recommendations where supported by published data. (PMID 40014670)
- A SCAI expert consensus covers STEMI management in the catheterisation laboratory, including options for large thrombus burden and varied anatomical and clinical circumstances. (PMID 39649824)
- A clinical consensus statement addresses reperfusion for STEMI in low- and middle-income countries, including patient education and secondary prevention. (PMID 40922666)
- Stress hyperglycaemia and fasting glucose predict short-term outcomes in STEMI, while HbA1c better predicts longer-term outcomes. (PMID 38529334)
- A meta-analysis of laboratory parameters aims to refine prediction of the no-reflow phenomenon after STEMI to guide prevention. (PMID 38400562)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Pharmaco-invasive strategy where PCI is delayed.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of primary angioplasty versus thrombolytic therapy acute myocardial infarction.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Primary PCI versus fibrinolysis.
- No-reflow after primary PCI.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in primary angioplasty versus thrombolytic therapy acute myocardial infarction.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Anterior STEMI presenting to a hospital without a catheterisation laboratory.
- A complex multimorbid patient with primary angioplasty versus thrombolytic therapy acute myocardial infarction presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Door-to-balloon time targets, antithrombotic regimens and complete revascularisation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32500800**: *Comparison of Reperfusion Strategies for ST-Segment-Elevation Myocardial Infarction: A Multivariate Network Meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1410] Primary biliary cholangitis pharmacotherapy

**Cluster**: pbc | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Second-line therapy in PBC after UDCA includes PPAR agonists and obeticholic acid; PPAR agonists reduce ALP, GGT and bilirubin when added to UDCA. Fatigue has no recommended pharmacological treatment.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found adding PPAR agonists to UDCA effectively reduces ALP, GGT and bilirubin compared with UDCA alone. (PMID 40200180)
- A network meta-analysis of second-line treatments in PBC found one drug associated with lower incidence of pruritus, while obeticholic acid was associated with more pruritus and serious adverse events at similar efficacy. (PMID 39720853)
- A European position paper finds that, based on available trials, no pharmacological treatment can be recommended for PBC-related fatigue and proposes an ASK-MEASURE-TREAT algorithm. (PMID 41082897)
- An EASL-AASLD Delphi consensus provides a framework on surrogate endpoints and real-world evidence to support regulatory pathways for new PBC therapies. (PMID 42191456)
- The global prevalence of PBC is rising, representing a growing healthcare burden, and an association with latitude requires further study. (PMID 40398833)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Hard-outcome benefit of second-line agents.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Primary biliary cholangitis pharmacotherapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pruritus and fatigue are major symptoms.
- UDCA first-line, add second-line for inadequate response.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Primary biliary cholangitis pharmacotherapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Middle-aged woman with itch, raised ALP and anti-mitochondrial antibodies.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary biliary cholangitis pharmacotherapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: First-line UDCA dosing, response criteria and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39720853**: *Second-Line Treatment for Patients With Primary Biliary Cholangitis: A Systematic Review With Network Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40200180**: *Efficacy and safety of PPAR agonists in primary biliary cholangitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40398833**: *Global Epidemiology of Primary Biliary Cholangitis: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41082897**: *Fatigue in people with primary biliary cholangitis: a position paper from the European Reference Network for Rare Liver Diseases* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42191456**: *EASL-AASLD Delphi consensus statement on surrogate endpoints and real-world evidence in primary biliary cholangitis* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1411] primary care mental health

**Cluster**: primary_care_mental | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In primary care, PHQ-9 is widely validated for depression screening in a two-stage process, but detection is very low in low- and middle-income countries. Psychotherapy matches pharmacotherapy, combined treatment may be best, and CBT is recommended for mild to moderate depression.

### Core Teaching Points
- PHQ-9 has been widely validated for depression screening in primary care and is recommended in a two-stage screening process, though longitudinal studies of screening effectiveness are needed. (PMID 33126078)
- A meta-analysis found extremely low depression detection by primary care clinicians in low- and middle-income countries, posing a serious threat to scaling up mental healthcare. (PMID 35123556)
- A network meta-analysis found psychotherapy likely effective for depression in primary care versus care as usual or waitlist, with effects comparable to pharmacotherapy and combined treatment possibly better than either alone. (PMID 34180847)
- A meta-analysis supports offering CBT to primary care patients with mild to moderate depression, regardless of whether delivered individually, in groups or as therapist-guided self-help. (PMID 30688184)
- E-health interventions for depression in primary care have a small effect and appear cost-effective, with no evidence for effectiveness in anxiety. (PMID 30447572)
- A review found treatment effects for CBT and problem-solving therapy in primary care depression and anxiety, and in-person and technology-assisted treatments were both effective. (PMID 30699860)
- An individual participant data meta-analysis found the collaborative care component most associated with reduced depressive symptoms was a therapeutic treatment strategy such as manual-based psychotherapy or family integration. (PMID 40136273)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Anxiety interventions in primary care have thin evidence.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of primary care mental health.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PHQ-9 two-stage screening.
- Collaborative care components.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in primary care mental health.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with low mood and sleep disturbance in a GP clinic.
- A complex multimorbid patient with primary care mental health presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Antidepressant choice, stepped-care thresholds, suicide risk assessment and anxiety, somatisation and substance use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 30447572**: *The effectiveness and cost-effectiveness of e-health interventions for depression and anxiety in primary care: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 30688184**: *Cognitive behaviour therapy for depression in primary care: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 30699860**: *The effectiveness of four empirically supported psychotherapies for primary care depression and anxiety: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 33126078**: *Screening for depression in primary care with Patient Health Questionnaire-9 (PHQ-9): A systematic review* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 34180847**: *Psychologic Treatment of Depression Compared With Pharmacotherapy and Combined Treatment in Primary Care: A Network Meta-Analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35123556**: *Under detection of depression in primary care settings in low and middle-income countries: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40136273**: *Effective Components of Collaborative Care for Depression in Primary Care: An Individual Participant Data Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1412] Primary CNS Lymphoma

**Cluster**: pcnsl | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Primary CNS lymphoma care includes rituximab-containing treatment, with preoperative corticosteroids reducing biopsy diagnostic yield. Ibrutinib and PD-1 inhibitors show activity in relapsed disease.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis suggests rituximab may have a positive impact on the prognosis of patients with PCNSL. (PMID 37453867)
- Preoperative corticosteroids reduce the diagnostic yield of stereotactic biopsies in PCNSL, with no evidence that tapering steroids before biopsy improves diagnostic rates, and CSF analysis has poor sensitivity. (PMID 38865324)
- In selected patients, surgical resection of PCNSL is associated with significantly better overall and progression-free survival than biopsy alone. (PMID 36449256)
- A meta-analysis of ibrutinib in CNS lymphoma calls for trials comparing it with standard therapies and for long-term safety data. (PMID 39689737)
- PD-1 inhibitors showed responses in relapsed PCNSL and primary testicular lymphoma, with mostly mild skin adverse effects. (PMID 40299829)
- Consensus recommendations define ideal and minimum-standard MRI and PET protocols at 3T and 1.5T for PCNSL imaging. (PMID 33560416)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal induction regimen is not covered.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of Primary CNS Lymphoma.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Avoid steroids before biopsy when lymphoma is suspected.
- Imaging protocols for PCNSL.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Primary CNS Lymphoma.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with periventricular enhancing lesion and suspected lymphoma awaiting biopsy.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary CNS Lymphoma presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: High-dose methotrexate induction and consolidation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33560416**: *Consensus recommendations for MRI and PET imaging of primary central nervous system lymphoma: guideline statement from the International Primary CNS Lymphoma Collaborative Group (IPCG)* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 36449256**: *Surgical resection versus biopsy in the treatment of primary central nervous system lymphoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37453867**: *Role of Rituximab in Treatment of Patients With Primary Central Nervous System Lymphoma (PCNSL): A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38865324**: *Preoperative Corticosteroids Reduce Diagnostic Accuracy of Stereotactic Biopsies in Primary Central Nervous System Lymphoma: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39689737**: *Efficacy and safety of ibrutinib in central nervous system lymphoma: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40299829**: *Safety and efficacy of programmed cell death-1 inhibitors in relapsed immune-privileged site lymphoma: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1414] primary hyperparathyroidism

**Cluster**: hyperparathyroidism | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
A French and Italian guideline set covers diagnosis, indications for treatment, genetics, syndromic disease and recurrent disease in primary hyperparathyroidism.

### Core Teaching Points
- Guidelines outline investigation to identify classical and non-classical features of primary hyperparathyroidism and the methodology to locate abnormal tissue, with surgical and conservative management. (PMID 39162944)
- The diagnosis chapter notes the oral calcium loading test and the Pro-FHH score contribute to diagnosis in atypical forms. (PMID 39818287)
- Genetic assessment should guide diagnosis of hereditary primary hyperparathyroidism, with genetic counselling for patient and family. (PMID 39818301)
- In syndromic disease, surgical indications should be discussed in an expert centre, and recurrence risk, particularly high in MEN1, requires long-term monitoring. (PMID 39818298)
- For recurrent or persistent disease, revision surgery should follow laryngoscopy in an expert centre, ideally with intraoperative PTH measurement and recurrent nerve neuromonitoring. (PMID 39818302)
- Treatment options are surgery, local destruction and medical management, with indications addressed in a separate chapter. (PMID 39818297, PMID 39818285)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Management of asymptomatic patients is only partly covered.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of primary hyperparathyroidism.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- MEN1 and high recurrence risk.
- Revision surgery precautions.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in primary hyperparathyroidism.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with hypercalcaemia and raised PTH deciding between surgery and observation.
- A complex multimorbid patient with primary hyperparathyroidism presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric surgical criteria for asymptomatic patients are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39162944**: *Primary hyperparathyroidism: from guidelines to outpatient clinic* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39818285**: *Chapter 9: Indications for the treatment of primary hyperparathyroidism* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39818287**: *Chapter 2: Primary Hyperparathyroidism: diagnosis* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1416] primary immunodeficiency immunoglobulin replacement

**Cluster**: pid_ig | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
UKPIN consensus guidelines give structured statements for starting, monitoring, home delivery and stopping immunoglobulin replacement in antibody deficiency. A 2025 practice parameter update covers immunoglobulin replacement, prophylaxis and transplant in inborn errors of immunity.

### Core Teaching Points
- A British consensus guideline sets 22 statements for initiation, 22 for monitoring, 11 for home therapy and 19 for discontinuation of immunoglobulin replacement therapy. (PMID 35924867)
- The 2025 practice parameter update on inborn errors of immunity includes recommendations for immunoglobulin replacement, antibiotic prophylaxis, haematopoietic stem cell transplantation, precision medicine and quality-of-life assessment. (PMID 42208906)
- A review of immunoglobulin replacement in antibody production defects gives practical guidance on selecting available modalities to improve adherence and effectiveness. (PMID 42104827)
- A scoping review found no international consensus guidelines for several highly prevalent inborn errors of immunity. (PMID 40902944)
- An Italian consensus aims to limit inappropriate use of genetic testing for inborn errors of immunity and reduce misdiagnosis. (PMID 39622296)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Dose targets are not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of primary immunodeficiency immunoglobulin replacement.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Indications for immunoglobulin replacement.
- Subcutaneous versus intravenous routes (not in abstracts).
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in primary immunodeficiency immunoglobulin replacement.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with recurrent sinopulmonary infections and low IgG.
- A complex multimorbid patient with primary immunodeficiency immunoglobulin replacement presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Trough IgG targets and dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1417] Primary Mediastinal B-Cell Lymphoma

**Cluster**: pmbcl | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Dose-intensive chemoimmunotherapy in PMBCL is associated with improved survival and may curtail radiotherapy exposure. LYSA, British and Chinese guidelines now exist.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis supports dose-intensive chemoimmunotherapy in PMBCL, with improved survival and reduced radiotherapy exposure and long-term toxicity, pending randomised trials. (PMID 37646662)
- The LYSA pragmatic guidelines give a practical framework for translating best available evidence in PMBCL into practice. (PMID 40157284)
- The British Society for Haematology guideline notes dose-intensive regimens and gives a framework for patients with Deauville 4 and 5 response on PET-CT after chemoimmunotherapy. (PMID 42634099)
- A Chinese expert consensus (2024) covers diagnosis and management of primary mediastinal large B-cell lymphoma. (PMID 38716591)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of radiotherapy and checkpoint inhibitors.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Primary Mediastinal B-Cell Lymphoma.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles

- Evidence-based therapeutic selection, primary dosing protocols, and contraindications in Primary Mediastinal B-Cell Lymphoma.

## [1418] Primary membranous nephropathy immunosuppression

**Cluster**: membranous | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Anti-PLA2R levels of at least 20 RU/mL with thorough secondary screening may make kidney biopsy unnecessary. Rituximab is comparable to cyclophosphamide and cyclosporine for idiopathic membranous nephropathy.

### Core Teaching Points
- At EUROIMMUN ELISA PLA2R antibody levels of at least 20 RU/mL with thorough secondary screening a kidney biopsy may be unnecessary, but lower or negative levels still warrant biopsy. (PMID 39820570)
- A meta-analysis found rituximab comparable in efficacy to cyclophosphamide and cyclosporine in idiopathic membranous nephropathy, with no significant difference in remission rates or severe adverse events, and treatment should be individualised. (PMID 41351015)
- Low-dose rituximab appears promising for primary membranous nephropathy as main-line and rescue therapy, but randomised trials are needed. (PMID 39508703)
- Glucocorticoid with cyclophosphamide was reviewed in membranous nephropathy, with limited data on comparisons against other drugs alone and on secondary disease. (PMID 39664513)
- Recurrent membranous nephropathy after kidney transplant has risk factors including living donor selection, pre-transplant anti-PLA2R levels, dialysis, induction immunosuppression and tacrolimus use. (PMID 40576095)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal rituximab dose.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Primary membranous nephropathy immunosuppression.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PLA2R antibody to avoid biopsy.
- Secondary causes must be screened.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Primary membranous nephropathy immunosuppression.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with nephrotic syndrome and positive anti-PLA2R antibodies.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary membranous nephropathy immunosuppression presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Supportive care, KDIGO risk-based treatment thresholds and remission definitions are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39508703**: *Low-Dose Rituximab in the Treatment of Primary Membranous Nephropathy - A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39664513**: *Efficacy and safety of glucocorticoid combined with cyclophosphamide therapy on membranous nephropathy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39820570**: *PLA2R autoantibodies, a multifaceted biomarker in nephrotic syndrome and membranous nephropathy* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40576095**: *Incidence and risk factors for recurrent membranous nephropathy after kidney transplantation: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41351015**: *Evaluating rituximab against cyclophosphamide or cyclosporine in idiopathic membranous nephropathy: a meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1419] Primary mitral regurgitation: watchful waiting vs surgical repair timing

**Cluster**: primary_mr | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
For degenerative mitral regurgitation, repair is associated with lower mortality than replacement, and waiting for Class I triggers is linked to postoperative survival loss, supporting early surgery. Guideline-adherent surveillance every 12 months or less improves outcomes in asymptomatic severe primary MR.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found mitral valve repair beneficial compared with replacement for degenerative MR across ages in all-cause mortality, with higher early mortality and reoperation in the replacement group. (PMID 30471709)
- A second meta-analysis found mitral replacement possibly associated with higher long-term mortality than repair in primary MR, though replacement is often a bailout option in more complex anatomy. (PMID 32648008)
- A registry analysis of 2,833 surgical DMR patients found that waiting for Class I or isolated Class IIa triggers before operating is associated with postoperative survival loss, encouraging an early surgical strategy. (PMID 38996050)
- In asymptomatic severe primary MR, guideline adherence with evaluations every 12 months or less was associated with earlier therapy and better long-term survival free from death or heart failure hospitalisation. (PMID 34325105)
- Baseline global longitudinal strain independently predicts postoperative outcomes in primary MR, with impaired values linked to higher mortality, though a preoperative threshold needs further analysis. (PMID 33783012)
- Percutaneous edge-to-edge repair appears efficacious and safe in both functional and degenerative MR in a meta-analysis, with randomised studies awaited. (PMID 28663365)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Exact GLS threshold for intervention.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Primary mitral regurgitation: watchful waiting vs surgical repair timing.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Repair preferred over replacement.
- Early surgery in asymptomatic severe MR.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Primary mitral regurgitation: watchful waiting vs surgical repair timing.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Asymptomatic patient with severe MR from mitral valve prolapse and preserved LVEF.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary mitral regurgitation: watchful waiting vs surgical repair timing presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Class I trigger thresholds (LVEF, LVESD), flail leaflet and AF triggers, and TEER role in primary MR are not stated beyond the excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28663365**: *Outcome after percutaneous edge-to-edge mitral repair for functional and degenerative mitral regurgitation: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2018) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 30471709**: *Meta-Analysis Comparing Mitral Valve Repair Versus Replacement for Degenerative Mitral Regurgitation Across All Ages* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 32648008**: *Comparison of Mitral Valve Replacement and Repair for Degenerative Mitral Regurgitation: a Meta-analysis and Implications for Transcatheter Mitral Procedures* (2020) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 33783012**: *Global longitudinal strain to determine optimal timing for surgery in primary mitral regurgitation: A systematic review* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 34325105**: *Relation of Guideline Adherence to Outcomes in Patients With Asymptomatic Severe Primary Mitral Regurgitation* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38996050**: *Survival loss linked to guideline-based indications for degenerative mitral regurgitation surgery* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1420] Primary Myelofibrosis

**Cluster**: myelofibrosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
German ONKOPEDIA and NCCN guidance were updated in 2025-2026 for primary myelofibrosis. Ruxolitinib-based combinations may enhance efficacy, with response influenced by prior JAK inhibitor exposure.

### Core Teaching Points
- The German ONKOPEDIA guideline 2025 for primary myelofibrosis notes that combination therapies and novel agents show efficacy and point to future developments but are not yet available for routine practice. (PMID 42324204)
- The ONKOPEDIA myelofibrosis recommendations were updated by an MPN expert panel, extending the earlier guideline in light of recent developments. (PMID 42324205)
- A meta-analysis of ruxolitinib-based combination therapy in myelofibrosis found added drugs may further enhance efficacy, with personalised selection essential because efficacy is influenced by prior JAK inhibitor exposure. (PMID 41761659)
- NCCN Guidelines Insights (version 2.2026) focus on recent updates for myelofibrosis within myeloproliferative neoplasms. (PMID 42425157)
- EBMT recommendations address allogeneic transplantation in non-classical MPNs and MDS/MPNs, citing a lack of robust evidence. (PMID 40106773)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Transplant timing and sequencing of newer agents.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Primary Myelofibrosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- JAK inhibitors in myelofibrosis.
- Role of allogeneic transplant.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Primary Myelofibrosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with splenomegaly, constitutional symptoms and leukoerythroblastic film.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary Myelofibrosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prognostic scores, JAK2/CALR/MPL diagnosis and fedratinib or pacritinib use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1421] primary prevention icd sudden cardiac death

**Cluster**: icd_primary | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
ICD therapy for primary prevention is associated with lower all-cause mortality, sudden cardiac death and arrhythmic death in ischaemic and non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy. Patient selection by LVEF alone remains imperfect.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found ICD therapy was associated with reduced all-cause mortality, sudden cardiac death and arrhythmic death in both ischaemic and non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy. (PMID 37952790)
- Pooled cohort data after myocardial infarction could not identify low-risk individuals with severely reduced LVEF or high-risk individuals with preserved LVEF, using LVEF or other predictors. (PMID 39378245)
- In non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy, patients without a guideline ICD indication had comparable rates of appropriate ICD therapy, suggesting current indications leave out a significant group. (PMID 40354022)
- Non-sustained VT was modestly associated with appropriate ICD therapy (HR 1.82) but not with VF or mortality in a primary prevention cohort, suggesting its clinical role be reconsidered. (PMID 41967966)
- Subcutaneous ICDs remain effective in obese patients, but technical challenges and higher complication rates warrant attention. (PMID 39019386)
- A study found a gap between guideline indications and real-world prescription of wearable cardioverter-defibrillators after acute myocardial infarction. (PMID 41655635)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Reassessing ICD need after LVEF recovery on quadruple therapy.
- Optimal treatment duration, monitoring intervals, and comparative effectiveness of second-line therapies in high-risk subgroups of primary prevention icd sudden cardiac death.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- LVEF threshold as imperfect risk marker.
- Subcutaneous ICD considerations.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in primary prevention icd sudden cardiac death.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with LVEF 30% after MI on optimal therapy considered for ICD.
- A complex multimorbid patient with primary prevention icd sudden cardiac death presenting with treatment intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific LVEF thresholds, timing after MI and DANISH-type trial results are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37952790**: *Implantable cardioverter defibrillators for primary prevention in patients with ischemic and non-ischemic cardiomyopathy: A meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39019386**: *Efficacy and safety of the subcutaneous implantable cardioverter-defibrillator in patients with and without obesity: A meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39378245**: *Sudden cardiac death after myocardial infarction: individual participant data from pooled cohorts* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40354022**: *Primary prevention ICD in non-ischaemic cardiomyopathy: an ongoing search for improvement of current indications : A retrospective study analysing the impact of the new Dutch guideline on the use of ICDs* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41655635**: *Gap between guideline-based indications and real-world implementation of wearable cardioverter-defibrillators after acute myocardial infarction* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41967966**: *Contribution of Non-Sustained Ventricular Tachycardia to the Primary Prevention of Sudden Cardiac Death　- Validation of Current Japanese Circulation Society / Japanese Heart Rhythm Society Guideline Recommendations in the Nippon Storm Study* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1422] Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis

**Cluster**: psc | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PSC is rare with variable global incidence, a high risk of hepatobiliary malignancy and uneven guideline adherence. MR imaging consensus and paediatric PSC-IBD position papers guide care.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of population-based studies reports incidence and prevalence of PSC and calls for further epidemiology in Asia, Africa and Latin America. (PMID 38052097)
- A meta-analysis of hepatobiliary malignancy in PSC found patients with PSC-IBD may be at higher risk for HCC, and suggests data may guide surveillance strategies. (PMID 39709139)
- An ESGAR consensus statement defines MR imaging in PSC with a structured reporting template. (PMID 40285815)
- An ESPGHAN position paper gives guidance on diagnostic criteria, follow-up and therapeutic strategies for children with PSC and IBD. (PMID 39741383)
- A five-year North American analysis found gaps in guideline adherence in PSC, notably in baseline fibrosis assessment and hepatobiliary cancer imaging. (PMID 41964270)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Effective medical therapy.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PSC and IBD association.
- Cholangiocarcinoma surveillance.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young man with ulcerative colitis and cholestatic liver tests.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, UDCA role and transplant indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38052097**: *Incidence and Prevalence of Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis: A Meta-analysis of Population-based Studies* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1424] Principles of Radiotherapy

**Cluster**: radiotherapy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Modern radiotherapy relies on site-specific guidelines. Stereotactic body radiotherapy is preferred for stage I NSCLC when surgery is unsuitable and is efficacious with low adverse events for bone metastases.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ACCP guideline states stereotactic body radiotherapy is the preferred approach for stage I NSCLC patients who are not appropriate surgical candidates. (PMID 40562304)
- ISRS-endorsed consensus on SBRT for non-spine bone metastases found it efficacious and associated with low adverse event rates. (PMID 39862925)
- An ESTRO guideline addresses patient selection for SBRT of spine metastases and safe implementation, and values prospective trial enrolment. (PMID 37925107)
- An ASTRO-ASCO-SSO guideline addresses postmastectomy radiation, including bolus use. (PMID 40955654)
- An ASTRO guideline addresses anal squamous cell carcinoma radiation dose, immunotherapy, adaptive RT and minimising long-term toxicity. (PMID 40023252)
- An international consensus defines clinical target volumes at different dose levels for nasopharyngeal carcinoma to improve coverage and standardisation. (PMID 40419028)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Dose-fractionation choices vary by site.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Principles of Radiotherapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- SBRT for inoperable early NSCLC.
- SBRT for oligometastatic bone disease.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Principles of Radiotherapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Frail patient with 2 cm peripheral stage I lung cancer.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Principles of Radiotherapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: General physics principles, radiosensitivity and toxicity grading are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1425] Procalcitonin and CRP in infection: diagnostic utility, antibiotic stewardship, limitations

**Cluster**: procalcitonin | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Procalcitonin has moderate accuracy for sepsis diagnosis and only slightly outperforms CRP. Guided discontinuation of antimicrobials in critically ill adults with sepsis is supported where antimicrobials would otherwise exceed seven days.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found procalcitonin offers moderate accuracy in diagnosing sepsis in adults admitted to the emergency department and distinguishes viral from bacterial inflammation rapidly. (PMID 38254218)
- Procalcitonin appears to slightly outperform CRP for sepsis diagnosis, but its discriminatory power remains limited. (PMID 40214293)
- A meta-analysis of PCT- or CRP-guided antimicrobial discontinuation in critically ill adults with sepsis supported guidance in Sepsis-3 where antimicrobials are used for more than 7 days, and called for studies of monitoring protocols and recurrence. (PMID 38949476)
- A GRADE-based guideline provides guidance on procalcitonin use in emergency departments. (PMID 39705853)
- In cirrhosis, PCT and presepsin have high sensitivity and specificity for bacterial infection, and PCT has good rule-in value for spontaneous bacterial peritonitis. (PMID 39958447)
- A diagnostic test accuracy meta-analysis evaluated procalcitonin and CRP for invasive and serious bacterial infection in young febrile infants. (PMID 38499017)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal stopping thresholds.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Procalcitonin and CRP in infection: diagnostic utility, antibiotic stewardship, limitations.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PCT-guided antibiotic discontinuation.
- PCT as rule-in test for SBP in cirrhosis.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Procalcitonin and CRP in infection: diagnostic utility, antibiotic stewardship, limitations.

### Case Generation Hooks
- ICU patient with sepsis on day 5 antibiotics and falling procalcitonin.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Procalcitonin and CRP in infection: diagnostic utility, antibiotic stewardship, limitations presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric PCT cut-offs and the effect on mortality are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38254218**: *Accuracy of procalcitonin for diagnosing sepsis in adult patients admitted to the emergency department: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38499017**: *Diagnostic test accuracy of procalcitonin and C-reactive protein for predicting invasive and serious bacterial infections in young febrile infants: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38949476**: *Benefits and Harms of Procalcitonin- or C-Reactive Protein-Guided Antimicrobial Discontinuation in Critically Ill Adults With Sepsis: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39705853**: *GRADE-based procalcitonin guideline for emergency departments* (2025) [guideline] - None

- **PMID 40214293**: *Diagnostic performances of procalcitonin and C-reactive protein for sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

- Procalcitonin appears to slightly outperform CRP for sepsis diagnosis, but its discriminatory power remains limited. (PMID 40214293)

- A GRADE-based guideline provides guidance on procalcitonin use in emergency departments. (PMID 39705853)
- In cirrhosis, PCT and presepsin have high sensitivity and specificity for bacterial infection, and PCT has good rule-in value for spontaneous bacterial peritonitis. (PMID 39958447)
- A diagnostic test accuracy meta-analysis evaluated procalcitonin and CRP for invasive and serious bacterial infection in young febrile infants. (PMID 38499017)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal stopping thresholds.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of procalcitonin guided antibiotic de escalation in icu.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PCT-guided antibiotic discontinuation.
- PCT as rule-in test for SBP in cirrhosis.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in procalcitonin guided antibiotic de escalation in icu.

### Case Generation Hooks
- ICU patient with sepsis on day 5 antibiotics and falling procalcitonin.
- A complex multimorbid patient with procalcitonin guided antibiotic de escalation in icu presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric PCT cut-offs and the effect on mortality are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Procalcitonin has moderate accuracy for sepsis diagnosis and only slightly outperforms CRP. Guided discontinuation of antimicrobials in critically ill adults with sepsis is supported where antimicrobials would otherwise exceed seven days.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found procalcitonin offers moderate accuracy in diagnosing sepsis in adults admitted to the emergency department and distinguishes viral from bacterial inflammation rapidly. (PMID 38254218)
- Procalcitonin appears to slightly outperform CRP for sepsis diagnosis, but its discriminatory power remains limited. (PMID 40214293)

- In cirrhosis, PCT and presepsin have high sensitivity and specificity for bacterial infection, and PCT has good rule-in value for spontaneous bacterial peritonitis. (PMID 39958447)
- A diagnostic test accuracy meta-analysis evaluated procalcitonin and CRP for invasive and serious bacterial infection in young febrile infants. (PMID 38499017)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties

- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Procalcitonin-guided antibiotic de-escalation in ICU.

## [1428] procedural sedation

**Cluster**: procedural_sedation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Remimazolam is a propofol alternative for procedural sedation with less hypotension and injection pain; elderly patients show more stable haemodynamics. ED sedation complications are usually respiratory rather than cardiovascular.

### Core Teaching Points
- Meta-analyses found remimazolam could be used as an alternative to propofol for procedural sedation and general anaesthesia, with no difference in sedation success, recovery or discharge, but more data are needed for general anaesthesia. (PMID 37334113, PMID 36326772)
- Remimazolam lowered the incidence of hypotension and injection pain compared with propofol across procedural sedation and general anaesthesia. (PMID 40503766)
- In elderly patients, remimazolam gave more stable haemodynamics after induction and may be safer than propofol. (PMID 39991057)
- Remimazolam did not increase postoperative delirium risk and maintained cognitive function versus propofol. (PMID 39748753)
- In emergency department and ICU sedation, recovery is shorter with propofol versus midazolam-opioids, and respiratory adverse events are less common with ketamine. (PMID 38185564)
- In adult emergency department procedural sedation, respiratory complications occur more often than hypotension, while laryngospasm, aspiration and intubation are rare but life-threatening. (PMID 39102153)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Haemodynamic benefit of remimazolam in induction is partly inconclusive.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Respiratory depression is the main risk.
- Ketamine causes fewer respiratory events.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Elderly patient needing cardioversion under procedural sedation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Fasting rules, monitoring standards and reversal agents are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36326772**: *Remimazolam versus propofol for procedural sedation and anesthesia: a systemic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37334113**: *Remimazolam <i>versus</i> propofol for procedural sedation: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38185564**: *Pharmacological agents for procedural sedation and analgesia in the emergency department and intensive care unit: a systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomised trials* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39102153**: *Safety of procedural sedation in emergency department settings among the adult population: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1429] prognosis of aortic stenosis

**Cluster**: as_prognosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Aortic stenosis prognosis worsens with heart failure and cardiac amyloidosis; TAVR is safe in amyloidosis and improves survival over medical therapy or SAVR. Cerebral embolic protection is not supported routinely.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found cardiac amyloidosis is associated with increased mortality in severe aortic stenosis, and TAVR is safe and improves survival more than medical therapy or SAVR. (PMID 39955158)
- An HFA consensus statement covers management of aortic stenosis with chronic heart failure, focusing on pre- and post-procedural care in patients undergoing AVR. (PMID 40948025)
- In aortic stenosis with coronary disease, PCI+TAVR was associated with higher mortality or adverse events than CABG+SAVR, but lower perioperative complications. (PMID 41067128)
- In low and intermediate-risk patients, TAVR versus SAVR shows short-term benefits, but higher permanent pacemaker, paravalvular leak and reintervention rates raise concerns for younger, lower-risk patients. (PMID 40425422)
- Evidence does not support routine cerebral embolic protection during TAVR given no significant benefit in key outcomes. (PMID 41729166, PMID 40910674)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term durability of TAVR in younger patients.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of prognosis of aortic stenosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Amyloidosis in severe AS.
- TAVR versus SAVR in low-risk patients.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in prognosis of aortic stenosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Octogenarian with severe AS, heart failure and suspected amyloid.
- A complex multimorbid patient with prognosis of aortic stenosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Natural history after symptom onset and the timing of intervention in asymptomatic patients are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39955158**: *Outcomes of aortic stenosis in patients with cardiac amyloidosis: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40425422**: *TAVR vs. SAVR for severe aortic stenosis in the low and intermediate surgical risk population: An updated meta-analysis, meta-regression, and trial sequential analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40910674**: *Efficacy of Cerebral Embolic Protection Device in Transcatheter Aortic Valve Replacement: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1430] prognosis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis

**Cluster**: ipf_prognosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
IPF carries high mortality: pooled 3-year survival is about 60-67%, and untreated mean overall survival is around 4 years. Antifibrotic therapy reduces all-cause mortality and acute exacerbation risk, and DLCO decline predicts death better than FVC in one registry.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found pooled 3-year cumulative survival of 59.9% before 2010 and 66.2% in the 2010s, noting no improving trend before 2010 and probable multifactorial improvement afterwards. (PMID 35295232)
- A systematic review of untreated IPF found pooled mortality of 12% at 1-2 years, 38% at 2-5 years and 69% at 5 years or more, with mean overall survival about 4 years. (PMID 32759374)
- A meta-analysis found antifibrotic treatment reduces all-cause mortality and the risk of acute exacerbation (pooled RR 0.63), with the acute exacerbation effect consistent for nintedanib but not pirfenidone. (PMID 34217681)
- Antifibrotic therapy protects against FVC decline in progressive pulmonary fibrosis, with similar efficacy for nintedanib and pirfenidone in IPF and non-IPF disease. (PMID 34895203)
- In an IPF registry, DLCO changes were a better predictor of mortality than FVC changes, and age 70 or above, high HRCT interstitial scores and DLCO fall of 15% or more at 12 months were adverse factors. (PMID 28862397)
- Real-world mortality and adverse events with pirfenidone and nintedanib were higher than in previous trials, with pirfenidone patients showing higher mortality. (PMID 38963453)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Real-world safety differences between antifibrotics.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of prognosis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Antifibrotics slow FVC decline and reduce exacerbations.
- DLCO as a prognostic marker.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in prognosis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with IPF and 10% FVC decline over 6 months.
- A complex multimorbid patient with prognosis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: GAP index, transplant referral criteria and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28862397**: *EMPIRE Registry, Czech Part: Impact of demographics, pulmonary function and HRCT on survival and clinical course in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis* (2018) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 32759374**: *Prognosis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis without anti-fibrotic therapy: a systematic review* (2020) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34217681**: *Impact of Antifibrotic Therapy on Mortality and Acute Exacerbation in Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34895203**: *Efficacy of antifibrotic drugs, nintedanib and pirfenidone, in treatment of progressive pulmonary fibrosis in both idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) and non-IPF: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1431] Progressive Supranuclear Palsy (PSP): hummingbird sign, vertical gaze palsy

**Cluster**: psp | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Evidence retrieved for PSP is diagnostic biomarker work: neurofilament light, neutrophil-lymphocyte ratio, transcranial sonography and susceptibility mapping. Dysphagia is common. Clinical diagnostic criteria are not covered.

### Core Teaching Points
- Dysphagia prevalence in PSP was pooled in a systematic review and meta-analysis. (PMID 38523230)
- Neurofilament light levels differ between certain atypical parkinsonian syndromes, with MSA showing the highest levels among Parkinson's disease and related disorders. (PMID 40180649)
- Transcranial sonography may be an important supplementary biomarker for diagnosing PSP, but its value for discriminating PSP from other atypical parkinsonism and between phenotypes needs more exploration. (PMID 37819487)
- In PSP the altered peripheral immune profile is mainly driven by neutrophils, which may inform targeted therapies. (PMID 39162120)
- Quantitative susceptibility mapping was assessed for differentiating Parkinson's disease from atypical parkinsonism, with standardised acquisition recommended. (PMID 42035898)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- No retrieved evidence on treatment.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Progressive Supranuclear Palsy (PSP): hummingbird sign, vertical gaze palsy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Dysphagia is a common PSP feature.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Progressive Supranuclear Palsy (PSP): hummingbird sign, vertical gaze palsy.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Progressive Supranuclear Palsy (PSP): hummingbird sign, vertical gaze palsy.
