## [1432] Prolactinoma dopamine agonist therapy

**Cluster**: pituitary_adenoma | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Prolactinomas are treated first with dopamine agonists, with surgery a reasonable alternative in small, non-invasive tumours. Paediatric and adult consensus guidelines stress specialist-centre management.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Pituitary Society international consensus covers diagnosis and management of prolactin-secreting adenomas and notes needs for additional therapeutic options and international registries. (PMID 37670148)
- Transsphenoidal surgery for prolactinomas may be particularly effective in small, non-invasive, treatment-naive tumours and may be a viable first-line alternative to dopamine agonists in such patients. (PMID 34325023)
- Italian guidelines for prolactinoma management address endocrinologists, neurosurgeons and gynaecologists and include options such as temozolomide for aggressive tumours. (PMID 37171003)
- Consensus guidelines for paediatric pituitary adenomas recommend surgery in specialist centres and collection of outcome data for new modalities. (PMID 38336897, PMID 38336898)
- Cabergoline prevented tumour progression after surgery in about half of non-functioning pituitary adenoma cases with few adverse events, though tumour shrinkage was uncommon. (PMID 35902444)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Duration of dopamine agonist therapy.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Prolactinoma dopamine agonist therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cabergoline as first-line for macroprolactinoma.
- Pituitary stalk effect versus prolactinoma by prolactin level.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Prolactinoma dopamine agonist therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with amenorrhoea, galactorrhoea and prolactin 3,000 mU/L.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Prolactinoma dopamine agonist therapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prolactin thresholds, dose schedules, withdrawal criteria and pregnancy management are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 35902444**: *Treatment of non-functioning pituitary adenoma with cabergoline: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1433] prone positioning severe acute respiratory distress syndrome

**Cluster**: prone_positioning | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Awake proning in COVID-19 hypoxaemic respiratory failure is associated with better survival without intubation, particularly with longer daily duration. In ARDS, prone positioning improved survival on VV-ECMO in a meta-analysis.

### Core Teaching Points
- An individual-participant-data meta-analysis found awake prone positioning in COVID-19 was associated with increased survival without intubation and reduced intubation and mortality, with 10 or more hours per day linked to better outcomes. (PMID 40063016)
- A meta-analysis supports prone positioning in conscious ICU patients with COVID-19 pneumonia to reduce mortality and intubation. (PMID 38245784)
- A rapid practice guideline recommends a trial of awake prone positioning in acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure due to COVID-19, with monitoring by staff able to detect deterioration quickly. (PMID 36691710)
- In ARDS patients receiving VV-ECMO, a meta-analysis found survival improved when prone positioning was used. (PMID 35037993)
- A meta-analysis found prone positioning is most beneficial in ARDS with higher BMI and longer durations. (PMID 35672215)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- ECMO plus proning needs randomised data.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of prone positioning severe acute respiratory distress syndrome.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Duration of prone sessions matters.
- Awake proning versus ventilated proning.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in prone positioning severe acute respiratory distress syndrome.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Hypoxaemic COVID-19 patient on high-flow nasal oxygen.
- A complex multimorbid patient with prone positioning severe acute respiratory distress syndrome presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Standard criteria (PaO2/FiO2 threshold and 16-hour sessions) for ventilated severe ARDS are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 35672215**: *Effectiveness of prone position in acute respiratory distress syndrome and moderating factors of obesity class and treatment durations for COVID-19 patients: A meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1434] prostate cancer

**Cluster**: prostate_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Current guidelines from NCCN, EAU and AUA/ASTRO emphasise shared decision-making across the disease course. In mCRPC, ASCO recommends radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only disease, and sequencing evidence is lacking.

### Core Teaching Points
- NCCN Prostate Cancer version 3.2026 emphasises shared decision-making in all disease settings based on preferences, prior treatment, biomarkers, extent and location of metastases, symptoms and side effects. (PMID 41213253)
- The ASCO mCRPC guideline recommends radium-223 for patients with symptomatic bone-only disease, and notes evidence for optimal sequencing of mCRPC regimens is lacking. (PMID 40315400)
- The AUA/SUO early detection guideline update (2026) revises recommendations on biomarkers and atypical small acinar proliferation. (PMID 41744286)
- The AUA/ASTRO guideline amendment for clinically localised prostate cancer adds shared decision-making emphasis and new direction on radiation and disease management. (PMID 41988960)
- EAU guidelines 2026 note that diagnosis, staging and treatment of localised prostate cancer evolve rapidly, with a second part covering relapsing and metastatic disease. (PMID 42457454, PMID 42457456)
- The French AFU update guides management of recurrent or metastatic prostate cancer according to patient and disease characteristics, prior therapies, genomic status and molecular imaging. (PMID 39581665)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing of systemic therapy in mCRPC.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of prostate cancer.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only mCRPC.
- Shared decision-making in screening and treatment.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in prostate cancer.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Man with rising PSA after radical prostatectomy.
- A complex multimorbid patient with prostate cancer presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: PSA screening thresholds, risk groups and ADT regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1435] prosthetic infections

**Cluster**: pji | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Retrieved evidence centres on PJI prevention and diagnosis: AAOS guidance, morbid obesity as a risk factor, topical vancomycin powder, and histology and sequencing tests. Retention and revision strategy is not covered.

### Core Teaching Points
- An AAOS clinical practice guideline summary addresses prevention of periprosthetic joint infection in total hip and knee arthroplasty, highlighting gaps in the literature. (PMID 40743492)
- Morbidly obese patients have markedly increased risk of PJI and other complications after primary THA, which should be discussed while preserving access to care. (PMID 40198881)
- Topical vancomycin powder appears to reduce PJI risk in total joint arthroplasty, but increased wound complications call for caution. (PMID 40971665)
- Patients with multiple prosthetic joints are at higher risk for metachronous PJI after a single-joint infection. (PMID 40102953)
- Histology differentiating septic from aseptic cases advocates a threshold of at least five PMNs per high-power field in at least five fields. (PMID 41173031)
- Metagenomic and targeted next-generation sequencing show comparable diagnostic accuracy for PJI and are valuable for confirming infection. (PMID 41270974)
- Infection is a leading cause of failure of revision total knee arthroplasty, alongside instability and aseptic loosening. (PMID 40669519)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- High-quality evidence on prevention remains limited.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of prosthetic infections.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Histology PMN threshold.
- Obesity as a PJI risk factor.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in prosthetic infections.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Painful knee replacement 6 months after surgery with raised CRP.
- A complex multimorbid patient with prosthetic infections presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: DAIR versus one-stage versus two-stage revision, antibiotic regimens and MSIS criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40102953**: *Risk factors for metachronous periprosthetic joint infection in patients with multiple prosthetic joints: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1436] Prosthetic joint infection diagnosis and retention strategy

**Cluster**: pji | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Retrieved evidence centres on PJI prevention and diagnosis: AAOS guidance, morbid obesity as a risk factor, topical vancomycin powder, and histology and sequencing tests. Retention and revision strategy is not covered.

### Core Teaching Points
- An AAOS clinical practice guideline summary addresses prevention of periprosthetic joint infection in total hip and knee arthroplasty, highlighting gaps in the literature. (PMID 40743492)
- Morbidly obese patients have markedly increased risk of PJI and other complications after primary THA, which should be discussed while preserving access to care. (PMID 40198881)
- Topical vancomycin powder appears to reduce PJI risk in total joint arthroplasty, but increased wound complications call for caution. (PMID 40971665)
- Patients with multiple prosthetic joints are at higher risk for metachronous PJI after a single-joint infection. (PMID 40102953)
- Histology differentiating septic from aseptic cases advocates a threshold of at least five PMNs per high-power field in at least five fields. (PMID 41173031)
- Metagenomic and targeted next-generation sequencing show comparable diagnostic accuracy for PJI and are valuable for confirming infection. (PMID 41270974)
- Infection is a leading cause of failure of revision total knee arthroplasty, alongside instability and aseptic loosening. (PMID 40669519)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- High-quality evidence on prevention remains limited.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Prosthetic joint infection diagnosis and retention strategy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Histology PMN threshold.
- Obesity as a PJI risk factor.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Prosthetic joint infection diagnosis and retention strategy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Painful knee replacement 6 months after surgery with raised CRP.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Prosthetic joint infection diagnosis and retention strategy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: DAIR versus one-stage versus two-stage revision, antibiotic regimens and MSIS criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1439] Prosthetic valve thrombosis: thrombolysis vs surgery, anticoagulation intensification

**Cluster**: valve_thrombosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Mechanical valve thrombosis management has changed little for decades and guidelines differ. Evidence retrieved on bioprosthetic and subclinical valve thrombosis after TAVI shows stroke risk and uncertain antithrombotic strategy.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review of mechanical valve thrombosis describes current management and differences between guidelines, noting no significant recent changes in management. (PMID 37499958)
- An ACC/AHA versus ESC comparison highlights differences in prosthetic heart valve management in areas of controversy. (PMID 30947924)
- Clinical bioprosthetic valve thrombosis after TAVI is associated with significantly increased stroke risk. (PMID 33065085)
- A meta-analysis of subclinical valve thrombosis after TAVI suggests anticoagulation or antiplatelet therapy may prevent it, but optimal antithrombotic regimen needs further study. (PMID 32217021)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Thrombolysis versus surgery is not covered in the retrieved excerpts.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Prosthetic valve thrombosis: thrombolysis vs surgery, anticoagulation intensification.

- Stroke risk with bioprosthetic valve thrombosis.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Prosthetic valve thrombosis: thrombolysis vs surgery, anticoagulation intensification.

## [1440] Protein-Losing Enteropathy

**Cluster**: ple | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Protein-losing enteropathy retrieved evidence is mostly Fontan-associated: budesonide can raise albumin in Fontan PLE, and paediatric nutrition guidance exists. Other causes of PLE are not covered.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review and meta-analysis found budesonide can increase albumin in patients with protein-losing enteropathy after Fontan operation, though outcome impact needs study. (PMID 31835979)
- Consensus severity-graded definitions of Fontan-associated complications were developed to characterise Fontan circulatory failure. (PMID 38452996)
- A review of long-term outcomes of Fontan patients with an extracardiac conduit found several adverse outcomes needing consensus-based definitions as the population ages. (PMID 39952466)
- A paediatric guideline outlines nutrition management of enteropathy, including dietary education, counselling and monitoring. (PMID 38849288)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Non-Fontan causes of PLE.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Protein-Losing Enteropathy.

## [1441] Proteinuria: Assessment and Clinical Significance

**Cluster**: proteinuria | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Retrieved evidence concerns testing and handling rather than interpretation: albuminuria testing adherence is poor, KDIGO categories track comorbidity burden, and standardised urine collection and handling reduce variability.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review found uACR testing rates in people with diabetes were suboptimal (1.5% to 76.6%, generally below 50%), highlighting poor guideline adherence. (PMID 41087290)
- A systematic review found guideline-recommended albuminuria testing allows timely identification, referral and treatment, whereas late referral led to higher costs and worse outcomes including higher mortality. (PMID 37644841)
- A systematic review by KDIGO categories found comorbidities become more prevalent with increasing albuminuria severity, with the highest burden in high-risk and very-high-risk groups. (PMID 33231861)
- A consensus on pre-analytical urine handling stresses standardised collection and handling to minimise variability, covering timing of collection, repeated measurements and conditions causing false-positive results. (PMID 42029072)
- Stage 1 and stage 2 hypertension (2017 ACC/AHA definitions) were associated with higher incidence of proteinuria than normal blood pressure. (PMID 35063272)
- Exercise training did not aggravate proteinuria in adult CKD patients without renal replacement therapy. (PMID 32393200)
- Elevated liver stiffness in NAFLD is associated with increased odds of kidney outcomes. (PMID 35053253)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Interpretation thresholds and aetiology are not covered.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Proteinuria: Assessment and Clinical Significance.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- uACR for screening in diabetes and hypertension.
- Albuminuria category and CKD risk.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Proteinuria: Assessment and Clinical Significance.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Diabetic patient with persistent albuminuria on repeat uACR.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Proteinuria: Assessment and Clinical Significance presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: uACR versus uPCR thresholds, nephrotic-range definition, orthostatic proteinuria, causes and referral criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32393200**: *Effects of exercise training on proteinuria in adult patients with chronic kidney disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 33231861**: *Burden of Chronic Kidney Disease by KDIGO Categories of Glomerular Filtration Rate and Albuminuria: A Systematic Review* (2021) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 35053253**: *Liver Stiffness, Albuminuria and Chronic Kidney Disease in Patients with NAFLD: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1442] Prothrombin G20210A Mutation

**Cluster**: factor_v_prothrombin | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Thrombophilia testing is variable in practice. Women with prothrombin G20210A mutation should generally not receive thrombosis prophylaxis in pregnancy based on thrombophilia and family history alone, and mild thrombophilia adds only modest risk with combined oral contraceptives.

### Core Teaching Points
- A Bayesian meta-analysis found women with factor V Leiden or prothrombin G20210A mutation should generally not be prescribed thrombosis prophylaxis in pregnancy on the basis of thrombophilia and family history alone. (PMID 29074563)
- Mild thrombophilia confers only a modest additive VTE risk with combined oral contraceptives, which can be offered when no other risk factors exist and alternatives are not tolerated. (PMID 27121914)
- Inherited thrombophilia is associated with increased risk of arterial ischaemic stroke in adults, with management implications needing further study. (PMID 31549567)
- An ISTH survey found thrombophilia testing practice is mostly restricted to single-variant testing, with multigene panels used by a minority of laboratories, and recommendations on when to test are needed. (PMID 41238194)
- An ACMG technical standard (2025 revision) governs laboratory testing for factor V Leiden and factor II c.*97G>A. (PMID 40616445)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Who should be tested.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Prothrombin G20210A Mutation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pregnancy prophylaxis is not indicated by thrombophilia alone.
- COC use in mild thrombophilia.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Prothrombin G20210A Mutation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with heterozygous prothrombin mutation and family history asking about pregnancy prophylaxis.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Prothrombin G20210A Mutation presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Absolute VTE risk figures and homozygous or compound states are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27121914**: *Combined oral contraceptives, thrombophilia and the risk of venous thromboembolism: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2016) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 29074563**: *Pregnancy, thrombophilia, and the risk of a first venous thrombosis: systematic review and bayesian meta-analysis* (2017) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 31549567**: *Inherited Thrombophilia and the Risk of Arterial Ischemic Stroke: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1443] Protocol-based versus goal-directed resuscitation in septic shock

**Cluster**: septic_shock_protocol | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Surviving Sepsis Campaign guidelines are the main evidence framework: the 2016 adult guideline issued 32 strong, 39 weak and 18 best-practice statements. Shorter delays to lactate, cultures, fluids and antibiotics are associated with better outcomes.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2016 Surviving Sepsis Campaign guideline gave 32 strong recommendations, 39 weak recommendations and 18 best-practice statements, with substantial expert agreement. (PMID 28098591)
- The 2021 adult Surviving Sepsis guideline serves as a bedside decision aid for adults with suspected or confirmed septic shock and sepsis-associated organ dysfunction. (PMID 35170043)
- A study of delays within the 3-hour bundle found shorter delays are associated with better outcomes, with significant delay thresholds of about 20 minutes for lactate, 50 minutes for blood cultures, 100 minutes for crystalloids and 125 minutes for antibiotics, and no evidence that 3 hours is safe. (PMID 29298189)
- A review aligned with Surviving Sepsis 2021 suggests preserving glycocalyx integrity through fluid volume changes, minimising catecholamine use, controlling hyperglycaemia and possibly corticosteroids and anticoagulants. (PMID 38658435)
- The 2026 paediatric Surviving Sepsis guidelines include 20 new recommendations and note only three are based on high or moderate certainty evidence. (PMID 41869844)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Protocolised versus usual care trials are not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Protocol-based versus goal-directed resuscitation in septic shock.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Time to antibiotics matters.
- Surviving Sepsis bundle elements.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Protocol-based versus goal-directed resuscitation in septic shock.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Septic shock patient with lactate 5 mmol/L after 30 mL/kg fluids.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Protocol-based versus goal-directed resuscitation in septic shock presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: ProCESS, ARISE and ProMISe trial results (protocolised versus goal-directed resuscitation) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28098591**: *Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock: 2016* (2017) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 29298189**: *Delay Within the 3-Hour Surviving Sepsis Campaign Guideline on Mortality for Patients With Severe Sepsis and Septic Shock* (2018) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 35170043**: *Surviving sepsis campaign: International guidelines for management of sepsis and septic shock in adults 2021 - endorsement by the Scandinavian society of anaesthesiology and intensive care medicine* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38658435**: *Managing sepsis and septic shock in an endothelial glycocalyx-friendly way: from the viewpoint of surviving sepsis campaign guidelines* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41869844**: *Surviving Sepsis Campaign International Guidelines for the Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock in Children 2026* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1444] Pseudohypoparathyroidism: end-organ PTH resistance, Albright hereditary osteodystrophy

**Cluster**: pseudohypoparathyroidism | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The first international consensus on pseudohypoparathyroidism recommends coordinated multidisciplinary care from infancy through adulthood with a transition programme. Genotype-phenotype work links PHP1a variants to round faces and subcutaneous ossifications.

### Core Teaching Points
- The first international consensus statement on pseudohypoparathyroidism and related disorders recommends a coordinated multidisciplinary approach from infancy through adulthood, including a transition programme. (PMID 29959430)
- An updated practical tool for physicians restates that long-term coordinated multidisciplinary care is recommended, starting as early as possible in infancy. (PMID 32756064)
- The paediatric implications of the consensus include insulin resistance, impaired glucose tolerance or type 2 diabetes, hypertension, ectopic ossification and impaired neurocognitive development. (PMID 30591400)
- A systematic review of PHP1a genotype-phenotype found associations between specific variants and round faces and subcutaneous ossifications. (PMID 37837607)
- A systematic review of dental findings in non-surgical hypoparathyroidism and pseudohypoparathyroidism found associations with enamel hypoplasia need larger studies. (PMID 29971010)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term outcomes with treatment.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pseudohypoparathyroidism: end-organ PTH resistance, Albright hereditary osteodystrophy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PTH resistance with high PTH and low calcium.
- Albright hereditary osteodystrophy features.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pseudohypoparathyroidism: end-organ PTH resistance, Albright hereditary osteodystrophy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Child with round face, short stature and hypocalcaemia with raised PTH.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pseudohypoparathyroidism: end-organ PTH resistance, Albright hereditary osteodystrophy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic biochemistry and GNAS imprinting classification are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1445] Pseudomonas aeruginosa eradication in bronchiectasis

**Cluster**: bronchiectasis_pseudomonas | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Eradication therapy for Pseudomonas in bronchiectasis clears sputum in about 40% at 12 months, with higher rates when systemic and inhaled antibiotics are combined. Long-term inhaled antibiotics reduce bacterial density and exacerbation risk but raise wheeze and bronchospasm.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found eradication treatment for P. aeruginosa in bronchiectasis results in eradication from sputum in about 40% at 12 months, with higher rates when combined systemic and inhaled antibiotics are used. (PMID 38296344)
- Long-term inhaled antibiotics in non-CF bronchiectasis reduce sputum bacterial density, increase P. aeruginosa eradication and reduce exacerbation risk, but cause more wheeze and bronchospasm. (PMID 25620629)
- Inhaled tobramycin for chronic Pseudomonas infection in non-CF bronchiectasis raised tolerability concerns, and a network meta-analysis was suggested to compare inhaled antibiotics. (PMID 33307314)
- The ERS clinical practice guideline provides an evidence-based framework for managing adult bronchiectasis. (PMID 41016738)
- A Chinese expert consensus recommends single active antimicrobial therapy with adequate pulmonary concentration for P. aeruginosa lower respiratory infection in patients without significant underlying disease. (PMID 35927044)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which inhaled antibiotic is best.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pseudomonas aeruginosa eradication in bronchiectasis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pseudomonas eradication after first isolation.
- Bronchospasm with inhaled antibiotics.

## [1446] Pseudomyxoma Peritonei and HIPEC

**Cluster**: pseudomyxoma | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Multisociety GRADE consensus supports cytoreductive surgery with HIPEC for pseudomyxoma peritonei, and PSOGI endorses HIPEC after CRS using CDDP/MMC or high-dose MMC regimens.

### Core Teaching Points
- A multisocietal GRADE consensus makes recommendations on cytoreductive surgery and HIPEC for pseudomyxoma peritonei, intended to raise awareness of the benefits in this rare disease. (PMID 40817850)
- The 2022 PSOGI consensus recommends HIPEC following CRS for pseudomyxoma peritonei and endorses CDDP/MMC and high-dose MMC regimens. (PMID 39008204)
- A review of national guidelines found concordance for CRS and HIPEC in pseudomyxoma peritonei and mesothelioma, but none for gastric cancer, and called for high-level evidence. (PMID 40413333)
- The PSOGI-ESGO-ISSPP Lyon consensus aims to standardise cytoreductive surgery techniques in peritoneal malignancy. (PMID 41926971)
- A meta-analysis of CRS plus HIPEC in advanced epithelial ovarian cancer after neoadjuvant chemotherapy found improved progression-free survival, with acute kidney failure in 10.6% receiving HIPEC. (PMID 39904852)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal HIPEC regimen.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pseudomyxoma Peritonei and HIPEC.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- CRS plus HIPEC as treatment of pseudomyxoma peritonei.
- Renal toxicity with cisplatin HIPEC.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pseudomyxoma Peritonei and HIPEC.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with jelly belly and appendiceal mucinous neoplasm.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pseudomyxoma Peritonei and HIPEC presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Peritoneal Cancer Index, completeness of cytoreduction and survival figures are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39008204**: *The 2022 PSOGI International Consensus on HIPEC Regimens for Peritoneal Malignancies: Pseudomyxoma Peritonei* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39904852**: *Cytoreductive Surgery Plus HIPEC in Recurrent or Newly Diagnosed Advanced Epithelial Ovarian Cancer: a Meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40413333**: *National Guidelines for Cytoreductive Surgery and Hyperthermic Intraperitoneal Chemotherapy (HIPEC) in Peritoneal Malignancies: A Worldwide Systematic Review and Recommendations of Strength Analysis* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40817850**: *Multisocietal Consensus on the use of Cytoreductive Surgery and HIPEC for the Treatment of Pseudomyxoma Peritonei: A GRADE Approach for Evidence Evaluation and Recommendation* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41926971**: *Principles of cytoreductive surgery for primary and metastatic peritoneal malignancies-the PSOGI-ESGO-ISSPP Lyon consensus* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1447] psoriasis

**Cluster**: psoriasis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Moderate-to-severe psoriasis is treated with biologics, and IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors show long drug survival. Routine latent TB testing is not required before IL-17 or IL-23 inhibitors, and dose reduction and switching strategies are emerging.

### Core Teaching Points
- A joint NPF and IPC position statement reached consensus that routine testing for latent TB infection is not required in psoriasis patients treated with IL-17 or IL-23 inhibitors. (PMID 41260526)
- IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors show drug survival that depends on effect modifiers such as biologic naivety and data source. (PMID 38630365)
- A meta-analysis on switching biologics found adalimumab, ixekizumab and risankizumab preferred for intraclass switching and guselkumab the primary agent for interclass transitions. (PMID 40545752)
- The first international consensus on dose reduction of biologics for psoriasis judged dose reduction feasible, though underlying evidence is still limited. (PMID 42012299)
- European-adapted S3 and French guidelines (2025-2026) give treatment recommendations, monitoring and advice for comorbid situations, pregnancy planning and special types of psoriasis. (PMID 41531416, PMID 41641952, PMID 41469898)
- A network meta-analysis of biologics in paediatric plaque psoriasis found trends towards improved CDLQI and PASI75 responses and called for better-powered trials. (PMID 41090545)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal dose-reduction criteria.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of psoriasis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PASI and DLQI for severity.
- Choosing biologic in psoriatic arthritis versus plaque disease.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in psoriasis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with PASI 15 failing methotrexate.
- A complex multimorbid patient with psoriasis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic features, topical therapy, severity thresholds and conventional systemic agents are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38630365**: *Drug Survival of IL-17 and IL-23 Inhibitors for Psoriasis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40545752**: *Switching biologic agent in patients with psoriasis: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41090545**: *Biologics for treatment of paediatric plaque psoriasis: A systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41260526**: *Joint position statement from the National Psoriasis Foundation Medical Board and the International Psoriasis Council on routine testing for latent tuberculosis infection prior to and during treatment of psoriasis patients with interleukin 17 or interleukin 23 inhibitors* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41469898**: *French guidelines on systemic treatments for moderate-to-severe psoriasis in adults: Update 2025* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41531416**: *S3 Guideline for the treatment of psoriasis vulgaris, adapted from EuroGuiDerm - part 1: Treatment recommendations and monitoring* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41641952**: *S3 Guideline for the Treatment of Psoriasis vulgaris, adapted from EuroGuiDerm - part 2: Specific clinical and comorbid situations* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42012299**: *International consensus on dose reduction of biologics for patients with psoriasis: The DR. Delphi study* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1449] psoriatic arthritis

**Cluster**: psoriatic_arthritis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
EULAR 2023 recommendations integrate current drugs in a progressive approach to PsA, and EULAR and GRAPPA now define difficult-to-manage and treatment-refractory PsA. IL-17, IL-12/23 and IL-23 inhibitors show favourable efficacy and safety.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2023 EULAR recommendations integrate all currently available drugs in a practical and progressive approach to pharmacological management of PsA, addressing remission. (PMID 38499325)
- EULAR and GRAPPA propose consensus definitions for difficult-to-manage and treatment-refractory PsA, which need prospective validation. (PMID 41168056, PMID 41310208)
- A network meta-analysis of IL-17, IL-12/23 and IL-23 inhibitors for PsA found a favourable balance of relatively high efficacy and low risk. (PMID 41050680)
- The APLAR recommendations for PsA were developed from available evidence and region-specific considerations by rheumatologists, dermatologists and patients. (PMID 40725984)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing of biologics.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of psoriatic arthritis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Definition of difficult-to-manage PsA.
- IL-17 and IL-23 inhibitors.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in psoriatic arthritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with plaque psoriasis, dactylitis and failed methotrexate.
- A complex multimorbid patient with psoriatic arthritis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: CASPAR criteria and phenotype-based drug selection are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38499325**: *EULAR recommendations for the management of psoriatic arthritis with pharmacological therapies: 2023 update* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40725984**: *The APLAR Recommendations for the Management of Psoriatic Arthritis* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41050680**: *Efficacy and safety of IL-17, IL-12/23, and IL-23 inhibitors for psoriatic arthritis: a network meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41168056**: *EULAR points to consider and consensus definitions for difficult-to-manage and treatment-refractory psoriatic arthritis* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41310208**: *Consensus definitions of complex-to-manage and treatment-refractory psoriatic arthritis: a GRAPPA initiative* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1453] Psychogenic non-epileptic seizures (PNES): video-EEG differentiation, management

**Cluster**: pnes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The AAN functional seizures guideline covers management. Psychological treatment improves non-seizure outcomes beyond seizure frequency, and psychiatric comorbidities are common.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AAN guideline executive summary on management of functional seizures identifies evidence gaps and avenues for future research, including medications in people without another indication. (PMID 41370742)
- A meta-analysis found psychological treatment of functional seizures is associated with clinical improvements across a broad array of non-seizure outcomes beyond seizure frequency. (PMID 37157188)
- A systematic review linked early trauma, abuse and neglect to a combination of psychiatric and functional conditions including PTSD, BPD, FND and PNES. (PMID 39619491)
- Cognitive performance in functional seizures is slightly less severely impaired than in many patients with epilepsy but still argues for clinical assessment. (PMID 38879275)
- A systematic review suggests PNES is more common than previously thought. (PMID 34126844)
- Paediatric PNES recommendations advise screening for mental health and neurodevelopmental difficulties. (PMID 37804168)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best psychological modality.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Psychogenic non-epileptic seizures (PNES): video-EEG differentiation, management.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Psychotherapy as the mainstay of PNES treatment.
- PTSD in epilepsy and PNES.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Psychogenic non-epileptic seizures (PNES): video-EEG differentiation, management.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young woman with seizures resistant to antiseizure medication and normal EEG.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Psychogenic non-epileptic seizures (PNES): video-EEG differentiation, management presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Video-EEG diagnosis and communicating the diagnosis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34126844**: *Incidence and prevalence of psychogenic nonepileptic seizures (functional seizures): a systematic review and an analytical study* (2023) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 37157188**: *A meta-analytic review of the effectiveness of psychological treatment of functional/dissociative seizures on non-seizure outcomes in adults* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37804168**: *Scoping review and expert-based consensus recommendations for assessment and management of psychogenic non-epileptic (functional) seizures (PNES) in children: A report from the Pediatric Psychiatric Issues Task Force of the International League Against Epilepsy* (2023) [guideline] - None

## [1454] psychosis

**Cluster**: psychosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
A trial of clozapine is supported after two other antipsychotics fail. Cognitive deficits are common in first-episode psychosis, and sex-sensitive prescribing guidance exists for females.

### Core Teaching Points
- Analysis of sequential antipsychotic trials found response falls at later stages and supports a clozapine trial after two other antipsychotics have been ineffective. (PMID 38493023)
- ACKR1/Duffy-null genotype testing for clozapine was proposed as cost-effective for facilitating clozapine access in treatment-resistant schizophrenia. (PMID 42125771)
- Antipsychotic-naive first-episode psychosis shows cognitive deficits with high variability, suggesting a role for cognitive remediation in more severely affected individuals. (PMID 38416480)
- A clinical practice guideline addresses choice of first antipsychotic for females with first-episode psychosis, supporting safer, sex-sensitive prescribing. (PMID 41793755)
- Psychiatrists' treatment preferences for schizophrenia diverge from guidelines in places, highlighting the need to integrate evidence and clinical consensus. (PMID 40745910)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Acute behavioural disturbance management is not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of psychosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Clozapine for treatment-resistant schizophrenia.
- Benign ethnic neutropenia and clozapine.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in psychosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient failed two antipsychotics at adequate dose.
- A complex multimorbid patient with psychosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Rapid tranquillisation, risk assessment and first-line drug choice for acute psychosis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38416480**: *Cognitive Function and Variability in Antipsychotic Drug-Naive Patients With First-Episode Psychosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38493023**: *Response rates to sequential trials of antipsychotic medications according to algorithms or treatment guidelines in psychotic disorders. A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40745910**: *Schizophrenia treatment preferences of psychiatrists versus guidelines: A European perspective* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41793755**: *Clinical Practice Guideline on the Choice of First Antipsychotic Medicine for Females Experiencing a First-Episode of Psychosis* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42125771**: *ACKR1/Duffy-null genotype testing for clozapine: A guideline developed by the UK Centre of Excellence in Regulatory Science and Innovation in Pharmacogenomics (CERSI-PGx)* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1456] ptsd trauma focused psychotherapy and ssri

**Cluster**: ptsd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Trauma-focused psychotherapy is the core PTSD treatment: EMDR performs comparably to TF-CBT. Guideline evidence for complex presentations is of low certainty, and NICE-derived advice favours psychotherapy when insomnia is present.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found EMDR was significantly better than waitlist or usual care, with no significant difference in treatment effect between EMDR and TF-CBT. (PMID 40616777)
- A guideline with five meta-analyses on psychotherapies for adults with complex PTSD rated the certainty of evidence as low, including for targeting dissociation, and called for high-quality trials. (PMID 40234083)
- A review of NICE-derived interventions advises psychotherapy when insomnia symptoms are present, with psychotherapy particularly recommended for females and pharmacotherapy emphasised for males. (PMID 40665297)
- The updated German S3 guideline sets trauma-informed care as a standard approach in health institutions and covers exercise and physical activity. (PMID 42593701)
- A network meta-analysis suggests yoga with meditation and mindfulness may help adolescents with PTSD, but evidence is limited. (PMID 40001042)
- An Australian guideline development process is addressing MDMA-assisted psychotherapy for PTSD. (PMID 40683535)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of pharmacotherapy versus psychotherapy.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of ptsd trauma focused psychotherapy and ssri.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Trauma-focused CBT and EMDR as first-line.
- Complex PTSD and dissociation.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in ptsd trauma focused psychotherapy and ssri.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Combat veteran with nightmares and avoidance.
- A complex multimorbid patient with ptsd trauma focused psychotherapy and ssri presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: SSRI/venlafaxine evidence and diagnostic criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40001042**: *Mind-body intervention for post-traumatic stress disorder in adolescents: a systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40234083**: *Psychotherapies for adults with complex presentations of PTSD: a clinical guideline and five systematic reviews with meta-analyses* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40616777**: *Clinical and cost-effectiveness of eye movement desensitization and reprocessing for treatment and prevention of post-traumatic stress disorder in adults: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40665297**: *Psychotherapeutic, pharmacological and other active interventions from the NICE guideline for post-traumatic stress disorder with sleep disorder: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40683535**: *Development of an Australian Clinical Practice Guideline on methylenedioxymethamphetamine (MDMA)-assisted Psychotherapy for Post-traumatic Stress Disorder* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 42593701**: *[Treatment of posttraumatic stress disorder-The updated S3 guidelines]* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1458] Pulmonary Alveolar Proteinosis

**Cluster**: pap | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
ERS guidelines cover diagnosis and management of pulmonary alveolar proteinosis: GM-CSF antibody testing identifies the autoimmune form, whole lung lavage is standard, and inhaled GM-CSF is an emerging alternative.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ERS guideline states diagnosing the cause requires GM-CSF antibody testing or genetic analysis, and lists whole lung lavage and GM-CSF augmentation as therapies with limited supporting evidence. (PMID 39147411)
- A case-based summary for clinicians advises referral to expert centres for diagnostic and therapeutic guidance in PAP. (PMID 40365091)
- A systematic review of nebulised GM-CSF in autoimmune PAP calls for comparative effectiveness data against whole lung lavage, the current standard of care. (PMID 37993127)
- A Chinese consensus statement provides practice guidance for inhaled recombinant human GM-CSF in autoimmune PAP. (PMID 36097923)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative effectiveness of lavage versus inhaled GM-CSF.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary Alveolar Proteinosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Anti-GM-CSF antibodies in autoimmune PAP.
- Whole lung lavage as standard treatment.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pulmonary Alveolar Proteinosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with progressive dyspnoea and crazy-paving pattern on CT.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary Alveolar Proteinosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Imaging, BAL appearance and lavage indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36097923**: *[Consensus statement on inhaled recombinant human granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor for autoimmune pulmonary alveolar proteinosis (2022)]* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 37993127**: *Nebulised granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor (GM-CSF) in autoimmune pulmonary alveolar proteinosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1459] Pulmonary arterial hypertension in connective tissue disease: screening, ERA, PDE5 inhibitors

**Cluster**: pah | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Sotatercept improves haemodynamics and function in PAH and lowers all-cause mortality and right ventricular failure in one meta-analysis, with mild thrombocytopenia. Guidance exists for intermediate-risk patients and portopulmonary hypertension.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found sotatercept improves pulmonary haemodynamics in PAH while reducing all-cause mortality and right ventricular failure, with a mild increase in thrombocytopenia risk. (PMID 40668414)
- A second meta-analysis found sotatercept improves functional and haemodynamic outcomes with a favourable safety profile, though mortality benefit remains uncertain. (PMID 40602626)
- A Belgian consensus places subcutaneous sotatercept in the future PAH treatment strategy and notes effects on platelet counts and haemoglobin. (PMID 39377139)
- An expert consensus identifies evidence gaps for intermediate-risk prevalent PAH patients, including switching within drug classes, adding a prostacyclin-pathway drug, and switching PDE5i to riociguat. (PMID 39265796)
- The International Liver Transplantation Society updated its practice guideline on portopulmonary hypertension from expert opinion and available evidence. (PMID 40094355)
- A thoracic society statement addresses interventional strategies for children with progressive pulmonary hypertension despite optimal therapy. (PMID 39531626)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing of sotatercept with combination therapy.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary arterial hypertension in connective tissue disease: screening, ERA, PDE5 inhibitors.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Sotatercept targets activin signalling (not in abstracts).
- Thrombocytopenia as adverse effect.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pulmonary arterial hypertension in connective tissue disease: screening, ERA, PDE5 inhibitors.

### Case Generation Hooks
- PAH patient at intermediate risk on dual oral therapy.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary arterial hypertension in connective tissue disease: screening, ERA, PDE5 inhibitors presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, risk scoring and CTD-PAH specifics are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39265796**: *Gaps in evidence in the treatment of prevalent patients with pulmonary arterial hypertension at intermediate risk: An expert consensus* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39377139**: *A Belgian consensus on sotatercept for the treatment of pulmonary arterial hypertension* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39531626**: *Interventional Strategies for Children with Progressive Pulmonary Hypertension Despite Optimal Therapy: An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40094355**: *International Liver Transplantation Society practice guideline update on portopulmonary hypertension* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40602626**: *Clinical outcomes and safety of sotatercept in pulmonary arterial hypertension: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40668414**: *Efficacy and safety of sotatercept in pulmonary arterial hypertension: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials with trial sequential analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1461] Pulmonary Arteriovenous Malformations

**Cluster**: pavm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PAVMs usually arise in hereditary haemorrhagic telangiectasia, so HHT guidelines are the main source. Retrieved material covers HHT epistaxis and bleeding definitions; PAVM embolisation specifics are not retrieved.

### Core Teaching Points
- The second international HHT guidelines update provide guidance including new areas of anaemia, paediatrics, and pregnancy and delivery. (PMID 32894695)
- An international consensus standardises terminology, definitions and outcome criteria for bleeding in HHT. (PMID 40662351)
- A network meta-analysis found tranexamic acid, tamoxifen and estriol were effective for epistaxis in HHT, with adverse events of tranexamic acid, tacrolimus, propranolol and estradiol to be considered. (PMID 37995018)
- A consensus addresses sclerotherapy for HHT-related epistaxis. (PMID 40773189)
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria include a 2023 update on pulmonary arteriovenous malformation. (PMID 38823949)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- PAVM-specific management not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary Arteriovenous Malformations.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles

- Epistaxis treatment in HHT.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pulmonary Arteriovenous Malformations.

## [1462] Pulmonary artery catheter utility in critically ill patients

**Cluster**: pa_catheter | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In cardiogenic shock, pulmonary artery catheter use is associated with lower mortality in observational pooled analyses, but randomised data are lacking. Echocardiography and thermodilution cardiac output measurements are broadly comparable.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of PAC monitoring in cardiogenic shock found a lower incidence of short-term mortality in adjusted observational studies, with prospective studies needed to confirm. (PMID 35344825)
- A further meta-analysis found PAC use in cardiogenic shock may be associated with decreased mortality and supports a randomised trial. (PMID 37100652)
- Right ventricular injury in ARDS was associated with increased overall and short-term mortality. (PMID 34020703)
- Echocardiographic and thermodilution cardiac output measurement showed no significant overall difference, and the techniques may be interchangeable under certain conditions. (PMID 31581196)
- A meta-analysis of cardiac output monitors in septic shock noted that real-time decision parameters were rarely assessed. (PMID 40652247)
- Passive leg raising predicts fluid responsiveness with good specificity but poor sensitivity when assessed by changes in pulse pressure. (PMID 26825952)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Randomised benefit of PAC.
- PAC hemodynamic guidance versus echocardiography and dynamic stroke volume variation (Vigileo/PiCCO) in complex cardiorenal and RV failure.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PAC in cardiogenic shock.
- Fluid responsiveness tests.
- Hemodynamic differentiation of shock states: distinguishing cardiogenic (high PCWP, low CI, high SVR) from septic/distributive shock (low PCWP, high CI, low SVR).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient in cardiogenic shock with uncertain filling pressures on inotropes.
- 65-year-old male with acute anterior STEMI complicated by acute mitral regurgitation, pulmonary oedema, and mixed shock requiring PAC-guided inotropic and vasopressor titration.

**Reviewer Gap**: Historical RCTs (ESCAPE, PAC-Man) and waveform interpretation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 26825952**: *Passive leg raising for predicting fluid responsiveness: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2016) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 31581196**: *Cardiac output measurements via echocardiography versus thermodilution: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2019) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34020703**: *The impact of right ventricular injury on the mortality in patients with acute respiratory distress syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35344825**: *Prognostic implications of pulmonary artery catheter monitoring in patients with cardiogenic shock: A systematic review and meta-analysis of observational studies* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37100652**: *Clinical impact of pulmonary artery catheter in patients with cardiogenic shock: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40652247**: *Cardiac output monitors in septic shock: do they deliver what matters? A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1463] pulmonary embolism anticoagulation duration

**Cluster**: pe | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
A 2026 multisociety AHA/ACC guideline covers evaluation and management of acute PE in adults. Troponin predicts short-term mortality and deterioration, and catheter-directed treatment options are addressed by several position statements.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 AHA/ACC multisociety guideline addresses diagnosis and management of acute PE including pharmacological therapy, advanced interventional therapies and in-hospital support. (PMID 41712677)
- Troponin is a strong and consistent predictor of short-term mortality and clinical deterioration in acute PE. (PMID 41121686)
- An ISTH review of risk stratification found substantial evidence gaps persist, using a PE severity index combined with clinical assessment of stability. (PMID 41354154)
- A meta-analysis of catheter-directed thrombectomy without thrombolysis in acute PE supports lowering the threshold to consider it, with randomised trials ongoing in intermediate and high-risk PE. (PMID 39515614)
- ESVM 2025 guidelines give recommendations on indications and management of catheter-based therapy in acute VTE. (PMID 40587333)
- A Spanish position statement on catheter-directed interventions in PE describes periprocedural support and a multidisciplinary approach, noting uncertainty from insufficient evidence. (PMID 39566820)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which intermediate-risk patients benefit from catheter therapy.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of pulmonary embolism anticoagulation duration.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Troponin in risk stratification.
- Catheter-directed therapy in intermediate-high-risk PE.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in pulmonary embolism anticoagulation duration.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Normotensive PE with RV strain and raised troponin.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pulmonary embolism anticoagulation duration presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: YEARS and D-dimer diagnostic pathways, anticoagulation duration and thrombolysis dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39515614**: *Safety and efficacy of catheter-directed thrombectomy without thrombolysis in acute pulmonary embolism: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39566820**: *Catheter-directed interventions in acute pulmonary embolism. Position statement of SEC-Interventional Cardiology Association/SEC-Ischemic Heart Disease and Acute Cardiovascular Care Association/SEC-Working Group on Pulmonary Hypertension* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40587333**: *2025 ESVM Guidelines on interventional treatment of venous thromboembolism* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41121686**: *The Prognostic Value of Troponin in Acute Pulmonary Embolism: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 41354154**: *Evidence-based risk stratification of patients with acute pulmonary embolism: communication from the ISTH SSC Subcommittee on Predictive and Diagnostic Variables in Thrombotic Disease* (2026) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41712677**: *2026 AHA/ACC/ACCP/ACEP/CHEST/SCAI/SHM/SIR/SVM/SVN Guideline for the Evaluation and Management of Acute Pulmonary Embolism in Adults: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines* (2026) [guideline] - None

## [1466] pulmonary embolism risk stratification

**Cluster**: pe | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
A 2026 multisociety AHA/ACC guideline covers evaluation and management of acute PE in adults. Troponin predicts short-term mortality and deterioration, and catheter-directed treatment options are addressed by several position statements.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 AHA/ACC multisociety guideline addresses diagnosis and management of acute PE including pharmacological therapy, advanced interventional therapies and in-hospital support. (PMID 41712677)
- Troponin is a strong and consistent predictor of short-term mortality and clinical deterioration in acute PE. (PMID 41121686)
- An ISTH review of risk stratification found substantial evidence gaps persist, using a PE severity index combined with clinical assessment of stability. (PMID 41354154)
- A meta-analysis of catheter-directed thrombectomy without thrombolysis in acute PE supports lowering the threshold to consider it, with randomised trials ongoing in intermediate and high-risk PE. (PMID 39515614)
- ESVM 2025 guidelines give recommendations on indications and management of catheter-based therapy in acute VTE. (PMID 40587333)
- A Spanish position statement on catheter-directed interventions in PE describes periprocedural support and a multidisciplinary approach, noting uncertainty from insufficient evidence. (PMID 39566820)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which intermediate-risk patients benefit from catheter therapy.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of pulmonary embolism risk stratification.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Troponin in risk stratification.
- Catheter-directed therapy in intermediate-high-risk PE.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in pulmonary embolism risk stratification.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Normotensive PE with RV strain and raised troponin.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pulmonary embolism risk stratification presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: YEARS and D-dimer diagnostic pathways, anticoagulation duration and thrombolysis dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39515614**: *Safety and efficacy of catheter-directed thrombectomy without thrombolysis in acute pulmonary embolism: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39566820**: *Catheter-directed interventions in acute pulmonary embolism. Position statement of SEC-Interventional Cardiology Association/SEC-Ischemic Heart Disease and Acute Cardiovascular Care Association/SEC-Working Group on Pulmonary Hypertension* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40587333**: *2025 ESVM Guidelines on interventional treatment of venous thromboembolism* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1469] Pulmonary hypertension crisis in the ICU: triggers, inhaled nitric oxide, prostacyclins

**Cluster**: ph_crisis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Retrieved evidence addresses inhaled nitric oxide in neonates and cardiac surgery and the framework for acute cor pulmonale. Evidence in adult ICU crisis is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- A framework for management of right ventricular failure secondary to acutely increased afterload (acute cor pulmonale) is needed across disciplines, as it has a major impact on prognosis. (PMID 38135288)
- A network meta-analysis of persistent pulmonary hypertension of the newborn found the most effective treatment was MNO + OSID, with OSID + milrinone preferred if iNO is not available. (PMID 38363176)
- In preterm infants receiving rescue iNO, immediate improvement in oxygenation was associated with reduced odds of death before discharge. (PMID 37542786)
- A Bayesian network meta-analysis of pulmonary vasodilators and inodilators in cardiac surgery found no clear mortality improvement, limited by few events and imprecision. (PMID 37652847)
- A meta-analysis of inhaled nitric oxide with cardiopulmonary bypass calls for large multicentre trials of dose and duration. (PMID 38556145)
- A consensus statement describes echocardiography as the gold standard for noninvasive haemodynamic assessment in ICU patients. (PMID 40463753)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Adult PH crisis management.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary hypertension crisis in the ICU: triggers, inhaled nitric oxide, prostacyclins.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Echocardiography in RV failure.
- iNO in PPHN.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pulmonary hypertension crisis in the ICU: triggers, inhaled nitric oxide, prostacyclins.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Post-cardiac-surgery patient with acute RV failure and rising PA pressures.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary hypertension crisis in the ICU: triggers, inhaled nitric oxide, prostacyclins presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Triggers, inhaled vasodilator choice and RV support in adult ICU crisis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37542786**: *Association between immediate oxygenation response and survival in preterm infants receiving rescue inhaled nitric oxide therapy for hypoxemia from pulmonary hypertension: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37652847**: *Pulmonary Vasodilator and Inodilator Drugs in Cardiac Surgery: A Systematic Review With Bayesian Network Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38135288**: *Diagnosis and treatment of right ventricular failure secondary to acutely increased right ventricular afterload (acute cor pulmonale): a clinical consensus statement of the Association for Acute CardioVascular Care of the European Society of Cardiology* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38363176**: *Comparison of Different Treatments of Persistent Pulmonary Hypertension of the Newborn: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 38556145**: *Inhaled nitric oxide and postoperative outcomes in cardiac surgery with cardiopulmonary bypass: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1470] Pulmonary hypertension: WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, targeted therapy

**Cluster**: pah | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Sotatercept improves haemodynamics and function in PAH and lowers all-cause mortality and right ventricular failure in one meta-analysis, with mild thrombocytopenia. Guidance exists for intermediate-risk patients and portopulmonary hypertension.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found sotatercept improves pulmonary haemodynamics in PAH while reducing all-cause mortality and right ventricular failure, with a mild increase in thrombocytopenia risk. (PMID 40668414)
- A second meta-analysis found sotatercept improves functional and haemodynamic outcomes with a favourable safety profile, though mortality benefit remains uncertain. (PMID 40602626)
- A Belgian consensus places subcutaneous sotatercept in the future PAH treatment strategy and notes effects on platelet counts and haemoglobin. (PMID 39377139)
- An expert consensus identifies evidence gaps for intermediate-risk prevalent PAH patients, including switching within drug classes, adding a prostacyclin-pathway drug, and switching PDE5i to riociguat. (PMID 39265796)
- The International Liver Transplantation Society updated its practice guideline on portopulmonary hypertension from expert opinion and available evidence. (PMID 40094355)
- A thoracic society statement addresses interventional strategies for children with progressive pulmonary hypertension despite optimal therapy. (PMID 39531626)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing of sotatercept with combination therapy.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary hypertension: WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, targeted therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Sotatercept targets activin signalling (not in abstracts).
- Thrombocytopenia as adverse effect.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pulmonary hypertension: WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, targeted therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- PAH patient at intermediate risk on dual oral therapy.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary hypertension: WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, targeted therapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: WHO classification, haemodynamic definitions, risk scoring and CTD-PAH specifics are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39265796**: *Gaps in evidence in the treatment of prevalent patients with pulmonary arterial hypertension at intermediate risk: An expert consensus* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39377139**: *A Belgian consensus on sotatercept for the treatment of pulmonary arterial hypertension* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39531626**: *Interventional Strategies for Children with Progressive Pulmonary Hypertension Despite Optimal Therapy: An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1471] Pulmonary Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis

**Cluster**: plch | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PLCH is strongly smoking-related, with pulmonary hypertension frequent and cannabis use reported as a trigger. Sotatercept shows encouraging but limited data for PLCH-associated pulmonary hypertension.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found substantial prevalence of pulmonary hypertension in PLCH, with variability by diagnostic modality, and recommends early right heart catheterisation screening. (PMID 40895983)
- A review of PLCH advises referring progressive disease to specialist centres and considering pharmacotherapy trials or transplant evaluation. (PMID 28689173)
- A case series and systematic review of sotatercept in PLCH-associated pulmonary hypertension reports encouraging effects limited by small sample size and retrospective design. (PMID 41613828)
- A case report and systematic review links PLCH to marijuana use. (PMID 39871311)
- A review notes smoking-related interstitial lung disease is increasingly found incidentally on thoracic CT. (PMID 29222007)
- Paired inspiratory and expiratory CT may help differentiate cystic lung diseases. (PMID 39625935)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Treatment beyond smoking cessation.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Smoking cessation is first-line.
- Cystic lung disease differential.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pulmonary Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young smoker with nodules and cysts on CT and pneumothorax.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: CT pattern evolution, BRAF involvement and cessation outcomes are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28689173**: *Current understanding and management of pulmonary Langerhans cell histiocytosis* (2017) [review] - None
- **PMID 29222007**: *Current Concepts in Pathogenesis, Diagnosis, and Management of Smoking-Related Interstitial Lung Diseases* (2018) [review] - None
- **PMID 39625935**: *Unlocking the clinical potential of paired inspiratory and expiratory CT scans in the differential diagnosis of cystic lung diseases: A systematic review* (2024) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 39871311**: *Pulmonary langerhans cell histiocytosis secondary to Marijuana use: a case report and systematic review of the literature* (2025) [systematic review] - None

## [1472] pulmonary nodule evaluation and fleischner follow up

**Cluster**: nodules | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fleischner 2017 guidance sets follow-up of incidental pulmonary nodules by size, and BTS guidance relies on volumetric analysis. Malignancy risk scoring and patient-centred discussion support decisions.

### Core Teaching Points
- The updated Fleischner Society guidelines use nodule size to make management recommendations, illustrated through eight challenging scenarios. (PMID 30207935)
- A clinical practice guideline addresses malignancy risk stratification for solitary pulmonary nodules, advising clinicians to choose the scheme that suits their situation. (PMID 35775869)
- The 2015 BTS guidelines require routine volumetric image-analysis software so that radiologists can manage nodules correctly. (PMID 26781558)
- A patient-centred, guideline-concordant discussion approach to pulmonary nodules is illustrated with pulmonologist and thoracic surgeon perspectives. (PMID 32081651)
- Paediatric pulmonary nodules have a separate imaging algorithm. (PMID 34836566)
- A Chinese consensus guides AI-assisted management of pulmonary nodules. (PMID 35340198)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of AI in nodule management.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of pulmonary nodule evaluation and fleischner follow up.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Fleischner size-based follow-up.
- Volumetric measurement.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in pulmonary nodule evaluation and fleischner follow up.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Incidental 7 mm solid nodule in a smoker.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pulmonary nodule evaluation and fleischner follow up presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific Fleischner size thresholds, intervals and PET/biopsy triggers are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 26781558**: *Return of the pulmonary nodule: the radiologist's key role in implementing the 2015 BTS guidelines on the investigation and management of pulmonary nodules* (2016) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 30207935**: *Updated Fleischner Society Guidelines for Managing Incidental Pulmonary Nodules: Common Questions and Challenging Scenarios* (2018) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 32081651**: *Patient-Centered, Guideline-Concordant Discussion and Management of Pulmonary Nodules* (2020) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 34836566**: *Pediatric Pulmonary Nodules: Imaging Guidelines and Recommendations* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 35340198**: *[Chinese Experts Consensus on Artificial Intelligence Assisted Management for  Pulmonary Nodule (2022 Version)]* (2022) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 35775869**: *Malignancy risk stratification for solitary pulmonary nodule: A clinical practice guideline* (2022) [guideline] - None
