## [1474] Pulmonary Rehabilitation

**Cluster**: pulm_rehab | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pulmonary rehabilitation improves quality of life in interstitial lung disease, with benefit mainly in physical domains. Remote and hybrid delivery can widen access.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of pulmonary rehabilitation in interstitial lung disease found improved health-related quality of life, though not the social function domain, and optimal duration and type-specific effects remain to be determined. (PMID 40178411)
- A consensus statement recommends cardiac and pulmonary rehabilitation for all eligible, willing patients, with virtual, remote and hybrid models as alternatives to widen participation. (PMID 41171981)
- Japanese guidelines for preoperative pulmonary assessment in lung cancer cover pulmonary rehabilitation and smoking cessation. (PMID 39969667)
- A TSANZ guideline notes home oxygen therapy requires attention to delivery devices, expectations, limitations and hazards. (PMID 39009413)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Rehabilitation in COPD is not covered in the retrieved set.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pulmonary rehabilitation improves exercise capacity and quality of life.
- Home oxygen safety.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with fibrotic ILD and exertional breathlessness.

**Reviewer Gap**: COPD evidence, programme content and exacerbation-related admissions are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39009413**: *Thoracic Society of Australia and New Zealand clinical practice guideline on adult home oxygen therapy* (2024) [guideline] - None

## [1475] Pulmonary sarcoidosis corticosteroid therapy

**Cluster**: sarcoidosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Sarcoidosis relapse is common and defined inconsistently. Biologics and targeted therapies have been reviewed, FDG-PET/CT reductions track functional improvement, and steroid-sparing agents are needed for hypercalcaemia.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of FDG-PET/CT in pulmonary sarcoidosis found reduction in SUVmax correlated with improved forced vital capacity, DLCO and subjective health scores. (PMID 39044038)
- A meta-analysis of biologic and targeted synthetic therapies in sarcoidosis reports efficacy signals (tofacitinib in cutaneous disease, efzofitimod in pulmonary disease), limited by small sample sizes and heterogeneity. (PMID 40393718)
- A meta-analysis of relapse in pulmonary sarcoidosis highlights inconsistent definitions and observational designs, with clinical assessment appearing comparable to complex definitions. (PMID 42144146)
- In sarcoidosis-related hypercalcaemia, response to corticosteroids and alternatives was examined, and the most effective steroid-sparing therapy remains to be determined. (PMID 40699493)
- An expert consensus on systemic autoimmune disease-associated ILD includes sarcoidosis among the diseases covered. (PMID 40582403)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal steroid dose and duration.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary sarcoidosis corticosteroid therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PET-CT for activity.
- Hypercalcaemia in sarcoidosis.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pulmonary sarcoidosis corticosteroid therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy and persisting cough on steroids.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary sarcoidosis corticosteroid therapy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Corticosteroid dosing, indications to treat and diagnosis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39044038**: *Meta-analysis of [<sup>18</sup>F]FDG-PET/CT in pulmonary sarcoidosis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 40393718**: *Systematic review and meta-analysis of the efficacy of biologic and targeted synthetic therapies in sarcoidosis* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1476] Pulmonary Tuberculosis

**Cluster**: tb | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra detects pulmonary TB with high sensitivity and specificity and rifampicin resistance. Truenat MTB Plus performs well, and survivors of pulmonary TB have mixed obstructive-restrictive lung function loss.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis reports Xpert Ultra for pulmonary TB with summary sensitivity 84.8% and specificity 86.2% in one analysis of 9 studies, and a second review shows it effectively diagnoses TB and rifampicin resistance. (PMID 40728034, PMID 39754613)
- For Truenat MTB Plus, summary sensitivity was 90.6% with high-certainty evidence for pulmonary TB and rifampicin resistance in adults and adolescents. (PMID 40122135)
- Post-pulmonary TB patients have significantly decreased lung function compared with controls, with FEV1 more affected than FVC, a mixed obstructive and restrictive picture with predominant airflow obstruction. (PMID 40412395)
- A review synthesises advances in TB treatment from 2020 to 2026, including novel regimens, new drugs and host-directed therapy. (PMID 42526491)
- A clinical practice guideline for tuberculous meningitis gives evidence-based recommendations for treatment and diagnosis, exposing substantial knowledge gaps. (PMID 40840485)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal short regimens.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary Tuberculosis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Xpert Ultra detects rifampicin resistance.
- Post-TB lung disease.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pulmonary Tuberculosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with cough, weight loss and a positive Xpert Ultra trace.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary Tuberculosis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Standard four-drug regimen, duration, drug-induced liver injury management and contact tracing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1477] Pulmonary Vasculitis

**Cluster**: pulm_vasculitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2024 KDIGO guideline and 2025 BSR recommendations strengthen the case for reduced-dose or no glucocorticoid induction in ANCA vasculitis. EGPA has its own evidence-based guideline.

### Core Teaching Points
- The KDIGO 2024 executive summary describes a stronger case for reduced-dose glucocorticoid induction or even complete replacement of glucocorticoids in ANCA-associated vasculitis. (PMID 38388147)
- The 2025 British Society for Rheumatology recommendations on ANCA-associated vasculitis include research and audit recommendations to support equitable access. (PMID 40499922)
- The Pan American League guideline gives guidance on glucocorticoids, non-glucocorticoid immunosuppressants and plasma exchange, weighted for regional socioeconomic characteristics. (PMID 38251580)
- An evidence-based guideline addresses diagnosis and management of eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis. (PMID 37161084)
- The German S3 guideline on ANCA-associated vasculitis is the first at S3 level in Germany. (PMID 40172651)
- The Indian Rheumatology Association guideline notes some recommendations rest on low-quality evidence with high expert agreement. (PMID 39349267)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal glucocorticoid taper.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary Vasculitis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Reduced-dose steroid regimens.
- Plasma exchange role.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Pulmonary Vasculitis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with haemoptysis, haematuria and positive PR3-ANCA.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary Vasculitis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Rituximab versus cyclophosphamide, avacopan and plasma exchange indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1478] Pulmonary Veno-Occlusive Disease

**Cluster**: pvod | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
PVOD is a rare PAH variant. Vasodilators may help some patients but trial evidence is lacking, and referral for lung transplant assessment matters. Right ventricular dilatation and elevated pressures portend poorer prognosis.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review of vasodilators for PVOD and pulmonary capillary haemangiomatosis found drawing firm conclusions difficult because of the lack of randomised controlled trials. (PMID 30473253)
- A systematic review of targeted therapy in PVOD suggests cautious use could improve or stabilise haemodynamics and exercise capacity in some patients, and patients may live longer than expected. (PMID 31856792)
- A 2025 systematic review of PVOD risk factors, presentation, diagnosis and treatment stresses early diagnosis and referral for transplant consideration, with RV dilatation and elevated pulmonary pressures indicating poorer prognosis. (PMID 39661909)
- The updated clinical classification of pulmonary hypertension places PVOD/PCH features within PAH because evidence suggests a continuum between arterial, capillary and venous involvement. (PMID 30545968)
- A systematic review catalogues drugs associated with pulmonary arterial hypertension. (PMID 29508628)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Safety of PAH drugs in PVOD.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pulmonary Veno-Occlusive Disease.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PVOD risk of pulmonary oedema with vasodilators (not in abstracts).
- Transplant referral.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pulmonary Veno-Occlusive Disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young patient with PAH, low DLCO and septal lines on CT.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pulmonary Veno-Occlusive Disease presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: CT findings, genetic causes (EIF2AK4) and pulmonary oedema risk with PAH therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1479] Pure Red Cell Aplasia

**Cluster**: prca | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Cyclosporine A, with or without corticosteroids, appears the single most effective immunosuppressive agent for acquired PRCA. Evidence is low quality, and thymoma-associated disease has distinct recurrence patterns.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review of pure red cell aplasia states cyclosporine A, with or without corticosteroids, appears the single most effective immunosuppressive agent, with specific pathogenic subtypes linked to specific therapies. (PMID 27881371)
- A systematic review and meta-analysis found substantial uncertainty remains about the best treatment of acquired PRCA absent high-quality evidence. (PMID 37624775)
- A meta-analysis of immunosuppressive therapy for PRCA found efficacy influenced by aetiology, drug combinations and mutations such as STAT3, supporting personalised strategies. (PMID 40105948)
- In thymoma-associated autoimmune PRCA, immunosuppressants, particularly cyclosporine, are effective, but infectious complications must be considered. (PMID 34049719)
- A review of thymoma-related paraneoplastic syndromes found new syndromes after surgery associate with recurrence of the first, and resolution associates with improved overall survival. (PMID 31982129)
- In thymoma-related aplastic anaemia the one-year mortality rate is 29.8%, and allo-HSCT remains preferred for young patients with severe disease and suitable donors. (PMID 41622196)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best treatment sequence.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pure Red Cell Aplasia.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Thymoma and PRCA.
- Cyclosporine as first-line immunosuppression.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pure Red Cell Aplasia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with severe anaemia, reticulocytopenia and an anterior mediastinal mass.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pure Red Cell Aplasia presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Parvovirus B19, drug causes, bone marrow criteria and thymectomy response are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 31982129**: *A systematic review of paraneoplastic syndromes associated with thymoma: Treatment modalities, recurrence, and outcomes in resected cases* (2020) [systematic review] - None

## [1480] pyelonephritis

**Cluster**: pyelonephritis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Short-course antibiotic therapy is at least as effective as longer courses for uncomplicated acute pyelonephritis. Emphysematous pyelonephritis needs risk-stratified, often minimally invasive treatment, and male UTI guidance lacks international consensus.

### Core Teaching Points
- A comparison of systematic reviews and guidelines found short-course antibiotic treatment at least as effective as longer courses for microbiological and clinical success in acute uncomplicated pyelonephritis. (PMID 32566969)
- A systematic review of male UTI guidelines found a lack of international consensus, with fluoroquinolone-sparing and short treatment for afebrile male UTI debated. (PMID 35833228)
- In emphysematous pyelonephritis, risk factors predict patients needing aggressive treatment, while minimally invasive modalities carry lower mortality risk. (PMID 35103928)
- In uncomplicated UTI in adult women, non-antibiotic strategies reduce overall antibiotic use but give poorer clinical outcomes, and tests for erythrocytes and bacteria could target prescribing. (PMID 35788049)
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria for acute pyelonephritis (2022) guide imaging in specific clinical scenarios. (PMID 36436954)
- The ACP best practice advice supports short-course antibiotics in common infections. (PMID 33819054)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Oral versus intravenous step-down choices are not covered.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of pyelonephritis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Short courses suffice for uncomplicated pyelonephritis.
- Emphysematous pyelonephritis in diabetics.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in pyelonephritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with fever, flank pain and positive urine culture.
- A complex multimorbid patient with pyelonephritis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Antibiotic choice by local resistance, admission criteria and imaging triggers are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32566969**: *Short vs long-course antibiotic therapy in pyelonephritis: a comparison of systematic reviews and guidelines for the SIMI choosing wisely campaign* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 33819054**: *Appropriate Use of Short-Course Antibiotics in Common Infections: Best Practice Advice From the American College of Physicians* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 35103928**: *A systematic review and meta-analysis of risk factors and treatment choices in emphysematous pyelonephritis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35788049**: *Reducing antibiotic use in uncomplicated urinary tract infections in adult women: a systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 35833228**: *Guidelines for the management of male urinary tract infections in primary care: a lack of international consensus-a systematic review of the literature* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 36436954**: *ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Acute Pyelonephritis: 2022 Update* (2022) [guideline] - None

## [1482] Pyoderma Gangrenosum

**Cluster**: pyoderma | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Pyoderma gangrenosum has limited trial evidence: systemic therapy meta-analyses are constrained by heterogeneity, outcome measures beyond complete healing are needed, and registries and trial eligibility criteria are being standardised. Tyrosine kinase inhibitors can cause it.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review and single-arm meta-analysis of systemic therapies for pyoderma gangrenosum found heterogeneous methods and low numbers hamper research, with unmet need for better outcome measures than complete healing. (PMID 40740034)
- Japanese clinical practice guidelines for pyoderma gangrenosum (2022) are available in English. (PMID 37311717)
- A Delphi consensus (UPGRADE) standardised eligibility criteria for active ulcerative pyoderma gangrenosum in clinical trials. (PMID 38110114)
- A multidisciplinary consensus defined a minimum dataset for treatment effectiveness in pyoderma gangrenosum for an international registry. (PMID 41784109)
- A systematic review of case reports describes tyrosine kinase inhibitor-associated pyoderma gangrenosum, urging clinicians to consider drug-induced disease in oncology patients. (PMID 34282745)
- Meta-analyses of inflammatory bowel disease quantify extraintestinal manifestations and vedolizumab effects on them, with some skin-related manifestations exacerbated during vedolizumab. (PMID 37042969, PMID 38740543)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best first-line systemic therapy.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Pyoderma Gangrenosum.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Association with IBD.
- Drug-induced pyoderma gangrenosum.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Pyoderma Gangrenosum.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with ulcerative colitis and a rapidly enlarging painful ulcer with violaceous border.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Pyoderma Gangrenosum presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, pathergy and specific steroid or biologic regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1483] Quality improvement and patient safety in the ICU: checklists, daily goals, handover

**Cluster**: icu_quality | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
ABCDE and ABCDEF care bundles may reduce ICU stay, ventilation time, delirium and mortality, though more rigorous studies are needed. Handover tools lack mortality evidence, and nurses report weakness in error reporting and staffing.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review and meta-analysis found implementation of ABCDE and ABCDEF care bundles may reduce ICU stay, mechanical ventilation time, delirium and ICU and hospital mortality and promote early mobilisation, though more rigorous studies are needed. (PMID 35758388)
- CAM-ICU is recommended as the first-choice tool for delirium assessment in critically ill patients, followed by ICDSC. (PMID 38538305)
- A review of ICU-to-ward handover found there is no evidence that handover tools improve mortality or readmission, though supplementary written or verbal status reports and a liaison nurse may improve safety. (PMID 28452913)
- A meta-analysis of ICU nurses' perceptions found teamwork positive but weaknesses in response to error, event reporting frequency and staffing adequacy. (PMID 40785646)
- An antibiotic care bundle (ABC-Bundle) for the ICU was developed, with low daily adherence of 2% to 37%, showing its potential role in stewardship. (PMID 28705675)
- In situ simulation is proposed as an effective strategy to promote safety culture and teamwork in ICU after COVID-19. (PMID 36673631)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which bundle elements drive benefit.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Quality improvement and patient safety in the ICU: checklists, daily goals, handover.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- ABCDEF bundle components.
- CAM-ICU for delirium.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Quality improvement and patient safety in the ICU: checklists, daily goals, handover.

### Case Generation Hooks
- ICU team implementing a daily checklist and delirium screening.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Quality improvement and patient safety in the ICU: checklists, daily goals, handover presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Central line and ventilator-associated pneumonia checklists, and rounding tools, are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1484] Quality measures in acute MI: PPCI door-to-balloon, secondary prevention audits

**Cluster**: mi_quality | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Secondary prevention after MI: colchicine lowers cardiovascular events by about 12% in prior coronary disease or stroke, and anti-inflammatory therapy reduces MACE with more gastrointestinal effects. Beta-blockers beyond one event-free year may not add benefit when ejection fraction is preserved.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found colchicine reduced the composite of cardiovascular death, MI or stroke by 12% in patients with prior coronary disease or stroke. (PMID 40314334)
- Long-term trials meta-analysis found colchicine reduced MACE, MI, ischaemic stroke and recurrent revascularisation compared with placebo. (PMID 40314333)
- A network meta-analysis of anti-inflammatory strategies after MI found significant MACE reduction without increased serious adverse events, but more gastrointestinal adverse events. (PMID 39756386)
- A meta-analysis found beta-blockers may not add mortality benefit beyond a 1-year event-free period post-MI without reduced EF, with possible detriment in preserved EF. (PMID 39298680)
- An individual-patient-data meta-analysis supports preferential use of clopidogrel over aspirin for secondary prevention in established coronary artery disease, based on lower risk of events and bleeding. (PMID 40902613)
- The Australian ACS guideline supports tailored LDL-C targets and PCSK9 inhibitor use in secondary prevention. (PMID 41693087)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Colchicine sub-groups and tolerability.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Quality measures in acute MI: PPCI door-to-balloon, secondary prevention audits.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Colchicine for secondary prevention.
- Cardiac rehabilitation as a pathway element.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Quality measures in acute MI: PPCI door-to-balloon, secondary prevention audits.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient 1 month after STEMI planning long-term secondary prevention.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Quality measures in acute MI: PPCI door-to-balloon, secondary prevention audits presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Door-to-balloon targets, performance measures and dual antiplatelet duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 39756386**: *A pairwise and network meta-analysis of anti-inflammatory strategies after myocardial infarction: the TITIAN study* (2025) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1485] RA and cardiovascular risk: accelerated atherosclerosis, NSAIDs, coxib caution

**Cluster**: ra_cvd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Cardiovascular risk is increased across inflammatory joint disease: RA carries a relative risk of about 1.55 for cardiovascular events. EULAR recommends CV risk management, and drug choice influences risk, with JAK inhibitors needing post-marketing data.

### Core Teaching Points
- The EULAR 2017 update on cardiovascular risk management in RA and other inflammatory joint disorders defines ten recommendations, underscoring that CVD risk is increased across the whole spectrum of inflammatory joint disease. (PMID 27697765)
- A meta-analysis found the relative risk of cardiovascular events in RA was 1.55 (95% CI 1.18-2.02). (PMID 34454117)
- A comparative meta-analysis suggested tocilizumab may be associated with reduced MACE risk versus TNF inhibitors, while csDMARDs may be associated with increased MACE and stroke risk. (PMID 30875456)
- Randomised trials showed no significant short-term change in cardiovascular risk with JAK inhibitors in RA, but postmarketing data are needed, especially for higher doses with thromboembolism risk for tofacitinib and baricitinib. (PMID 31088790)
- Hydroxychloroquine may benefit the metabolic profile in RA, with lower diabetes incidence among ever-users (HR 0.59) and possibly fewer cardiovascular events. (PMID 28970215)
- Italian recommendations advise more stringent CV risk assessment in RA patients needing NSAIDs or with hypertension, dyslipidaemia or diabetes, intima-media thickness measurement for moderate risk and cardiological evaluation for high risk. (PMID 36912349)
- A collaboration between rheumatology and general practice raised CVD risk screening in RA patients to 88% after reminder letters. (PMID 29167154)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best risk calculator in RA.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of RA and cardiovascular risk: accelerated atherosclerosis, NSAIDs, coxib caution.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Increased CV risk in RA.
- JAK inhibitor cardiovascular safety.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in RA and cardiovascular risk: accelerated atherosclerosis, NSAIDs, coxib caution.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with long-standing RA and hypertension asking about cardiovascular risk.
- A complex multimorbid patient with RA and cardiovascular risk: accelerated atherosclerosis, NSAIDs, coxib caution presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: EULAR risk multiplier (1.5) and statin thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1486] Rabies pre- and post-exposure prophylaxis

**Cluster**: rabies | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Timely post-exposure prophylaxis is crucial: rabies despite prophylaxis is rare. Monoclonal antibodies can replace immunoglobulin in some settings, and older individuals mount lower antibody responses.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review of fatal breakthrough infections concluded prompt PEP administration is crucial, and that rabies despite adherence to core practices, even in high-risk or immunosuppressed people, remains exceedingly rare. (PMID 36535276)
- Current US guidelines address rabies pre-exposure prophylaxis and post-exposure prophylaxis regimens. (PMID 32752569)
- The Indian Academy of Pediatrics endorses WHO 2018 post-exposure prophylaxis, and monoclonal rabies antibody can be given as an alternative to rabies immunoglobulin. (PMID 30745480)
- An expert consensus standardises clinical use of ormutivimab for rabies post-exposure prophylaxis in China. (PMID 37427985)
- A dose-response meta-analysis of rabies vaccination suggests schedule choice should consider patient needs, cost and ability to return, and that older individuals develop lower antibody responses. (PMID 33478786)
- A Southeast Asia review identifies rural unavailability of vaccine and misinformation about seeking treatment after dog bites as drivers of rabies mortality. (PMID 37164462)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal abbreviated schedules.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Rabies pre- and post-exposure prophylaxis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PEP after animal exposure includes wound washing, vaccine and immunoglobulin.
- Immunosuppression reduces response.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Rabies pre- and post-exposure prophylaxis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Traveller bitten by a stray dog abroad.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Rabies pre- and post-exposure prophylaxis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Wound care steps, exact vaccine schedules and category of exposure are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1487] Radial versus femoral access for PCI

**Cluster**: radial_access | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Radial access reduces bleeding, vascular complications and mortality compared with femoral access in coronary interventions, with comparable stroke and MI. Distal radial and ulnar sites are viable secondary options.

### Core Teaching Points
- An updated meta-analysis of randomised trials found radial access associated with significant reductions in bleeding, vascular complications and mortality versus femoral access, with comparable stroke and MI. (PMID 33507598)
- A network meta-analysis of access sites for coronary angiography supports distal radial and ulnar access as a default secondary access site before femoral access. (PMID 39027936)
- A SCAI consensus update covers best practices for transradial intervention, including ulnar access, collateral flow testing, strategies to prevent radial artery occlusion and radial access for primary PCI. (PMID 31880380)
- A review of distal radial access gives practical recommendations on technique, pitfalls and complications. (PMID 40656779)
- An AHA scientific statement sees promise in radial access for peripheral vascular interventions as technology advances. (PMID 39629587)
- In NSTEMI PCI, bivalirudin reduced serious bleeding versus heparin from access and non-access sites without different rates of mortality or ischaemic events. (PMID 37746717)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role in unstable shock patients.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Radial versus femoral access for PCI.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Radial access lowers bleeding and mortality.
- Radial artery occlusion prevention.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Radial versus femoral access for PCI.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with STEMI on anticoagulation: choice of access site.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Radial versus femoral access for PCI presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Allen's test relevance, anticoagulant dosing and trial names (MATRIX, RIVAL) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

- **PMID 33507598**: *Radial versus femoral access for coronary interventions: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized trials* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37746717**: *Bivalirudin Versus Heparin During PCI in NSTEMI: Individual Patient Data Meta-Analysis of Large Randomized Trials* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1488] Radiation-induced heart disease: coronary artery disease, valvular, pericardial

**Cluster**: rihd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Radiation-induced heart disease arises from endothelial injury, inflammation and oxidative stress, producing vascular and cardiac disease. Evidence for imaging, screening and prevention is mostly review-level.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review classifies radiation-induced heart disease and links it to endothelial injury, inflammatory response and oxidative stress, urging prevention. (PMID 31592122)
- A practical review provides clinical and imaging manifestations, multimodality screening recommendations and management options for radiation-associated cardiac disease. (PMID 30092970)
- A state-of-the-art review focuses on radiation-induced vascular disease, covering mechanisms, manifestations, prevention, screening, diagnosis and management. (PMID 33860001)
- A systematic review of nuclear imaging for early radiation-induced cardiotoxicity calls for larger populations, longer follow-up and heart dosimetric data. (PMID 37876964)
- A review of oxidative stress in radiotherapy cardiotoxicity summarises prevention and treatment methods. (PMID 32190171)
- A bibliometric analysis stresses prevention, consistent monitoring, prompt diagnosis and timely treatment to decrease RIHD in cancer survivors. (PMID 38655134)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal screening interval.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Radiation-induced heart disease: coronary artery disease, valvular, pericardial.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Radiation causes coronary, valvular, pericardial and myocardial disease.
- Latency of RIHD.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Radiation-induced heart disease: coronary artery disease, valvular, pericardial.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Survivor of mediastinal radiotherapy with new exertional angina.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Radiation-induced heart disease: coronary artery disease, valvular, pericardial presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Dose thresholds, latency figures and surveillance intervals are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1489] Radiation necrosis vs tumour progression: perfusion MRI and PET scan utility

**Cluster**: radionecrosis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Amino acid PET, FDG-PET, SPECT and MRS help distinguish radiation necrosis from recurrence and are considered more useful than standard MRI. Treatment includes observation, steroids, bevacizumab, LITT and surgery, with low-quality evidence.

### Core Teaching Points
- A diagnostic meta-analysis ranked SPECT and MRS as more useful than standard MRI for distinguishing radiation necrosis from tumour recurrence. (PMID 36853489)
- A meta-analysis found amino acid PET and FDG-PET had good diagnostic accuracy in differentiating brain metastasis recurrence from radionecrosis, though 60% of studies were retrospective. (PMID 29242363)
- A systematic review of radiological diagnosis of brain radiation necrosis found combining imaging studies improves accuracy, but risk of bias prevents firm conclusions on the best technique. (PMID 30728041)
- A systematic review informing management of symptomatic radionecrosis after stereotactic radiosurgery found low-quality evidence across treatment modalities and needs head-to-head comparisons. (PMID 37482137)
- The DEGRO guidelines propose a multistep diagnostic approach and detailed treatment strategies including observation, surgery, steroids and bevacizumab, discussed in an interdisciplinary setting. (PMID 36038669, PMID 36038670)
- Meta-analyses of bevacizumab found radiographic response and clinical improvement without serious adverse events, and outcomes mostly comparable to laser interstitial thermal therapy, with LITT possibly better for survival in selected patients. (PMID 33593308, PMID 34218396)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which patients benefit most from bevacizumab versus LITT.
- Comparative efficacy and cognitive safety of laser interstitial thermal therapy (LITT) versus medical therapy with bevacizumab and high-dose dexamethasone.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Perfusion MRI and PET to differentiate necrosis from progression.
- Bevacizumab for symptomatic radionecrosis.
- Distinguishing cerebral radiation necrosis from recurrent glioblastoma/metastasis: relative cerebral blood volume (rCBV) hypoperfusion on DSC-MRI vs amino acid PET (18F-FET/11C-MET) uptake.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient 8 months after radiosurgery with an enlarging enhancing lesion and oedema.
- 58-year-old female with resected lung adenocarcinoma brain metastasis treated with SRS 9 months ago presents with focal seizures and a ring-enhancing lesion with extensive vasogenic oedema.

**Reviewer Gap**: Perfusion MRI metrics and steroid dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29242363**: *Diagnostic Accuracy of Amino Acid and FDG-PET in Differentiating Brain Metastasis Recurrence from Radionecrosis after Radiotherapy: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2018) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 30728041**: *Radiological diagnosis of brain radiation necrosis after cranial irradiation for brain tumor: a systematic review* (2019) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 33593308**: *Bevacizumab for radiation necrosis following radiotherapy of brain metastatic disease: a systematic review & meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 34218396**: *Bevacizumab vs laser interstitial thermal therapy in cerebral radiation necrosis from brain metastases: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 36038669**: *DEGRO practical guideline for central nervous system radiation necrosis part 1: classification and a multistep approach for diagnosis* (2022) [guideline] - None

## [1490] Radiation Pneumonitis

**Cluster**: rad_pneumonitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Radiation pneumonitis is a diagnosis of exclusion, with ESTRO and Delphi consensus guidance on diagnosis and management. Combining radiotherapy with immune checkpoint inhibitors, and pre-existing interstitial lung abnormalities, raise risk.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ESTRO guideline states radiation pneumonitis remains a diagnosis of exclusion, proposes a diagnostic algorithm and outlines practical treatment approaches. (PMID 40185160)
- An international Delphi consensus gives practical guidance on diagnosis and management of radiation pneumonitis, emphasising risk stratification, especially in interstitial lung disease. (PMID 38788551)
- A meta-analysis examined pneumonitis incidence with radiotherapy combined with immune checkpoint inhibitors in lung cancer, with significant heterogeneity requiring prospective validation. (PMID 40313247)
- Interstitial lung abnormalities are associated with increased mortality, lung cancer risk and cancer treatment-related complications. (PMID 38538680)
- A radiomics-based hybrid model for predicting radiation pneumonitis may identify higher-risk patients. (PMID 38906047)
- A meta-analysis of functional lung imaging in radiotherapy for lung cancer supports its use in planning and delivery, with heterogeneity in approaches. (PMID 30082143)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal steroid regimens.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of Radiation Pneumonitis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Radiation pneumonitis as exclusion diagnosis.
- Immunotherapy increases risk.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in Radiation Pneumonitis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient 8 weeks after chest radiotherapy with dry cough and dyspnoea.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Radiation Pneumonitis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Time course, grading and steroid dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1491] radiculopathy

**Cluster**: radiculopathy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Epidural steroid injections give short- to medium-term relief of lumbar radiculopathy and sciatica; platelet-rich plasma showed comparable benefit in one meta-analysis. Guidelines advise clinical assessment with restricted imaging.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found epidural steroid injection relieves sciatica from lumbar disc herniation in the short to medium term and is a viable option. (PMID 38841695)
- A comparative systematic review of placebo-controlled and active-controlled trials rated epidural injections for lumbar radiculopathy as Level I evidence at 1 and 3 months and Level II at 6 and 12 months. (PMID 36288577)
- A meta-analysis found epidural platelet-rich plasma offers benefit comparable to epidural steroid with a similar safety profile for radiculopathy due to lumbar disc disease. (PMID 39968415)
- A Saudi guideline advises that assessment of low back pain and sciatica be guided by clinical assessment, with restricted imaging and careful selection of pharmacological, psychological, multidisciplinary and procedural interventions. (PMID 41598467)
- A Chinese medicine guideline for cervical radiculopathy gave strong recommendations for acupuncture and manual therapy. (PMID 41376234)
- An image-guided epidural steroid injection practice parameter addresses indications, contraindications and technique to reduce serious neurologic complications. (PMID 42235968)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term benefit beyond 12 months.
- Optimal management duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic de-escalation in high-risk subgroups of radiculopathy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Epidural steroid for short-term sciatica relief.
- Imaging only when red flags exist.
- Recognizing treatment refractoriness, adverse drug reaction monitoring, and urgent escalation pathways in radiculopathy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with L5 radiculopathy and disc herniation after 6 weeks of conservative care.
- A complex multimorbid patient with radiculopathy presenting with therapeutic intolerance, contraindications, or disease escalation requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Red-flag criteria, indications for surgery and cervical radiculopathy specifics are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources

## [1493] Rapidly Progressive Glomerulonephritis

**Cluster**: rpgn | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
RPGN is mainly ANCA vasculitis, anti-GBM disease or immune-complex disease. Plasma exchange lowers 12-month ESKD risk in ANCA vasculitis but raises serious infection risk, and cyclophosphamide or rituximab remain induction options.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of plasma exchange in ANCA-associated vasculitis found it reduces the 12-month risk of ESKD but increases the risk of serious infections. (PMID 35217545)
- The KDIGO update advises cyclophosphamide and rituximab or combinations to induce remission in patients with severe kidney involvement in ANCA vasculitis, with considerations for plasma exchange. (PMID 41151615)
- The EULAR 2022 update on ANCA-associated vasculitis management notes substantial data gaps remain and informed physician-patient decision-making matters. (PMID 36927642)
- A review of treatment advances in ANCA vasculitis notes the FDA proposed in April 2026 to withdraw approval for avacopan for lack of substantial evidence of effectiveness. (PMID 41817218)
- A systematic review of anti-GBM disease covers epidemiology, clinical features, risk factors and outcomes, with caution over observational heterogeneity. (PMID 38493958)
- A systematic review suggests plasma exchange may benefit some patients with Henoch-Schönlein purpura nephritis and IgA nephropathy presenting with RPGN. (PMID 36835388)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Place of avacopan.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Rapidly Progressive Glomerulonephritis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Plasma exchange: ESKD reduction versus infection risk.
- Crescentic glomerulonephritis causes.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Rapidly Progressive Glomerulonephritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with haematuria, rapidly rising creatinine and positive ANCA.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Rapidly Progressive Glomerulonephritis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Crescent biopsy findings, anti-GBM treatment and steroid regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1494] Rate versus rhythm control in atrial fibrillation

**Cluster**: af_rate_rhythm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Recent guidelines favour early rhythm control and catheter ablation, especially for paroxysmal AF or heart failure, alongside anticoagulation and risk-factor modification. Guidance for older patients on rhythm control and ablation is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- A clinical practice guideline states available evidence supports evaluating indications for oral anticoagulation, early rhythm control and catheter ablation, especially in paroxysmal AF or heart failure. (PMID 40526576)
- A new AF guideline commentary clarifies which patients should be considered for catheter ablation and delves into thrombosis risk calculations when deciding on anticoagulation. (PMID 40312119)
- An analysis of the 2024 ESC guideline in older patients found recommendations on rhythm control and catheter ablation lack specific advice for older people. (PMID 41212796)
- A meta-analysis found GLP-1 receptor agonist therapy was associated with reduced AF recurrence after ablation, with cautious interpretation needed. (PMID 42033283)
- A guideline on heart failure with AF discusses rate versus rhythm control separately for HFrEF and HFpEF and gives subtype-specific recommendations. (PMID 42170354)
- The 2026 ACC scientific statement covers direct oral anticoagulants in primary and secondary prevention of thrombotic events. (PMID 42377292)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Rhythm control in older and frail patients.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Rate versus rhythm control in atrial fibrillation.

- Early rhythm control and ablation.

## [1495] RA treat-to-target strategy: DAS28, SDAI, CDAI remission thresholds

**Cluster**: ra_t2t | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Treat-to-target in RA pursues low disease activity with stepwise DMARD escalation. The best target remains uncertain, and JAK inhibitor safety (herpes zoster) needs monitoring.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review and meta-regression asking which target is best in RA treat-to-target found direct comprehensive comparisons of targets are still needed because of residual confounding and lack of safety and overtreatment data. (PMID 37116986)
- South African guidelines advise continuing DMARD therapy in a stepwise algorithm until low disease activity is achieved, with screening for comorbidities and vaccination. (PMID 39513243)
- Pan American League recommendations provide a practical, evidence-based framework for managing RA in Latin America. (PMID 41240967)
- A meta-analysis of upadacitinib in immune-mediated inflammatory diseases found herpes zoster needs caution, with thorough assessment, monitoring and individualised dosing vital. (PMID 40666511)
- Glucocorticoid therapy is a major risk factor for osteoporosis in RA, and higher BMI is protective. (PMID 41188646)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which composite index to target.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of RA treat-to-target strategy: DAS28, SDAI, CDAI remission thresholds.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treat-to-target with escalation until low disease activity.
- JAK inhibitor and zoster risk.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in RA treat-to-target strategy: DAS28, SDAI, CDAI remission thresholds.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with RA and moderate disease activity on methotrexate.
- A complex multimorbid patient with RA treat-to-target strategy: DAS28, SDAI, CDAI remission thresholds presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: DAS28, SDAI and CDAI remission cut-offs are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## [1496] Raynaud phenomenon: primary vs secondary, digital ulcers, calcium channel blockers, PDE5 inhibitors

**Cluster**: raynaud | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Evidence for Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis is limited: non-pharmacological measures have modest effects, EULAR 2023 and Portuguese recommendations guide drug choice, and botulinum toxin appears a safe adjunct for refractory digital ischaemia.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review supporting Portuguese recommendations found non-pharmacological interventions have only modest effects and that high-quality evidence for the optimal treatment is limited. (PMID 38306796)
- Portuguese recommendations give comprehensive, personalised management of Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis and other connective tissue diseases, with a research agenda on non-pharmacologic interventions. (PMID 38956991)
- A meta-analysis found botulinum toxin injections appear a safe and effective adjunct for refractory digital ischaemia and ulcers in systemic sclerosis, needing prospective trials. (PMID 41369954)
- EULAR 2023 recommendations for systemic sclerosis include first-line combination therapy for newly diagnosed pulmonary arterial hypertension and therapies for fibrotic manifestations. (PMID 39874231)
- A meta-analysis suggests vascular endothelial growth factor as a potential biomarker in systemic sclerosis. (PMID 39669565)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal drug for digital ulcer prevention.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Raynaud phenomenon: primary vs secondary, digital ulcers, calcium channel blockers, PDE5 inhibitors.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Primary versus secondary Raynaud.
- Botulinum toxin for refractory digital ischaemia.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Raynaud phenomenon: primary vs secondary, digital ulcers, calcium channel blockers, PDE5 inhibitors.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with cold-induced colour changes and a new fingertip ulcer.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Raynaud phenomenon: primary vs secondary, digital ulcers, calcium channel blockers, PDE5 inhibitors presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: Calcium channel blockers, PDE5 inhibitors, bosentan and capillaroscopy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38306796**: *Systematic literature review to inform the Portuguese recommendations for the management of Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis and other connective tissue diseases* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 38956991**: *Portuguese Recommendations for the management of Raynaud's phenomenon and digital ulcers in systemic sclerosis and other connective tissue diseases* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39669565**: *Vascular endothelial growth factor as a potential biomarker in systemic sclerosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39874231**: *EULAR recommendations for the treatment of systemic sclerosis: 2023 update* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 41369954**: *Botulinum Toxin for Refractory Digital Ischemia and Ulcers in Systemic Sclerosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis] - None

## [1498] RCRI score

**Cluster**: periop_risk | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Perioperative risk assessment combines clinical risk indices such as the Revised Cardiac Risk Index, functional capacity, biomarkers and procedure-specific risk, as set out in the 2024 AHA/ACC guideline, to decide who needs further testing or optimisation.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Revised Cardiac Risk Index was derived and prospectively validated as a simple index that identifies higher-risk patients undergoing nonurgent major noncardiac surgery and outperformed other published indexes. (PMID 10477528)
- The 2024 AHA/ACC multisociety guideline provides evidence-based strategies for perioperative cardiovascular management in noncardiac surgery, including pharmacological therapy, monitoring and devices. (PMID 39316661, PMID 39320289)
- ESAIC focused guidelines advise on perioperative use of cardiac troponin and natriuretic peptides in noncardiac surgery patients for three scopes of application. (PMID 37265332)
- The 2025 ESPEN surgery guideline adds recommendations for frailty assessment, sarcopenia diagnosis and prehabilitation. (PMID 40957230)
- EASL guidance advises individualised multidisciplinary risk assessment for extrahepatic abdominal surgery in cirrhosis. (PMID 40348682)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of routine biomarkers and stress testing.
- RCRI vs NSQIP surgical risk calculator: performance variability across vascular, orthopaedic, and intra-abdominal procedures.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- RCRI items: ischaemic heart disease, heart failure, cerebrovascular disease, insulin treatment, creatinine above 2 mg/dL, high-risk surgery (not in abstracts).
- Functional capacity of 4 METs.
- Postoperative troponin surveillance indications and management of perioperative myocardial injury (MINS) in patients with RCRI >= 2.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 68-year-old with diabetes and prior MI before an elective hernia repair.
- 71-year-old woman with chronic kidney disease (eGFR 28 mL/min), insulin-treated type 2 diabetes, and transient ischemic attack undergoing elective hip arthroplasty.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric RCRI risk by score, stress testing indications and specific optimisation steps are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 10477528**: *Derivation and prospective validation of a simple index for prediction of cardiac risk of major noncardiac surgery* (1999) [other] - None
- **PMID 37265332**: *ESAIC focused guideline for the use of cardiac biomarkers in perioperative risk evaluation* (2023) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39316661**: *2024 AHA/ACC/ACS/ASNC/HRS/SCA/SCCT/SCMR/SVM Guideline for Perioperative Cardiovascular Management for Noncardiac Surgery: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 39320289**: *2024 AHA/ACC/ACS/ASNC/HRS/SCA/SCCT/SCMR/SVM Guideline for Perioperative Cardiovascular Management for Noncardiac Surgery: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40348682**: *EASL Clinical Practice Guidelines on extrahepatic abdominal surgery in patients with cirrhosis and advanced chronic liver disease* (2025) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40957230**: *ESPEN guideline on clinical nutrition in surgery - Update 2025* (2025) [guideline] - None

## [1499] Reactive Arthritis

**Cluster**: reactive_arthritis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Reactive arthritis follows genitourinary or enteric infection; only a small percentage of Campylobacter, Salmonella, Shigella or Yersinia infections lead to it. BASHH guidance covers sexually acquired reactive arthritis.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found the percentage of people developing reactive arthritis after Campylobacter, Salmonella, Shigella or Yersinia infection is relatively low (2.6%). (PMID 34255548)
- A 2024 meta-analysis found a significant proportion of Salmonella, Shigella and Yersinia cases resulted in reactive arthritis, with substantial heterogeneity between studies. (PMID 38929962)
- The BASHH national guideline gives management of sexually acquired reactive arthritis within UK sexual health services. (PMID 34014782)
- Updated estimates of sequelae after Campylobacter infection were provided, including for Crohn's disease and ulcerative colitis for the first time, with broad uncertainty. (PMID 40496789)
- A South African peripheral spondyloarthritis guideline advises a multidisciplinary team, treat-to-target escalation and screening for comorbidities. (PMID 39508216)
- A systematic review of intravesical BCG found autoimmune disorders, including articular and ocular groups, as complications. (PMID 37061015)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Efficacy of prolonged antibiotics is not covered.
- Optimal treatment duration, biomarker monitoring thresholds, and personalized therapeutic sequencing in high-risk subgroups of Reactive Arthritis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Reactive arthritis after enteric or chlamydial infection.
- Management follows treat-to-target principles.
- Diagnostic criteria, biomarker thresholds, and risk stratification in Reactive Arthritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young man with urethritis, conjunctivitis and asymmetric oligoarthritis.
- A complex multimorbid patient with Reactive Arthritis presenting with therapeutic intolerance, refractory symptoms, or acute complication requiring multidisciplinary management.

**Reviewer Gap**: HLA-B27 role, triad definition, NSAID and DMARD treatment, and antibiotic evidence are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34014782**: *British Association of Sexual Health and HIV national guideline on the management of sexually acquired reactive arthritis 2021* (2021) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 34255548**: *Enteric Pathogens and Reactive Arthritis: Systematic Review and Meta-Analyses of Pathogen-Associated Reactive Arthritis* (2021) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 37061015**: *Autoimmune disorders caused by intravesical bacillus Calmette-Guerine treatment: A systematic review* (2023) [systematic review] - None
- **PMID 38929962**: *Enteric Infection-Associated Reactive Arthritis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis] - None
- **PMID 39508216**: *South African Rheumatism and Arthritis Association 2024 guidelines for the management of peripheral spondyloarthritis* (2024) [guideline] - None
- **PMID 40496789**: *Probability of sequelae following &lt;i&gt;Campylobacter&lt;/i&gt; spp. infections: Update of systematic reviews and meta-analyses* (2023) [meta-analysis] - None
